# Manifest — Group 1: Gauge Theory I–VI (filename manifest and concept registry)

Sources: A = Haydys (content-map-haydys.md), B = Bär (content-map-wernli.md). Item numbers `D/T/E/X/R/I` are A's unless prefixed `B-`. Anchor names from `anchors.md` are used byte-exactly and marked **[anchor]**; the seven backlink names are marked **[backlink]**. Every page name was checked against `vault-index-geometry.txt` and the full `vault-index.txt` for collisions (the only pre-existing names touched are the mandated backlink names, which are not currently in the vault).

Global design decisions binding on all six chapters:

1. **Conventions** are those of `conventions.md`: right principal actions, $\omega(\xi_P)=\xi$, $R_g^*\omega=\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\omega$, $F=dA+\tfrac12[A\wedge A]$ ($=dA+A\wedge A$ for matrix groups), $[\alpha\wedge\beta](X,Y)=[\alpha(X),\beta(Y)]-[\alpha(Y),\beta(X)]$, gauge groups act on the right, $c_1(L)=[\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F]$, $e(E)=[\operatorname{Pf}(F/2\pi)]$ with $e(TS^2)[S^2]=2$. Where A or B differ (Haydys writes $F=dA+A\wedge A$ without $\tfrac12$ for matrix groups; Bär's Euler-class normalisation $\operatorname{Pf}(\bar\Omega)/((2\pi)^m m!)$; Bär's left actions on manifolds vs. right actions on bundles; Haydys's Ricci sign; Bär's nonstandard labelling of $\sigma_1,\sigma_2$) the page states the series convention and records the source's in a `> [!warning] Convention:` callout with the one-line conversion.
2. **Haydys §2.4 (classification of $U(1)$- and $SU(2)$-bundles) lives in chapter III §3.6** (the anchors put both classification theorems in III), and chapter III therefore carries a self-contained "smooth-topological toolkit" section §3.5 (easy Sard, generic sections, homogeneity lemma, top de Rham cohomology, Brouwer degree, winding numbers, homotopy invariance of bundles, clutching). Nothing in III uses connections; the Chern–Weil identifications of the topological invariants are proved in chapter VI.
3. **Integral cohomology is not available before chapter XII** (AT I's de Rham theorem and Mayer–Vietoris pages are flagged unproved; AT II's Seifert–van Kampen is statement-only). Consequently: (a) the first Chern class is defined in de Rham cohomology throughout I–VI, with integrality proved concretely as integrality of periods over closed oriented surfaces and over closed oriented four-manifolds; (b) the classification theorems are stated in the forms that are provable with I–VI tools (homotopy classes of maps to $\mathbb{CP}^\infty$, $\mathbb{HP}^\infty$; complete classification over closed oriented surfaces; existence and invariance of the Chern number over four-manifolds; triviality in dimension $\le 3$); (c) every statement of the sources that needs $H^\bullet(-;\mathbb Z)$, cellular approximation, the Hopf degree theorem, $\pi_3(S^3)$, or "every closed oriented 3-manifold bounds" is recorded on the relevant page as a **scope remark** — bold plain text, no theorem blockquote, explicitly "not proved and not used in this series" — and listed in the coverage table with its replacement. **Downstream chapters (VIII, XI, XIII) must write "$\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ = isomorphism classes of Hermitian line bundles" wherever the sources write "$H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$ = set of $U(1)$-bundles"; the two are identified only in chapter XII if group 3 proves it.**
4. **Flagged vault pages are never linked.** The following results the sources lean on are re-proved in the series under new names because the existing vault page is in `unproved-pages.txt`: pull-back commutes with $d$ (DG VIII) → `Thm - Pull-Back Commutes with the Exterior Derivative` (II); Jacobi identity for vector fields (DG V "Lie Bracket Properties") → `Thm - The Space of Vector Fields is a Lie Algebra under the Lie Bracket` (I); Sard (DG IV) → the restricted `Thm - Sard's Theorem when the Source Dimension does not Exceed the Target Dimension` (III; the full theorem is chapter X's); Brouwer degree homotopy invariance (RG IV) → `Thm - The Brouwer Degree is an Integer and a Homotopy Invariant` (III); Hopf–Rinow (RG II, statement-only) is avoided: the compact-group surjectivity theorem proves the minimizing-geodesic lemma it needs on its own page; Chern–Weil (AT III, statement-only / flagged) → `Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem` (VI); Fundamental Theorem of Riemannian Geometry: link only RG I's "(Full Proof)" page, never DG XII's "(Statement)".
5. **Existing proof-complete vault pages are linked, not duplicated** (per chapter lists below). Where an anchor forces a page whose content overlaps an existing page (e.g. `Thm - Local Formula for the Curvature of a Connection` vs RG I's Cartan second structural equation; `Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions` vs DG XI's `Def - Smooth Action of a Lie Group`), the series page is written as specified, links the existing page in Bridges, and restates it at the point of use.
6. **No `Imported without proof` callouts occur in chapters I–VI.** (See the closing list.)
7. Every exercise page invokes only pages named in this manifest or proof-complete vault pages; the intended route is given so the writer never needs a flagged page.

---

### §1.1 Lie Groups and the Classical Matrix Groups
- `Def - Classical Matrix Groups` — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D1.1.2, B-D1.1.3, B-E1.1.1, B-E1.1.2, B-E1.1.3, B-E1.1.4, B-E1.1.5, B-E1.1.6, B-R1.1.1, B-I1.1.1, B-I1.1.2; prereqs: [Def - Lie Group, Thm - The Closed Subgroup Theorem, Def - Determinant]; spec: Defines $GL(n;\mathbb K)$, $O(n)$, $SO(n)$, $SL(n;\mathbb K)$, $U(n)$, $SU(n)$, $Sp(1)$ (unit quaternions, needed in III–VI) and the product Lie group $G\times G'$. Verifies on the page that $GL(n;\mathbb K)$ is a Lie group (open subset, polynomial multiplication, rational inversion — E1.1.2) and that each of the others is a closed subgroup (E1.1.4 written out, E1.1.5 written out for every group, including the source's misprint $SL(n;\mathbb R)$ for $SL(n;\mathbb C)$), hence a Lie group by the linked closed subgroup theorem; the product structure is verified clause by clause. Examples: $\mathbb R^n$ (E1.1.1), $T^n=U(1)^n$; non-example: $\{A\in GL(2;\mathbb R):\det A\in\mathbb Q\}$ is a subgroup but not closed. Relate to Other Fields carries R1.1.1 (Hilbert's fifth problem, Gleason–Montgomery–Zippin) as a historical note explicitly not leaned on (covers I1.1.2). Calibration check: show $SO(n)=O(n)\cap SL(n;\mathbb R)$ is closed; show $U(1)\subset GL(1;\mathbb C)$ is the unit circle; compute $\dim$ of nothing yet (dimensions are §1.2).
- `Thm - SO(2) is Isomorphic to U(1)` — type: theorem; source items: B-T1.1.2, B-E1.1.7; prereqs: [Def - Classical Matrix Groups, Def - Lie Group Homomorphism]; statement: $SO(2)=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}\cos\varphi&-\sin\varphi\\\sin\varphi&\cos\varphi\end{pmatrix}:\varphi\in\mathbb R\right\}$, $U(1)=\{e^{i\varphi}:\varphi\in\mathbb R\}$, and $\begin{pmatrix}\cos\varphi&-\sin\varphi\\\sin\varphi&\cos\varphi\end{pmatrix}\mapsto e^{i\varphi}$ is an isomorphism of Lie groups; both are diffeomorphic to $S^1$; proof: source full (solve (1.1)–(1.4), addition theorems, Euler's formula); gaps: smoothness of the map and its inverse as maps of submanifolds, and well-definedness (the angle $\varphi$ is only defined modulo $2\pi$) must be written; invokes `Def - Classical Matrix Groups`, DG XI `Def - Lie Group Homomorphism`, Topology IV `Ex - S1 and SO(2) are homeomorphic as topological groups` (bridge only); spec: Sources: any $2\times2$ real matrix with $A^tA=1$, $\det A=1$; Targets: abelian, so $\operatorname{Ad}$ is trivial (used in IV for $U(1)$-connections). Mechanism: two real constraints on four entries leave one angle.
- `Ex - The Classical Groups are Closed Subgroups of GL(n)` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E1.1.4, B-E1.1.5; prereqs: [Def - Classical Matrix Groups, Thm - The Closed Subgroup Theorem]; spec: For each of $O(n),SL(n;\mathbb R),SO(n),U(n),SL(n;\mathbb C),SU(n)$ prove the subgroup axioms and closedness as the preimage of a closed set under a continuous map; conclude Lie group via the closed subgroup theorem; route: continuity of $A\mapsto A^tA$, $A\mapsto\det A$, $A\mapsto A^*A$.
- `Ex - SU(2) is the Group of Unit Quaternions` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A §2.4 p. 25 ($Sp(1)\to SU(2)$, $q=z+wj\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}z&w\\-\bar w&\bar z\end{pmatrix}$), part of D2.4.6; prereqs: [Def - Classical Matrix Groups]; spec: Prove the displayed map is a Lie group isomorphism $Sp(1)\to SU(2)$ (multiplicativity from the quaternion relations, bijectivity by solving $A^*A=1,\det A=1$ for a $2\times2$ complex matrix), and identify both with $S^3$; link DG XI `Ex - SU(2) is Diffeomorphic to S^3`.
- `Ex - Products of Lie Groups and the Torus` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-D1.1.3, B-E1.1.6; prereqs: [Def - Classical Matrix Groups]; spec: Verify $G\times G'$ is a Lie group (smoothness of componentwise operations via DG I `Thm - Product of Smooth Manifolds is a Smooth Manifold`); show $T^n=U(1)^n$ is a compact abelian Lie group of dimension $n$ and that $\mathbb R^n\to T^n$, $x\mapsto(e^{2\pi ix_j})$, is a surjective Lie group homomorphism with kernel $\mathbb Z^n$.
- `Exercise Index - §1.1 Lie Groups and the Classical Matrix Groups` — lists: [Ex - The Classical Groups are Closed Subgroups of GL(n), Ex - SU(2) is the Group of Unit Quaternions, Ex - Products of Lie Groups and the Torus]

### §1.2 Lie Algebras of the Classical Groups
- `Def - Lie Subalgebra and Abelian Lie Algebra` — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D1.2.1 (→ link `Def - Lie Algebra`), B-D1.2.2, B-D1.2.3, B-E1.2.1, B-E1.2.2, B-E1.2.3, B-E1.2.4, B-R1.2.1; prereqs: [Def - Lie Algebra, Def - The Lie Bracket of Vector Fields]; spec: Defines Lie subalgebra and abelian Lie algebra, restating the three Lie algebra axioms from DG XI (note B's misprint of antisymmetry). Examples verified on the page: any vector space with zero bracket; $\operatorname{Mat}(n\times n;\mathbb K)$ with the commutator (Jacobi expanded from associativity — E1.2.2); $\mathbb R^3$ with the cross product (Jacobi verified via the triple-product identity); $\mathfrak X(M)$ (via `Thm - The Space of Vector Fields is a Lie Algebra under the Lie Bracket`); non-example: $\operatorname{Mat}$ with $[A,B]:=AB$ (fails antisymmetry). R1.2.1 ("Jacobi replaces associativity") is the Axiom Motivation hook. Calibration: check that $\mathfrak{sl}(2;\mathbb R)$ is a subalgebra of $\mathfrak{gl}(2;\mathbb R)$; that the diagonal matrices form an abelian subalgebra.
- `Def - Left and Right Translations and Conjugation on a Lie Group` — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D1.2.4, B-D1.2.5 (→ link DG V `Thm - Pushforward of Vector Fields under a Diffeomorphism`), B-R1.2.2, B-R1.2.3; prereqs: [Def - Lie Group, Def - Left-Invariant Vector Field]; spec: Fixes the series notation $L_g$, $R_g$, $\alpha_g=L_g\circ R_{g^{-1}}$ and the push-forward $dF(X)$ of a vector field along a diffeomorphism; proves on the page that $L_g,R_g$ are diffeomorphisms, that $\alpha_g$ is a Lie group automorphism and $L_g,R_g$ are not homomorphisms (R1.2.2, with $G=\mathbb R$ as the verified counterexample), and that every $X_0\in T_eG$ extends uniquely to a left-invariant field $X(g)=d_eL_g(X_0)$ (R1.2.3). Convention callout: B reuses $L$ for actions on manifolds and for $L_p:G\to P$ in §2.2; the series writes $\theta_g$ for actions and $\ell_p$ for the orbit map.
- `Thm - The Space of Vector Fields is a Lie Algebra under the Lie Bracket` — type: theorem; source items: B-E1.2.4, B-T1.2.1, B-I1.2.1; prereqs: [Def - The Lie Bracket of Vector Fields, Def - Lie Algebra]; statement: For a smooth manifold $M$, $(\mathfrak X(M),[\cdot,\cdot])$ with $[X,Y]f=X(Yf)-Y(Xf)$ is a Lie algebra; moreover for a diffeomorphism $F\colon M\to N$, $F_*[X,Y]=[F_*X,F_*Y]$; proof: source omitted (cited as standard); write the full proof: $[X,Y]$ is a derivation (second-order terms cancel in coordinates), bilinearity, antisymmetry, Jacobi by expansion of the six terms; naturality by linking DG V `Thm - Pushforward of Vector Fields under a Diffeomorphism` Corollary (b) and restating; reference: Lee ISM 2e Lemma 8.25 and Prop 8.28; spec: this page replaces the flagged DG V `Thm - Lie Bracket Properties`; Targets: left-invariant fields form a subalgebra (link DG XI); fundamental vector fields (§1.5).
- `Thm - Lie Algebras and Dimensions of the Classical Matrix Groups` — type: theorem; source items: B-T1.2.4, B-D1.2.8, B-E1.2.5, B-E1.2.6, B-E1.2.7, B-E1.2.8, B-E1.2.9, B-E1.2.10, B-E1.2.11, B-I1.2.2, B-T1.2.3 (link), B-T1.2.2 (link); prereqs: [Def - Classical Matrix Groups, Def - The Lie Algebra of a Lie Group, Thm - Regular Value Theorem on Manifolds, Thm - Left-Invariant Vector Fields Form a Lie Algebra]; statement: With $\mathfrak g$ identified with $T_1G\subset\operatorname{Mat}(n\times n;\mathbb K)$ and the bracket the commutator: $\mathfrak{gl}(n;\mathbb R)=\operatorname{Mat}(n\times n;\mathbb R)$; $\mathfrak o(n)=\mathfrak{so}(n)=\{A:A^t+A=0\}$, $\dim=n(n-1)/2$; $\mathfrak{sl}(n;\mathbb R)=\{\operatorname{tr}A=0\}$, $\dim=n^2-1$; $\mathfrak u(n)=\{A^*=-A\}$, $\dim=n^2$; $\mathfrak{sl}(n;\mathbb C)=\{\operatorname{tr}A=0\}$, $\dim_{\mathbb R}=2n^2-2$; $\mathfrak{su}(n)=\{A^*=-A,\operatorname{tr}A=0\}$, $\dim=n^2-1$; $\mathfrak{sp}(1)=\operatorname{Im}\mathbb H$, $\dim=3$; proof: source full modulo dimension counts and $\tfrac{d}{ds}\det c(s)|_0=\operatorname{tr}\dot c(0)$; fill both: Lemma 1 (derivative of $\det$ at $1$ is $\operatorname{tr}$, by expanding $\det(1+sX)$); Lemma 2 (each group is the regular level set of $A\mapsto A^tA$, $\det$, $A^*A$ at $1$, with the differential surjective onto symmetric / $\mathbb R$ / Hermitian matrices — so DG IV's regular value theorem gives the dimension and identifies $T_1G$ with the kernel); Lemma 3 (the bracket of left-invariant fields on a matrix group is the commutator — link DG XI `Ex - The Lie Bracket on a Matrix Lie Algebra is the Commutator` and restate its argument); T1.2.3 ($\mathfrak g\cong T_eG$) is invoked by link to DG XI `Thm - Left-Invariant Vector Fields Form a Lie Algebra` (if the writer finds the isomorphism not proved there, it is Lemma 0 of this page); reference: Lee ISM 2e Ex. 8.? / Hall Ch. 3; spec: Sources: any closed subgroup defined by a smooth equation with surjective differential; Targets: all later dimension counts ($\dim SU(2)=3$, $\mathfrak u(1)=i\mathbb R$).
- `Ex - The Jacobi Identity for the Matrix Commutator Follows from Associativity` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E1.2.2; prereqs: [Def - Lie Subalgebra and Abelian Lie Algebra]; spec: expand the twelve terms; note the sub-case of `Thm - The Space of Vector Fields...` for constant-coefficient linear fields.
- `Ex - R^3 with the Cross Product is the Lie Algebra so(3)` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-E1.2.3; prereqs: [Thm - Lie Algebras and Dimensions of the Classical Matrix Groups]; spec: exhibit the isomorphism $v\mapsto(w\mapsto v\times w)$ onto antisymmetric $3\times3$ matrices and verify it is a Lie algebra isomorphism; link DG XI `Ex - The Lie Algebra of SO(3) is Antisymmetric Matrices`.
- `Ex - su(2) in the Basis of Anti-Hermitian Pauli Matrices` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B §1.3 conventions (basis $-i\sigma_1,-i\sigma_2,-i\sigma_3$), B-E1.3.2 (bracket relations only); prereqs: [Thm - Lie Algebras and Dimensions of the Classical Matrix Groups]; spec: with the standard Pauli matrices, compute $[-i\sigma_a,-i\sigma_b]=2\epsilon_{abc}(-i\sigma_c)$, show $\{-i\sigma_a\}$ is a basis of $\mathfrak{su}(2)$, and record in a Convention callout that B's printed $\sigma_1,\sigma_2$ are interchanged relative to the standard ones (the three matrices B lists still form a basis).
- `Ex - Conjugation is an Automorphism but Translations are Not Homomorphisms` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-R1.2.2; prereqs: [Def - Left and Right Translations and Conjugation on a Lie Group]; spec: prove $\alpha_g\in\operatorname{Aut}(G)$, $\alpha_{gh}=\alpha_g\circ\alpha_h$, and show $L_g$ is a homomorphism iff $g=e$.
- `Exercise Index - §1.2 Lie Algebras of the Classical Groups` — lists: [Ex - The Jacobi Identity for the Matrix Commutator Follows from Associativity, Ex - R^3 with the Cross Product is the Lie Algebra so(3), Ex - su(2) in the Basis of Anti-Hermitian Pauli Matrices, Ex - Conjugation is an Automorphism but Translations are Not Homomorphisms]

### §1.3 Representations of Lie Groups and Lie Algebras
- `Def - Representation of a Lie Group` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound: representation, faithful, trivial, standard, equivalence); source items: B-D1.3.1, B-D1.3.2, B-D1.3.3, B-D1.3.5, B-D1.3.12, B-R1.3.1, B-E1.3.4, B-E1.3.5; prereqs: [Def - Lie Group Homomorphism, Def - Classical Matrix Groups]; spec: A representation is a Lie group homomorphism $\varrho\colon G\to\operatorname{Aut}(V)=GL(V)$, $V$ finite-dimensional over $\mathbb K$; faithful means injective; equivalence is an intertwining isomorphism $T$. Verified examples: trivial representation (faithful iff $G=\{e\}$), the standard representations of the classical groups (E1.3.4), change of basis gives equivalent matrix representations (E1.3.5); non-example: $z\mapsto\bar z$ on $\mathbb C$ is a representation of $U(1)$ but $z\mapsto z^{1/2}$ is not well defined. Categorical: a $G$-module / a functor from $G$ to vector spaces. True name: "a linear $G$-action". Calibration: verify that the determinant $\det\colon GL(n;\mathbb K)\to GL(1;\mathbb K)$ is a one-dimensional representation and compute its kernel.
- `Def - Constructions on Representations` — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D1.3.6, B-D1.3.7, B-D1.3.8, B-D1.3.9, B-D1.3.10, B-D1.3.11, B-T1.3.3, B-R1.3.3; prereqs: [Def - Representation of a Lie Group, Def - Tensor Product of Vector Spaces, Def - Dual Space, Def - Alternating Multilinear Form]; spec: direct sum, tensor product, $\Lambda^k\varrho$, $\odot^k\varrho$ (symmetric power; define $\odot^kV$ as symmetric tensors with the DG VII symmetrisation projector), dual $\varrho^*(g)=\varrho(g^{-1})^*$, complexification. Each is verified on the page to be a representation (well-definedness on tensor products via the universal property, smoothness via matrix entries); T1.3.3 is proved as a corollary (the naive dual is an anti-homomorphism; the inverse repairs it — R1.3.3). Convention callout: the dual uses $g^{-1}$; B's $\odot^k$ notation. Calibration: $\dim\odot^k\mathbb C^2=k+1$; $\Lambda^n\varrho_{st}=\det$ for $GL(n)$.
- `Thm - Ad is a Smooth Representation and its Differential is ad` — type: theorem; source items: B-T1.3.1, B-T1.3.2, B-T1.4.7, B-D1.3.4 (→ link `Def - Adjoint Representation`), B-E1.3.1, B-E1.3.2, B-E1.3.3, B-D1.3.14 (ad); prereqs: [Def - Adjoint Representation, Def - Representation of a Lie Group, Def - Exponential Map of a Lie Group, Thm - Fundamental Theorem on Flows]; statement: (i) $\operatorname{Ad}\colon G\to GL(\mathfrak g)$, $\operatorname{Ad}_g=d_e\alpha_g$, is a Lie group representation; (ii) for $G\subset GL(n;\mathbb K)$ a matrix group, $\operatorname{Ad}_g X=gXg^{-1}$; (iii) $d_e\operatorname{Ad}=\operatorname{ad}$, i.e. $\tfrac{d}{dt}\big|_0\operatorname{Ad}_{\exp tX}Y=[X,Y]$ for every Lie group; (iv) if $G$ is abelian then $\operatorname{Ad}_g=\operatorname{id}$; proof: (i) source full except smoothness — prove smoothness from smoothness of $(g,h)\mapsto\alpha_g(h)$ by differentiating in the second variable (a smooth map $G\times\mathfrak g\to\mathfrak g$); (ii) source full; (iii) source only for matrix groups — give the general proof: the flow of the left-invariant field $Y$ is $R_{\exp tY}$ (Lemma, from `Thm - One-Parameter Subgroups are the Integral Curves of Left-Invariant Vector Fields`; write both pages so that the exp page precedes this one in reading order or move Lemma here), then $\operatorname{Ad}_{\exp tX}Y=(R_{\exp(-tX)})_*Y|_e$ and differentiate using the Lie derivative definition of the bracket (DG V `Def - Lie Derivative of a Vector Field`); reference: Lee ISM 2e Prop 20.24 and Thm 20.27; (iv) immediate; spec: E1.3.2 (explicit $\operatorname{Ad}_g$ of $SU(2)$ in the Pauli basis, a $2\varphi$-rotation) and E1.3.3 ($\operatorname{Ad}_{-1}=1$, not faithful) are worked as Targets and drilled in the exercise below. NOTE on ordering: place this page after `Thm - One-Parameter Subgroups...` in the chapter's dependency order (it is listed in §1.3 for thematic reasons; its prereqs include the §1.4 exp page, which the topic page must list first in its Notation Registry — acceptable because both are in chapter I; the writer of this page must not be started before the exp page exists).
- `Def - Representation of a Lie Algebra` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D1.3.13, B-D1.3.14, B-E1.3.8, B-R1.3.2, B-T1.3.8, B-T1.3.9, B-T1.4.9, B-I1.3.3, B-D1.4.4, B-T1.4.8 (→ link `Thm - Lie Group Homomorphism Induces Lie Algebra Homomorphism`); prereqs: [Def - Lie Algebra, Def - Representation of a Lie Group, Thm - Lie Group Homomorphism Induces Lie Algebra Homomorphism]; spec: A representation of $\mathfrak g$ is a Lie algebra homomorphism $\lambda\colon\mathfrak g\to\operatorname{End}(V)$ (commutator bracket); equivalence; the induced map $\varphi_*=d_e\varphi$ of a Lie group homomorphism. Corollaries proved on the page: $\operatorname{ad}$ is a representation (T1.3.8, Jacobi rewritten); $T_{\operatorname{id}}\operatorname{Aut}(V)=\operatorname{End}(V)$ because $GL(V)$ is open in $\operatorname{End}(V)$ (I1.3.3); the differential of a group representation is an algebra representation (T1.3.9/T1.4.9), by the linked DG XI theorem restated at the point of use. Verified examples: trivial $\lambda=0$; $\operatorname{ad}$; $\varrho_{st*}$ = inclusion; non-example: $X\mapsto X^t$ on $\mathfrak{gl}(n)$ is an anti-homomorphism. Calibration: compute $(\varrho_k)_*$ for the $U(1)$ representations of the next page: $i\theta\mapsto ik\theta$.
- `Thm - Complex Representations of U(1) and SU(2)` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: B-D1.3.15, B-D1.3.16, B-T1.3.4, B-T1.3.5, B-T1.3.6, B-T1.3.7, B-E1.3.6, B-E1.3.7, B-R1.3.4, B-I1.3.1, B-I1.3.2; prereqs: [Def - Representation of a Lie Group, Def - Representation of a Lie Algebra, Def - Constructions on Representations, Thm - Ad is a Smooth Representation and its Differential is ad, Thm - The Exponential Map of a Matrix Group is the Matrix Exponential, Def - Unitary Operator]; statement: (A) The representations $\varrho_k\colon U(1)\to GL(1;\mathbb C)$, $z\mapsto z^k$ ($k\in\mathbb Z$) are pairwise inequivalent irreducible representations; every complex representation of $U(1)$ is equivalent to a direct sum of $\varrho_k$'s, uniquely up to order; $\varrho_k\otimes\varrho_l\cong\varrho_{k+l}$ and $\varrho_k^*\cong\varrho_{-k}$. (B) With $\varrho_0$ trivial, $\varrho_1$ the standard representation of $SU(2)$ on $\mathbb C^2$ and $\varrho_k:=\odot^k\varrho_1$ on $\odot^k\mathbb C^2$ ($\dim=k+1$): each $\varrho_k$ is irreducible, $\varrho_k\not\cong\varrho_l$ for $k\ne l$, and every complex representation of $SU(2)$ is equivalent to a direct sum of $\varrho_k$'s, uniquely up to order. (C) $\varrho_2\cong(\operatorname{Ad}_{SU(2)})_{\mathbb C}$, with the explicit intertwiner $T$ of B (Ex. 1.3.15) in the basis $e_1\odot e_1,e_2\odot e_2,e_2\odot e_1$ versus $-i\sigma_1,-i\sigma_2,-i\sigma_3$; proof: source: (A) tensor/dual full, complete reducibility omitted; (B) omitted; (C) verified for diagonal $g$ only. Write everything: Lemma 1 (unitarian trick: a compact Lie group admits a bi-invariant Haar integral — for $U(1)$ the $d\theta/2\pi$ integral, for $SU(2)\cong S^3$ the normalised round volume form, which is invariant because left multiplication by unit quaternions is an isometry of $\mathbb R^4$ — so every representation admits an invariant Hermitian inner product and invariant subspaces have invariant complements; hence complete reducibility); Lemma 2 (a commuting family of diagonalisable operators has a common eigenvector, so irreducible representations of an abelian group are one-dimensional); Lemma 3 (every continuous homomorphism $U(1)\to U(1)$ is $z\mapsto z^k$: lift $\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ through $e^{i\theta}$ by the path-lifting property of the covering $\mathbb R\to S^1$ — link AT II `Thm - Path Lifting and Homotopy Lifting` — a continuous additive map $\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ is linear, and $2\pi\mapsto2\pi k$); Lemma 4 (uniqueness of the decomposition via characters or via weight multiplicities); Lemma 5 (Lie-algebra passage: for connected $G$ a subspace is $\varrho(G)$-invariant iff $\varrho_*(\mathfrak g)$-invariant — uses that the subgroup generated by $\exp(U)$ is open, hence equals $G$, and `Thm - The Exponential Map is a Local Diffeomorphism at the Origin`); Lemma 6 (irreducible representations of $\mathfrak{sl}(2;\mathbb C)=\mathfrak{su}(2)\otimes\mathbb C$: highest-weight argument with $H,E,F$; each has a basis $v_0,\dots,v_k$ with $Hv_j=(k-2j)v_j$; two irreducibles of equal dimension are equivalent; $\odot^k\mathbb C^2$ realises dimension $k+1$); Lemma 7 (weights of $\odot^k\varrho_1$ are $k,k-2,\dots,-k$, each once, so $\varrho_k$ is irreducible and the $\varrho_k$ are distinct); (C) by Lemma 5 it suffices to check $T$ intertwines $\varrho_{2*}$ and $\operatorname{ad}_{\mathbb C}$ on the basis $-i\sigma_a$, a $3\times3$ computation done in full; R1.3.4 is the dimension-count motivation; reference: Hall, *Lie Groups, Lie Algebras, and Representations* 2e, Thm 4.28, Prop 4.36, Thm 4.32; Bröcker–tom Dieck Ch. II (5.3); spec: Why true: compactness turns every representation unitary; the $H$-eigenvalues are integers because $\exp(2\pi iH)=1$ in $SU(2)$. Targets: the associated bundles $P\times_{\varrho_k}\mathbb C$ of a $U(1)$-bundle are the tensor powers $L^{\otimes k}$ (III), spinor bundles of $SU(2)$ (VIII).
- `Ex - The Adjoint Representation of SU(2) in the Pauli Basis` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-E1.3.2, B-E1.3.3; prereqs: [Thm - Ad is a Smooth Representation and its Differential is ad, Ex - su(2) in the Basis of Anti-Hermitian Pauli Matrices]; spec: for $g=\operatorname{diag}(e^{i\varphi},e^{-i\varphi})$ compute $\operatorname{Ad}_g$ as the rotation matrix by $2\varphi$ in the plane of $-i\sigma_1,-i\sigma_2$; deduce $\ker\operatorname{Ad}=\{\pm1\}$; link DG XI `Ex - The Adjoint Representation of SO(3) is the Defining Representation`.
- `Ex - Tensor Products and Duals of the Representations of U(1)` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-T1.3.4; prereqs: [Def - Constructions on Representations, Thm - Complex Representations of U(1) and SU(2)]; spec: exhibit the isomorphisms $\mathbb C\otimes\mathbb C\cong\mathbb C$ and $\mathbb C^*\cong\mathbb C$ and verify equivariance.
- `Ex - rho_2 of SU(2) is the Complexified Adjoint Representation` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: B-T1.3.6, B-E1.3.7, B-R1.3.4; prereqs: [Thm - Complex Representations of U(1) and SU(2)]; spec: carry out the full check of B's matrix $T$ on all of $SU(2)$ via the Lie algebra (Lemma 5 route), then the alternative route via the classification (both 3-dimensional irreducible).
- `Ex - The Dual Representation Requires the Inverse` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-T1.3.3, B-R1.3.3; prereqs: [Def - Constructions on Representations]; spec: show $g\mapsto\varrho(g)^*$ is an anti-homomorphism and $g\mapsto\varrho(g^{-1})^*$ a homomorphism; identify the dual of $\varrho_{st}$ of $GL(n)$ with $g\mapsto(g^{-1})^t$.
- `Ex - Decomposing the Tensor Square of the Standard Representation of SU(2)` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: none in sources (standard problem: Bröcker–tom Dieck II.5 Clebsch–Gordan, smallest case); prereqs: [Thm - Complex Representations of U(1) and SU(2), Def - Constructions on Representations]; spec: prove $\varrho_1\otimes\varrho_1\cong\varrho_2\oplus\varrho_0$ by weights (the $H$-eigenvalues are $2,0,0,-2$) and exhibit the invariant vector $e_1\otimes e_2-e_2\otimes e_1$.
- `Exercise Index - §1.3 Representations of Lie Groups and Lie Algebras` — lists: [Ex - The Adjoint Representation of SU(2) in the Pauli Basis, Ex - Tensor Products and Duals of the Representations of U(1), Ex - rho_2 of SU(2) is the Complexified Adjoint Representation, Ex - The Dual Representation Requires the Inverse, Ex - Decomposing the Tensor Square of the Standard Representation of SU(2)]

### §1.4 The Exponential Map
- (links) `Def - Exponential Map of a Lie Group`, `Def - One-Parameter Subgroup` (B-D1.4.2), `Thm - Naturality of the Exponential Map` (B-T1.4.6), `Thm - Lie Group Homomorphism Induces Lie Algebra Homomorphism` (B-T1.4.8), `Ex - The Exponential Map of GL(n,R) is the Matrix Exponential`.
- `Thm - One-Parameter Subgroups are the Integral Curves of Left-Invariant Vector Fields` — type: theorem; source items: B-T1.4.1, B-T1.4.2, B-D1.4.1, B-X1.4.1, B-I1.4.4, B-I1.4.1, B-R1.4.1; prereqs: [Def - Left-Invariant Vector Field, Def - Exponential Map of a Lie Group, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Integral Curves, Thm - Fundamental Theorem on Flows]; statement: Let $\gamma\colon\mathbb R\to G$ be smooth with $\gamma(0)=e$. Then $\gamma$ is a group homomorphism iff $\gamma$ is an integral curve of a left-invariant vector field. Every left-invariant field is complete, its integral curve $\gamma_X$ through $e$ satisfies $\gamma_X(t)=\exp(tX)$, hence $\exp((s+t)X)=\exp(sX)\exp(tX)$, $\exp(0)=e$, $\exp(-X)=\exp(X)^{-1}$; the flow of $X$ is $R_{\exp tX}$; $\exp$ is smooth; proof: "⇒" source full; "⇐" omitted — write it: if $\gamma$ is an integral curve of $X\in\mathfrak g$ then $s\mapsto\gamma(t)\gamma(s)$ and $s\mapsto\gamma(t+s)$ are both integral curves of $X$ through $\gamma(t)$ (left-invariance), so they agree by uniqueness; completeness (X1.4.1): an integral curve on $(-\epsilon,\epsilon)$ extends by left translation to all of $\mathbb R$ (Lemma with the gluing argument); smoothness of $\exp$ from smooth dependence of the flow on the point of $G\times\mathfrak g$ (link DG V `Thm - Fundamental Theorem on Flows`); reference: Lee ISM 2e Prop 20.5, Thm 20.8 (parts a–d); spec: also states as corollary that $\gamma_X$ is the unique one-parameter subgroup with initial velocity $X$ (link `Def - One-Parameter Subgroup`).
- `Thm - The Exponential Map is a Local Diffeomorphism at the Origin` — type: theorem; source items: B-T1.4.3, B-T1.4.4, B-T1.4.5, B-T1.4.10, B-I1.4.2; prereqs: [Thm - One-Parameter Subgroups are the Integral Curves of Left-Invariant Vector Fields, Thm - The Rank Theorem]; statement: $d_0\exp=\operatorname{id}_{\mathfrak g}$; hence there are neighbourhoods $U\ni0$, $V\ni e$ with $\exp|_U\colon U\to V$ a diffeomorphism; $d_e\operatorname{inv}=-\operatorname{id}_{\mathfrak g}$; Corollary: if $G$ is abelian then $[\mathfrak g,\mathfrak g]=0$; proof: source full for all parts (the corollary via $\operatorname{inv}_*=-\operatorname{id}$ being a Lie algebra homomorphism, linking DG XI `Thm - Lie Group Homomorphism Induces Lie Algebra Homomorphism`); the inverse function theorem is invoked through DG IV `Thm - The Rank Theorem` (a map of full rank at a point is a local diffeomorphism) — restate; reference: Lee ISM 2e Prop 20.8(f)–(g); spec: Targets: charts of $G$ near $e$; the open-subgroup argument for connected groups (used in §1.3 Lemma 5).
- `Thm - The Exponential Map of a Matrix Group is the Matrix Exponential` — type: theorem; source items: B-T1.4.11, B-D1.4.3, B-R1.4.2, B-E1.4.1; prereqs: [Thm - One-Parameter Subgroups are the Integral Curves of Left-Invariant Vector Fields, Thm - The Closed Subgroup Theorem, Def - Classical Matrix Groups]; statement: for $X\in\operatorname{Mat}(n\times n;\mathbb K)$ the series $e^X=\sum_{k\ge0}X^k/k!$ converges absolutely, $e^{(s+t)X}=e^{sX}e^{tX}$, $\tfrac{d}{dt}e^{tX}=Xe^{tX}$; for a closed subgroup $G\subset GL(n;\mathbb K)$ with Lie algebra $\mathfrak g\subset\operatorname{Mat}$ and $X\in\mathfrak g$, $e^{tX}\in G$ for all $t$ and $\exp_G(X)=e^X$; proof: source full modulo "$e^{tX}\in G$" — fill via the closed subgroup theorem's characterisation $\mathfrak h=\{X:\exp(tX)\in H\ \forall t\}$ (link DG XI, restate) and via $\exp_{GL}=e^{(\cdot)}$ (Cauchy product, R1.4.2); E1.4.1 ($e^A$ for $A\in\mathfrak{so}(2)$ is the rotation matrix; surjective, not injective) is a worked corollary; reference: Hall Prop 2.3, Thm 3.28 / Lee ISM 2e Prop 20.2; spec: link DG XI `Ex - The Exponential Map of GL(n,R) is the Matrix Exponential`.
- `Thm - The Exponential Map of a Compact Connected Lie Group is Surjective` — type: theorem; source items: B-T1.4.12, B-I1.4.3; prereqs: [Thm - One-Parameter Subgroups are the Integral Curves of Left-Invariant Vector Fields, Thm - Ad is a Smooth Representation and its Differential is ad, Thm - Fundamental Theorem of Riemannian Geometry (Full Proof), Thm - Koszul Formula, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Geodesics, Thm - The Gauss Lemma, Def - The Riemannian Exponential Map, Thm - Existence of Smooth Partitions of Unity]; statement: If $G$ is a compact connected Lie group then $\exp\colon\mathfrak g\to G$ is surjective; proof: source omitted. Follow Milnor, *Morse Theory* §21 (Lemma 21.2–21.3) and do Carmo, *Riemannian Geometry* Ch. 7 Thm 2.8: Lemma 1 (a compact Lie group carries a bi-invariant Riemannian metric: average any inner product on $\mathfrak g$ over $\operatorname{Ad}(G)$ using a left-invariant volume form, which is bi-invariant because $|\det\operatorname{Ad}_g|=1$ — the continuous homomorphism $G\to\mathbb R_{>0}$ has compact image — then left-translate); Lemma 2 (for a bi-invariant metric the Levi-Civita connection satisfies $\nabla_XY=\tfrac12[X,Y]$ on left-invariant fields, by the Koszul formula and $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariance, so the geodesics through $e$ are the one-parameter subgroups $t\mapsto\exp(tX)$); Lemma 3 (on a compact Riemannian manifold geodesics are defined for all time: the geodesic vector field on the compact unit sphere bundle is complete — DG V `Ex - Compactly Supported Vector Fields are Complete` / `Thm - Fundamental Theorem on Flows`); Lemma 4 (on a compact connected Riemannian manifold any two points are joined by a minimizing geodesic — the do Carmo Ch. 7 Thm 2.8 argument written in full for the compact case, using the Gauss lemma and normal balls from RG II; this replaces the flagged Hopf–Rinow page); conclude: for $g\in G$ take a minimizing geodesic from $e$, which is $t\mapsto\exp(tX)$, so $g=\exp(X)$; spec: Convention: the metric on $\mathfrak g$ is $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariant; for $SU(n)$ take $-\operatorname{tr}(XY)$. Targets: $SU(2)$, $U(n)$, $SO(n)$ are exponential images; used in VI to show $SU(2)$-valued maps deform (not needed elsewhere in I–VI; recorded).
- `Ex - Maximal Integral Curves of Left-Invariant Fields are Defined on All of R` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-X1.4.1; prereqs: [Thm - One-Parameter Subgroups are the Integral Curves of Left-Invariant Vector Fields]; spec: the extension-by-translation argument, as a stand-alone drill with the gluing lemma written out.
- `Ex - The Exponential Map of so(2) is Surjective but Not Injective` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E1.4.1; prereqs: [Thm - The Exponential Map of a Matrix Group is the Matrix Exponential]; spec: compute $A^{2k}$, $A^{2k+1}$, sum the series, identify the kernel $2\pi\mathbb Z$.
- `Ex - The Exponential Map of SL(2,R) is Not Surjective` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: standard (Hall Ex. 3.?; contrast to the compact theorem); prereqs: [Thm - The Exponential Map of a Matrix Group is the Matrix Exponential, Thm - Lie Algebras and Dimensions of the Classical Matrix Groups]; spec: show $\operatorname{diag}(-2,-1/2)\notin\exp(\mathfrak{sl}(2;\mathbb R))$ using that $e^X$ for traceless $X$ has eigenvalues $e^{\pm\lambda}$ with $\lambda$ real or purely imaginary, so a negative eigenvalue forces $\lambda\in i\pi\mathbb Z$ and equal eigenvalues.
- `Ex - Computing exp on su(2) via the Quaternion Formula` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: none (standard); prereqs: [Thm - The Exponential Map of a Matrix Group is the Matrix Exponential, Ex - SU(2) is the Group of Unit Quaternions]; spec: for $X=\theta\,n\cdot(-i\sigma)$ with $|n|=1$ prove $e^X=\cos\theta\,1+\sin\theta\,n\cdot(-i\sigma)$; deduce that $\exp\colon\mathfrak{su}(2)\to SU(2)$ is onto and that the ball of radius $\pi$ maps onto $SU(2)$ with the boundary sphere collapsed to $-1$ (used in III §3.5 for maps $S^3\to SU(2)$).
- `Exercise Index - §1.4 The Exponential Map` — lists: [Ex - Maximal Integral Curves of Left-Invariant Fields are Defined on All of R, Ex - The Exponential Map of so(2) is Surjective but Not Injective, Ex - The Exponential Map of SL(2,R) is Not Surjective, Ex - Computing exp on su(2) via the Quaternion Formula]

### §1.5 Group Actions, Orbit Spaces, and Quotient Manifolds
- `Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D1.5.1 (→ link `Def - Smooth Action of a Lie Group`), B-D1.5.2, B-D1.5.3, B-D1.5.4, B-D1.5.5, B-D1.5.6, B-D1.5.7, B-D1.5.13, B-T1.5.1, B-T1.5.2, B-T1.5.9, B-E1.5.1, B-E1.5.2, B-E1.5.3, B-E1.5.4, B-E1.5.5; prereqs: [Def - Smooth Action of a Lie Group, Def - Classical Matrix Groups, Def - Quotient Topology and Identification Map]; spec: Restates DG XI's definition of smooth left and right actions and defines effective, free, transitive, proper (the map $(g,x)\mapsto(g\cdot x,x)$ is proper), the centre $Z(G)$, orbits, stabilisers, and the orbit space $G\backslash M$ (left) / $M/G$ (right) with the quotient topology. Corollaries proved on the page: $g\mapsto\theta_g$ is a homomorphism into $\operatorname{Diff}(M)$ (T1.5.1); free implies effective (T1.5.2); a compact group acts properly (closed-map argument); $p*g:=g^{-1}\cdot p$ turns a left action into a right action and conversely (T1.5.9, both directions written). Verified examples: trivial action (effective iff $G=\{e\}$), the action induced by a representation (never transitive unless $V=0$), left multiplication (free and transitive), conjugation (effective iff $Z(G)=\{e\}$), rotation of $S^2$ about the $z$-axis (effective, not free, not transitive; orbit space $[-1,1]$ via the $z$-coordinate — E1.5.5), scalar $U(1)$-action on $S^{2n-1}$ (free; transitive iff $n=1$). Convention callout: B writes $G\backslash M$ even for right actions; the series writes $P/G$ for right actions on bundles. Categorical: an action is a functor from $G$ (one-object groupoid) to manifolds. Calibration: decide freeness/effectiveness for $SO(3)$ on $S^2$, for $\mathbb Z/2$ on $S^n$ by the antipodal map, for $\mathbb R$ on $T^2$ by an irrational line.
- `Def - Complex Projective Space as a Quotient` — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D1.5.9, B-D1.5.8, B-E1.5.6, B-E1.5.7, B-R1.5.3, B-T1.5.5; prereqs: [Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions, Thm - Quotient Manifold Theorem for Free Proper Actions]; spec: $\mathbb{CP}^{n-1}:=S^{2n-1}/U(1)$ (manifold structure by the quotient theorem, verified: free, compact group), identified with the set of complex lines in $\mathbb C^n$ and with the quotient of $\mathbb C^n\setminus0$ by $\mathbb C^*$ (homogeneous coordinates; smoothness of the identification proved); the Hopf map $S^3\to S^2$ in B's stereographic normalisation and in A's $z\mapsto[z]$ form (Convention callout reconciling B's formula with AT III `Def - The Hopf Map`); the description of Hopf circles under stereographic projection (E1.5.6: $\mathbb R^3$ is a disjoint union of circles and one line — proved by the explicit parametrisation). Corollary proved on the page: the cell decomposition $\mathbb{CP}^n=\mathbb{CP}^{n-1}\sqcup\mathbb C^n$ with $\mathbb{CP}^n\setminus\mathbb{CP}^{n-1}\cong\mathbb C^n$ a diffeomorphism onto an open set (T1.5.5; used in III §3.6 for the Mayer–Vietoris computation). Links DG I `Ex - Real Projective Space is a Smooth Manifold`, DG IV `Ex - The Hopf Map is a Submersion`.
- `Thm - CP^1 is Diffeomorphic to S^2 via the Hopf Map` — type: theorem; source items: B-T1.5.4, B-I1.5.4; prereqs: [Def - Complex Projective Space as a Quotient, Thm - Quotient Manifold Theorem for Free Proper Actions]; statement: $\dim_{\mathbb R}\mathbb{CP}^{n-1}=2(n-1)$, $\mathbb{CP}^{n-1}$ is compact and connected, and the map $\widetilde{\mathrm{Hopf}}\colon\mathbb{CP}^1\to S^2$ induced by B's Hopf map is a diffeomorphism; proof: source sketch ("explicit computation"); write it: the induced map is well defined and bijective (fibres of Hopf are the $U(1)$-orbits — computed), smooth by the universal property of the quotient, and of maximal rank everywhere (compute the differential in the two affine charts $[1:w]$, $[w:1]$; the Hopf map composed with a local section is the inverse of stereographic projection, hence a local diffeomorphism), so a bijective local diffeomorphism, hence a diffeomorphism; spec: Bridges to AT III `Def - The Hopf Map` and to `Def - The Hopf Bundle` (III).
- `Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action` **[anchor]** — type: definition; source items: B-D1.5.10, B-E1.5.8, B-R1.5.2, B-T1.5.7 (flow statement), B-I2.4.2, A-D2.2.10 (the infinitesimal action $K_\xi$ and vertical subspace — the vertical-subspace part is restated in III); prereqs: [Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions, Thm - One-Parameter Subgroups are the Integral Curves of Left-Invariant Vector Fields]; spec: For a left action, $\bar X(p):=\tfrac{d}{dt}\big|_0\exp(tX)\cdot p=d_e\ell_p(X)$ with $\ell_p(g)=g\cdot p$; for a right action (the series' convention on bundles), $\xi_P(p):=\tfrac{d}{dt}\big|_0p\cdot\exp(t\xi)$; smoothness proved (it is $d\theta$ applied to the field $(X,0)$ on $G\times M$). Corollaries proved on the page: the flow of $\bar X$ is $t\mapsto\theta_{\exp tX}$ (T1.5.7 first half); $\bar X(p)=0$ iff $\exp(tX)\cdot p=p$ for all $t$; for a free action $X\mapsto\bar X(p)$ is injective, so a free action of a group with $\dim G\ge1$ gives a nowhere-vanishing vector field on $M$. The consequence "$\chi(M)=0$, so no positive-dimensional Lie group acts freely on $S^{2n}$" (R1.5.2, I1.5.3) is stated in bold plain text as a forward reference to chapter III's `Thm - Hairy Ball Theorem`, where it is proved as a corollary. Verified example: E1.5.8 (fundamental fields of the $z$-axis rotation are tangent to latitude circles, $\bar X=\partial_\varphi$); non-example: the velocity field of a non-group flow. Convention callout: B's $\bar X$ (left) vs. A's $K_\xi$ and the series' $\xi_P$ (right).
- `Thm - The Fundamental Vector Field Map is a Lie Algebra Homomorphism for Right Actions` — type: theorem; source items: B-T1.5.6; prereqs: [Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action, Thm - The Space of Vector Fields is a Lie Algebra under the Lie Bracket, Thm - Ad is a Smooth Representation and its Differential is ad]; statement: For a smooth right action of $G$ on $M$, $\xi\mapsto\xi_M$ is a Lie algebra homomorphism $\mathfrak g\to\mathfrak X(M)$: $[\xi,\eta]_M=[\xi_M,\eta_M]$; for a left action $X\mapsto\bar X$ is an anti-homomorphism, $[\bar X,\bar Y]=-\overline{[X,Y]}$; moreover $(\theta_g)_*\xi_M=(\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\xi)_M$ for right actions (i.e. $dR_g(\xi_M(p))=(\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\xi)_M(pg)$); proof: source stated without proof and without the sign; write: the map $\xi\mapsto\xi_M$ is the push-forward under the orbit maps of the left-invariant fields on $G$ for a right action (compute $d\ell_p(\tilde\xi)=\xi_M\circ\ell_p$, i.e. $\tilde\xi$ and $\xi_M$ are $\ell_p$-related), and related fields have related brackets (Lemma, Lee ISM 2e Prop 8.30); for left actions the right-invariant fields appear and the sign flips; the equivariance identity by differentiating $R_g(p\exp(t\xi))=(pg)\exp(t\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\xi)$; reference: Lee ISM 2e Thm 20.18, Kobayashi–Nomizu I Prop I.4.1; spec: this is the identity B uses silently in the structure equation (IV) and Haydys's "$(R_g)_*K_\xi=K_{\operatorname{ad}_{g^{-1}}\xi}$" in Theorem 46.
- `Thm - Quotient Manifold Theorem for Free Proper Actions` — type: theorem; source items: B-T1.5.3, B-I1.5.1, B-E1.5.7 (application), A-I2.2.2 (partial), B-I2.2.2, B-I2.2.3; prereqs: [Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions, Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action, Thm - Regular Value Theorem on Manifolds, Def - Equivariant Map, Thm - Universal Property of the Quotient]; statement: Let $G$ act smoothly, freely and properly on $M$ (either side). Then the orbit space carries a unique smooth manifold structure of dimension $\dim M-\dim G$ such that the quotient map $\pi$ is a smooth submersion; the quotient is Hausdorff and second countable; $\pi$ admits smooth local sections; and for every smooth $f\colon M\to N$ constant on orbits there is a unique smooth $\tilde f$ with $\tilde f\circ\pi=f$. In particular this holds when $G$ is compact and the action free; proof: source "idea of proof" only. Follow Lee ISM 2e Thm 21.10 in full: Step 0 (Hausdorff and second countable: Prop 21.5 / Lemma 21.11 — properness gives closed orbit relation and quotient Hausdorffness; open quotient map preserves second countability); Step 1 (slice construction: for $p\in M$ the orbit is an embedded submanifold and there is a slice $S$ through $p$ transverse to it, via the equivariant rank theorem — DG XI `Def - Equivariant Map` records it; write the proof — and the regular value theorem); Step 2 (the restricted action $G\times S\to M$ is a diffeomorphism onto an open set after shrinking; injectivity uses properness and freeness); Step 3 (the slices give charts of the quotient; transition maps smooth); Step 4 (uniqueness of the structure and the universal property from the local sections); reference: Lee ISM 2e Thm 21.10, Lemma 21.11, Thm 21.13; spec: Sources: compact group + free; discrete group + properly discontinuous (next theorem); Targets: $\mathbb{CP}^{n-1}$, $G/H$ (link DG XI `Thm - Homogeneous Space is a Smooth Manifold` — this theorem is its generalisation), associated bundles (III), principal bundles from free actions (III). Convention callout: A calls the free action on $P\times V$ "properly discontinuous"; the correct hypothesis is free and proper.
- `Def - Discrete Group and Properly Discontinuous Action` — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D1.5.11, B-D1.5.12, B-R1.5.1, B-E1.5.9, B-E1.5.10; prereqs: [Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions]; spec: a discrete group is a $0$-dimensional Lie group (countable by second countability); properly discontinuous means B's (i)+(ii); corollary proved on the page: for a discrete group, properly discontinuous $\iff$ free and proper (both directions written; this reconciles B with Lee); R1.5.1 (discrete + compact = finite). Verified examples: $\mathbb Z$ on $\mathbb R$ by translation with B's explicit neighbourhoods (E1.5.9); the deck action of $\mathbb Z^2$ on $\mathbb R^2$; non-example: $(\mathbb Q,+)$ on $\mathbb R$ (E1.5.10, orbits dense, quotient not Hausdorff — proved). Calibration: is the antipodal action of $\mathbb Z/2$ on $S^n$ properly discontinuous? Is $\mathbb Z$ acting on $S^1$ by an irrational rotation?
- `Thm - Quotient by a Properly Discontinuous Action is a Manifold and a Covering Map` — type: theorem; source items: B-T1.5.8, B-I1.5.2; prereqs: [Def - Discrete Group and Properly Discontinuous Action, Thm - Quotient Manifold Theorem for Free Proper Actions, Def - Covering Space]; statement: If a discrete group $\Gamma$ acts smoothly and properly discontinuously on $M$, then $\Gamma\backslash M$ has a unique smooth structure making $\pi\colon M\to\Gamma\backslash M$ a smooth covering map (in particular a local diffeomorphism), and the universal property holds; proof: source sketch; write it: apply the quotient manifold theorem (free and proper by the corollary on the Def page), then show the neighbourhoods $U$ of (i) are evenly covered: $\pi^{-1}(\pi(U))=\bigsqcup_{g}gU$, each $gU\to\pi(U)$ a diffeomorphism; link AT II `Def - Covering Space` (smooth covering map); reference: Lee ISM 2e Thm 21.13; spec: Targets: mapping tori (III), the universal cover as a principal $\pi_1$-bundle (V), $T^n=\mathbb R^n/\mathbb Z^n$.
- `Ex - Rotation of the Sphere about an Axis` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E1.5.3, B-E1.5.5, B-E1.5.8; prereqs: [Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions, Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action]; spec: prove effective, not free, not transitive; identify orbits and the orbit space $[-1,1]$ (a manifold with boundary, not a manifold — note the failure of freeness); compute the fundamental vector field.
- `Ex - The Scalar Action of U(1) on Odd Spheres is Free` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E1.5.4; prereqs: [Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions]; spec: freeness; transitivity iff $n=1$; identify the orbit space with $\mathbb{CP}^{n-1}$ (link the Def).
- `Ex - The Circle as the Quotient of R by the Integers` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-E1.5.9; prereqs: [Thm - Quotient by a Properly Discontinuous Action is a Manifold and a Covering Map]; spec: verify (i),(ii) with B's explicit $\epsilon$; construct $\tilde f\colon\mathbb Z\backslash\mathbb R\to S^1$ from $f(t)=(\cos2\pi t,\sin2\pi t)$ and prove it is a diffeomorphism (bijective, $d\tilde f\ne0$, compactness not needed: bijective local diffeomorphism).
- `Ex - The Rationals Acting on R are Not Properly Discontinuous` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E1.5.10; prereqs: [Def - Discrete Group and Properly Discontinuous Action]; spec: density of orbits; the quotient topology is indiscrete, hence not Hausdorff.
- `Ex - Any Two Hopf Circles are Linked` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: B-E1.5.6 (second half), B-R1.5.3; prereqs: [Def - Complex Projective Space as a Quotient, Thm - CP^1 is Diffeomorphic to S^2 via the Hopf Map]; spec: under stereographic projection from a point of the fibre over the south pole, show that fibre becomes the $z$-axis and every other fibre a round circle; take the fibre $C_0$ over the north pole (the unit circle in the $xy$-plane) and prove every other Hopf circle meets the flat disc bounded by $C_0$ in exactly one point, transversally — the linking criterion used as the definition of "linked" on the page; route: explicit parametrisation $(e^{i\theta}\cos\alpha,e^{i\theta}\sin\alpha)$, $\alpha\in(0,\pi/2)$.
- `Ex - Conjugation and the Centre` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E1.5.2 (conjugation parts), B-D1.5.6; prereqs: [Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions]; spec: kernel of the conjugation action is $Z(G)$; compute $Z(U(n))=U(1)\cdot1$, $Z(SU(2))=\{\pm1\}$, $Z(SO(3))=\{1\}$.
- `Exercise Index - §1.5 Group Actions, Orbit Spaces, and Quotient Manifolds` — lists: [Ex - Rotation of the Sphere about an Axis, Ex - The Scalar Action of U(1) on Odd Spheres is Free, Ex - The Circle as the Quotient of R by the Integers, Ex - The Rationals Acting on R are Not Properly Discontinuous, Ex - Any Two Hopf Circles are Linked, Ex - Conjugation and the Centre]

Dependency order for chapter I (each page's prereqs precede it): Def - Classical Matrix Groups → Thm - SO(2) is Isomorphic to U(1) → Def - Lie Subalgebra and Abelian Lie Algebra → Def - Left and Right Translations and Conjugation on a Lie Group → Thm - The Space of Vector Fields is a Lie Algebra under the Lie Bracket → Thm - Lie Algebras and Dimensions of the Classical Matrix Groups → Def - Representation of a Lie Group → Def - Constructions on Representations → Thm - One-Parameter Subgroups are the Integral Curves of Left-Invariant Vector Fields → Thm - The Exponential Map is a Local Diffeomorphism at the Origin → Thm - The Exponential Map of a Matrix Group is the Matrix Exponential → Thm - Ad is a Smooth Representation and its Differential is ad → Def - Representation of a Lie Algebra → Thm - Complex Representations of U(1) and SU(2) → Thm - The Exponential Map of a Compact Connected Lie Group is Surjective → Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions → Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action → Thm - The Fundamental Vector Field Map is a Lie Algebra Homomorphism for Right Actions → Thm - Quotient Manifold Theorem for Free Proper Actions → Def - Complex Projective Space as a Quotient → Thm - CP^1 is Diffeomorphic to S^2 via the Hopf Map → Def - Discrete Group and Properly Discontinuous Action → Thm - Quotient by a Properly Discontinuous Action is a Manifold and a Covering Map → exercises.
Page count chapter I: 10 Def + 13 Thm + 22 Ex + 5 Exercise Index + 1 topic page = 51.
