### §2.1 Vector Bundles, Bundle-Valued Forms, and Pull-Backs
- (links) DG VI definitions above; A-E2.1.1 (product bundle, $TM$) and A-R2.1.1 (informal description) are restated inside `Def - Operations on Vector Bundles and Pull-Back Bundles` (Axiom Motivation / Examples); B-E2.1.5 (sections of $TB$, $T^*B$, $\Lambda^kT^*B$, tensor bundles are vector fields, forms, tensor fields) and B-R2.1.1 (the zero section; general fibre bundles may have no section) go inside the same page (Examples).
- `Def - Operations on Vector Bundles and Pull-Back Bundles` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.1.3, A-D2.1.4, A-R2.1.2, A-E2.1.1, A-R2.1.1, B-D2.1.11 (vector-bundle case), B-T2.1.2 (vector-bundle case), B-E2.1.4, B-E2.1.5, B-E2.1.6, B-R2.1.1; prereqs: [Def - Vector Bundle, Def - Local Trivialization, Def - Transition Function of a Vector Bundle, Thm - Vector Bundle Construction Lemma, Def - Tensor Product of Vector Spaces, Def - Dual Space]; spec: Defines fibrewise $E^*$, $\Lambda^pE$, $E\oplus F$, $E\otimes F$, $\operatorname{Hom}(E,F)$, $\operatorname{End}E$, the pull-back $f^*E$ along $f\colon M'\to M$ and the restriction $E|_{M'}$. R2.1.2 is discharged on the page: for each operation the local trivialisations and transition functions are written down ($\tau^*=(\tau^{-1})^t$, $\Lambda^p\tau$, $\tau_E\oplus\tau_F$, $\tau_E\otimes\tau_F$, $\phi\mapsto\tau_F\phi\tau_E^{-1}$, $\tau\circ f$) and the construction lemma is invoked; the pull-back is also shown to be the fibre-product submanifold $\{(m',e):f(m')=\pi(e)\}$ (B's definition) with $\operatorname{pr}_2$ a fibrewise isomorphism (B-T2.1.2 written in full for vector bundles; the general fibre-bundle version is `Thm - The Pull-Back of a Fibre Bundle is a Fibre Bundle` in III). Verified examples: $\operatorname{Hom}(E,F)\cong E^*\otimes F$ (see X2.1.1 exercise; on the page verify fibrewise and check the transition functions agree), $T^*M=(TM)^*$, $\Lambda^pT^*M$, vector fields along a curve as sections of $\gamma^*TM$ (B-E2.1.6, with the velocity field as an example); non-example: the "bundle" $\bigsqcup_m E_m\otimes E_m$ with an arbitrary non-smooth choice of identifications fails local triviality. Calibration: rank of $\Lambda^pE$; $f^*E$ for constant $f$ is trivial; $\operatorname{End}E$ has the identity section.
- `Def - Bundle-Valued Differential Forms` — type: definition (compound); source items: A §2.1 conventions ($\Omega^p(E)=\Gamma(\Lambda^pT^*M\otimes E)$, $\Omega^1(\operatorname{End}E)$), A-E2.1.2, A-D2.1.10's "wedge combined with the contraction $\operatorname{End}E\otimes E\to E$"; prereqs: [Def - Operations on Vector Bundles and Pull-Back Bundles, Def - Differential k-Form on a Manifold, Def - The Wedge Product on a Manifold]; spec: $\Omega^p(M;E)$; the wedge $\Omega^q(M)\times\Omega^p(M;E)\to\Omega^{p+q}(M;E)$; the pairing $\Omega^q(M;\operatorname{End}E)\times\Omega^p(M;E)\to\Omega^{p+q}(M;E)$ and the wedge-composition $\Omega^q(M;\operatorname{End}E)\times\Omega^p(M;\operatorname{End}E)\to\Omega^{p+q}(M;\operatorname{End}E)$ (in a frame: matrix multiplication with wedge of entries); the local description $\omega=\sum_j\omega_j\otimes e_j$ with $\omega_j\in\Omega^p(U)$ and its transformation under $e'=eg$ ($\omega'=g^{-1}\omega$ for the column of coefficients — proved); the evaluation $\omega(X_1,\dots,X_p)\in\Gamma(E)$; graded (anti)commutation rules for $E=\underline{\mathbb R}$ recovered. E2.1.2 ($df$, $df_1\wedge\dots\wedge df_p$) as verified example; non-example: $\operatorname{tr}$ of an $\operatorname{End}E$-valued form is a scalar form but an $E$-valued form has no trace. Convention callout: A writes $\Omega^p(E)$, the series $\Omega^p(M;E)$. Calibration: $\Omega^0(M;\operatorname{End}E)=\Gamma(\operatorname{End}E)$; $\Omega^n(M;E)$ for $n=\dim M$; the pairing is not commutative.
- `Thm - Pull-Back Commutes with the Exterior Derivative` — type: theorem; source items: none of the sources (re-proof of a flagged prerequisite used by A-D2.2.16, A-T2.2.9, B-T2.4.6, B-T2.5.4 and everywhere local forms are pulled back by sections); prereqs: [Def - Pullback of a Differential Form on a Manifold, Def - Exterior Derivative on a Manifold, Thm - Coordinate Expression for the Exterior Derivative, Thm - Pullback Commutes with d for 1-Forms]; statement: For a smooth map $F\colon M\to N$ and $\omega\in\Omega^k(N)$, $F^*(d\omega)=d(F^*\omega)$; moreover $F^*(\omega\wedge\eta)=F^*\omega\wedge F^*\eta$; the same holds for $V$-valued forms for a fixed vector space $V$; proof: DG VIII's page is flagged; write the full proof: locality of $d$ (bump functions), the coordinate formula $d(f\,dx^I)=df\wedge dx^I$, $F^*$ commutes with wedge and with $d$ on functions (chain rule), then induction on the number of factors; reference: Lee ISM 2e Prop 14.23 and Prop 14.26; spec: Targets: local connection and curvature forms $s^*\omega$, $s^*\Omega$ (IV); Chern–Weil naturality (VI).
- `Ex - Hom(E,F) is Isomorphic to E-Dual Tensor F` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-X2.1.1; prereqs: [Def - Operations on Vector Bundles and Pull-Back Bundles]; spec: fibrewise isomorphism $\alpha\otimes v\mapsto(u\mapsto\alpha(u)v)$ is smooth and a bundle isomorphism; route: check in local frames that it is the identity on coefficient matrices, so it respects the transition functions.
- `Ex - Local Frames Correspond to Local Trivialisations` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-X2.1.3, A-R2.1.3; prereqs: [Def - Local Frame, Def - Local Trivialization, Def - Vector Bundle]; spec: given $e$ define $\psi_U^{-1}(m,x)=e(m)\cdot x$ and prove it is a diffeomorphism fibrewise linear; conversely $e_j=\psi_U^{-1}(\cdot,\epsilon_j)$; the two constructions are inverse; derive $\sigma'=g\sigma$ under $e=e'g$ (R2.1.3). Link DG VI `Thm - Local Frames Span Sections`.
- `Ex - Vector Fields along a Curve are Sections of the Pull-Back of the Tangent Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E2.1.6; prereqs: [Def - Operations on Vector Bundles and Pull-Back Bundles]; spec: identify $\Gamma(\lambda^*TB)$ with smooth $V\colon(-\epsilon,\epsilon)\to TB$, $V(t)\in T_{\lambda(t)}B$; show $\dot\lambda$ is a section; note Jacobi fields as a forward example (link RG II `Def - Jacobi Field`).
- `Ex - Local Triviality of Dual, Tensor, and Exterior Power Bundles` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-R2.1.2; prereqs: [Def - Operations on Vector Bundles and Pull-Back Bundles, Thm - Vector Bundle Construction Lemma]; spec: verify the cocycle condition for each of the derived transition functions and that $\operatorname{Hom}(E,F)$'s transition $\phi\mapsto\tau_F\phi\tau_E^{-1}$ is smooth into $GL$; conclude via the construction lemma; also show the total space topology is the one making $E^*\to M$ a submersion.
- `Exercise Index - §2.1 Vector Bundles, Bundle-Valued Forms, and Pull-Backs` — lists: [Ex - Hom(E,F) is Isomorphic to E-Dual Tensor F, Ex - Local Frames Correspond to Local Trivialisations, Ex - Vector Fields along a Curve are Sections of the Pull-Back of the Tangent Bundle, Ex - Local Triviality of Dual, Tensor, and Exterior Power Bundles]

### §2.2 Covariant Derivatives and Connection Matrices
- `Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle` **[backlink]** — type: definition; source items: A-D2.1.7, A-R2.1.4, A-D2.1.11, A-E2.1.3, A §2.1 conventions ("connection" = "covariant derivative", $\mathcal A(E)$); prereqs: [Def - Vector Bundle, Def - Section of a Vector Bundle, Def - Bundle-Valued Differential Forms]; spec: A covariant derivative (the page states both names, "connection" and "covariant derivative", as required by backlink-map.md) is an $\mathbb R$-linear $\nabla\colon\Gamma(E)\to\Omega^1(M;E)$ with $\nabla(fs)=df\otimes s+f\nabla s$; directional derivative $\nabla_vs:=\iota_v\nabla s$, partial derivatives $\nabla_is$, $\nabla s=\sum dx_i\otimes\nabla_is$ (D2.1.11); equivalence with RG I's $(X,s)\mapsto\nabla_Xs$ formulation proved on the page (both directions, including $C^\infty$-linearity in $X$), with a bridge to `Def - Affine Connection on a Vector Bundle`. Axiom Motivation from R2.1.4 (the difference quotient of a section is undefined; the Leibniz rule is the whole axiom; per-clause failure: drop linearity — $s\mapsto\nabla s+\|s\|^2\theta$; drop the $df\otimes s$ term — a tensor, not a derivative). Verified examples: $d$ on the product bundle (coefficientwise derivative); the projected connection $\nabla s=\operatorname{pr}(ds)$ on $TM\subset\underline{\mathbb R^N}$ (E2.1.3, Leibniz rule computed on the page); the difference of two connections is $C^\infty$-linear (proved; it feeds the affine-space theorem); non-example: $s\mapsto ds$ on a non-trivial bundle is not defined (frame dependence exhibited on the Möbius bundle). Categorical: a connection is a splitting of the Atiyah sequence — stated only as an outlook in bold. True name: "a Leibniz-rule derivative in the base direction". Calibration: the connection matrix of $d$ is $0$; $\nabla_v$ of a constant section of the trivial bundle vanishes; $\nabla(fs)$ for $f$ locally constant.
- `Def - Connection Matrix and Local Form of a Connection` **[anchor]** — type: definition; source items: A-D2.1.8, A-R2.1.5 (statement of (14), proof by link), A-R2.1.3; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle, Def - Local Frame, Def - Connection 1-Forms (Cartan)]; spec: $\nabla e=e\cdot A$ with $A\in\Omega^1(U;\mathfrak{gl}_k(\mathbb R))$; $\nabla(e\sigma)=e(d\sigma+A\sigma)$, written $\nabla=d+A$; existence and uniqueness of $A$ given $\nabla$ and $e$ proved; the converse that every $A$ is a connection matrix (define $\nabla$ locally by the formula and check Leibniz) proved on the page; the transformation law $A'=g^{-1}Ag+g^{-1}dg$ for $e'=eg$ (14) is stated and its proof linked to RG I `Thm - Gauge Transformation Law for Connection 1-Forms` (identical convention; restate). Convention callout: Haydys's row-vector convention $e\cdot A$ versus RG I's $\nabla e_b=e_a\otimes\omega^a{}_b$ (same matrix). Examples: coordinate frame of $TM$ gives $A^k{}_j=\Gamma^k_{ij}dx^i$ (link `Def - Christoffel Symbols`); the product connection in a rotated frame $e'=eg$ has $A'=g^{-1}dg$ (verified). Calibration: compute $A$ for $\nabla=d+\alpha\otimes\operatorname{id}$ on a trivial bundle; check (14) reduces to $A'=A$ when $g$ is constant and $A$ scalar.
- `Thm - Tensoriality Lemma for C-Infinity-Linear Maps` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.1.2, A-R2.3.1 (Target); prereqs: [Def - Bundle-Valued Differential Forms, Thm - Existence of Smooth Bump Functions, Def - Section of a Vector Bundle]; statement: Let $A\colon\Gamma(E)\to\Omega^p(M;F)$ be $\mathbb R$-linear and $C^\infty(M)$-linear, $A(fs)=fA(s)$. Then there is a unique $a\in\Omega^p(M;\operatorname{Hom}(E,F))$ with $A(s)=a\cdot s$ for all $s$; conversely every such $a$ defines a $C^\infty$-linear map. The same holds for multilinear maps in several section arguments (the version used for torsion and curvature); proof: source left as exercise; write: Step 1 (locality: if $s$ vanishes on an open $U$ then $A(s)$ vanishes on $U$, via bump functions); Step 2 (pointwise dependence: $s(m)=0\Rightarrow A(s)(m)=0$, by writing $s=\sum\sigma_je_j$ near $m$ with $\sigma_j(m)=0$ and using a bump function to globalise); Step 3 (define $a_m(v):=A(s)(m)$ for any $s$ with $s(m)=v$ and verify smoothness in a frame); reference: Lee ISM 2e Lemma 12.24 (link DG VII `Thm - Tensor Field is C-Infinity Multilinear over C-Infinity Functions` as the scalar-valued special case); spec: Sources: any operator whose Leibniz terms cancel (difference of connections, torsion, $d^\nabla\circ d^\nabla$); Targets: existence of the curvature form, tensoriality of torsion (R2.3.1), well-definedness of $\operatorname{Hom}$-valued forms.
- `Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.1.1, A-I2.1.2; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle, Def - Connection Matrix and Local Form of a Connection, Thm - Tensoriality Lemma for C-Infinity-Linear Maps, Thm - Existence of Smooth Partitions of Unity]; statement: For every vector bundle $E\to M$: (a) $\mathcal A(E)\ne\varnothing$; (b) for $\nabla,\hat\nabla\in\mathcal A(E)$ the difference $\nabla-\hat\nabla$ is $C^\infty$-linear, hence an element $a\in\Omega^1(M;\operatorname{End}E)$; (c) for $\nabla\in\mathcal A(E)$ and $a\in\Omega^1(M;\operatorname{End}E)$, $\nabla+a$ is a connection; hence $\mathcal A(E)$ is an affine space modelled on $\Omega^1(M;\operatorname{End}E)$; proof: source full modulo Lemma 12 and the partition-of-unity gluing "as for Riemannian metrics" — write all: convexity of $\mathcal A(E)$ (Leibniz rule is affine in $\nabla$); local connections $\nabla_U=\psi_U^{-1}\circ d\circ\psi_U$; gluing $\nabla:=\sum_\alpha\rho_\alpha\nabla_{U_\alpha}$ with the meaning $\nabla s:=\sum_\alpha\rho_\alpha\nabla_{U_\alpha}(s|_{U_\alpha})$ extended by zero (justify the extension by zero using $\operatorname{supp}\rho_\alpha\subset U_\alpha$), check Leibniz using $\sum\rho_\alpha=1$; (b) by the tensoriality lemma; (c) written out; reference: Lee ISM 2e Prop 12.? (existence of connections), Bär–? [BT03, Thm 3.3.7] cited by A; spec: Mechanism summary: "the Leibniz rule is an inhomogeneous linear condition, so its solution set is an affine space over the homogeneous solutions (tensors); local solutions exist, and convex combinations of solutions are solutions, so a partition of unity glues." Targets: gauge orbits as subsets of an affine space (§2.4); the principal-bundle version (IV) as a corollary pattern.
- `Ex - The Projected Connection on an Embedded Submanifold` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-E2.1.3; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle]; spec: verify the Leibniz rule for $\nabla s=\operatorname{pr}(ds)$, show $\nabla_XY$ is tangent, and identify it with the Levi-Civita connection of the induced metric (forward link to §2.5; the identification is proved in the §2.5 exercise on $S^2$).
- `Ex - Connection Matrix of the Product Connection in a Rotated Frame` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-R2.1.5 (14) drill; prereqs: [Def - Connection Matrix and Local Form of a Connection]; spec: for $\nabla=d$ on $U\times\mathbb R^k$ and $e'=eg$, compute $A'=g^{-1}dg$ directly and via (14); for $k=1$, $g=e^{f}$, get $A'=df$.
- `Ex - Every Matrix of 1-Forms is a Connection Matrix` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-D2.1.8 (last claim), A-T2.1.1(b)-(c) drill; prereqs: [Def - Connection Matrix and Local Form of a Connection, Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space]; spec: given $A\in\Omega^1(U;\mathfrak{gl}_k)$ prove $d+A$ is a connection on $E|_U$; then on $TS^2$ compute the connection matrix of the Levi-Civita connection in the coordinate frame $(\partial_\theta,\partial_\varphi)$ (link RG I `Ex - Christoffel Symbols of the Round Metric on the Sphere`) and exhibit the difference tensor between it and the flat connection $d$ of the chart.
- `Ex - Convex Combinations of Connections are Connections` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-T2.1.1 step (1); prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle]; spec: prove $t\nabla+(1-t)\hat\nabla$ satisfies the Leibniz rule, and more generally $\sum\rho_\alpha\nabla_\alpha$ for a partition of unity.
- `Exercise Index - §2.2 Covariant Derivatives and Connection Matrices` — lists: [Ex - The Projected Connection on an Embedded Submanifold, Ex - Connection Matrix of the Product Connection in a Rotated Frame, Ex - Every Matrix of 1-Forms is a Connection Matrix, Ex - Convex Combinations of Connections are Connections]

### §2.3 The Exterior Covariant Derivative and Curvature
- `Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Vector Bundle` **[anchor]** — type: definition; source items: A-D2.1.9; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle, Def - Bundle-Valued Differential Forms, Def - Exterior Derivative on a Manifold]; spec: $d^\nabla(\omega\otimes s):=d\omega\otimes s+(-1)^p\omega\wedge\nabla s$ extended linearly; well-definedness on $\Omega^p(M;E)=\Omega^p(M)\otimes_{C^\infty}\Gamma(E)$ proved (check compatibility with $f\omega\otimes s=\omega\otimes fs$); the graded Leibniz rule $d^\nabla(\alpha\wedge\omega)=d\alpha\wedge\omega+(-1)^q\alpha\wedge d^\nabla\omega$ proved as a corollary; local formula $d^\nabla(e\sigma)=e(d\sigma+A\wedge\sigma)$ for a column $\sigma$ of $p$-forms proved; the sequence $\Omega^0(M;E)\to\Omega^1(M;E)\to\cdots$ need not be a complex (verified example: $d+A$ on the trivial bundle over $\mathbb R^2$ with $A=x\,dy\otimes\operatorname{id}$ has $d^\nabla d^\nabla s=dx\wedge dy\otimes s\ne0$); example: for $E=\underline{\mathbb R}$ with $\nabla=d$ it is the de Rham differential. Calibration: $d^\nabla=\nabla$ on $\Omega^0$; $d^\nabla(f\omega)=df\wedge\omega+fd^\nabla\omega$.
- `Def - Curvature of a Vector-Bundle Connection` **[anchor]** — type: definition; source items: A-D2.1.10, A-R2.1.6, conventions ($F_\nabla(X,Y)s=\nabla_X\nabla_Ys-\nabla_Y\nabla_Xs-\nabla_{[X,Y]}s$); prereqs: [Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Vector Bundle, Thm - Existence of the Curvature Form]; spec: $F_\nabla\in\Omega^2(M;\operatorname{End}E)$ with $d^\nabla\circ d^\nabla=F_\nabla\wedge\cdot$; the three equivalent descriptions proved on the page as corollaries (using the existence theorem): (i) $d^\nabla d^\nabla s=F_\nabla s$; (ii) $F_\nabla(\partial_i,\partial_j)=\nabla_i\nabla_j-\nabla_j\nabla_i$ (R2.1.6 written out); (iii) the commutator formula with $[X,Y]$ for arbitrary vector fields (from (ii) by tensoriality). Convention callout: RG I's curvature 2-forms $\Omega^a{}_b$ are the matrix entries of $F_\nabla$; RG III's Riemann tensor $R(X,Y)Z$ equals $F_{\nabla^{LC}}(X,Y)Z$ (link both; proved in §2.5). Verified examples: $F=0$ for the product connection; $F=dx\wedge dy\otimes\operatorname{id}$ for the example above; non-example: $\nabla_X\nabla_Y-\nabla_Y\nabla_X$ alone is not tensorial. True name: "the obstruction to $d^\nabla$ being a differential" / "the commutator of covariant derivatives". Calibration: $F_\nabla$ is antisymmetric; $F_{\nabla}(X,Y)$ is $C^\infty$-linear in $s$; on a line bundle $F$ is a scalar 2-form.
- `Thm - Existence of the Curvature Form` — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.1.3; prereqs: [Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Vector Bundle, Thm - Tensoriality Lemma for C-Infinity-Linear Maps]; statement: For every connection $\nabla$ on $E$ there is a unique $F_\nabla\in\Omega^2(M;\operatorname{End}E)$ such that $d^\nabla(d^\nabla\omega)=F_\nabla\wedge\omega$ for all $\omega\in\Omega^p(M;E)$, all $p$; proof: source full except the two Leibniz applications for $p=0$ — write them: $d^\nabla(\nabla(fs))=d^\nabla(df\otimes s+f\nabla s)=-df\wedge\nabla s+df\wedge\nabla s+fd^\nabla\nabla s$; then the tensoriality lemma; then the $p>0$ computation with the sign bookkeeping displayed line by line; reference: Taubes, *Differential Geometry* Ch. 11 §11.? / Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. III; spec: Mechanism: "the second derivative of the Leibniz rule kills the derivative of $f$ twice with opposite signs."
- `Thm - Local Formula for the Curvature of a Connection` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-R2.1.7, A-R2.1.8, A §2.1 conventions ((18),(20)); prereqs: [Def - Curvature of a Vector-Bundle Connection, Def - Connection Matrix and Local Form of a Connection, Thm - Gauge Transformation Law for Connection 1-Forms]; statement: In a local frame $e$ with connection matrix $A$, the curvature matrix $F$ (defined by $F_\nabla e=e\cdot F$) is $F=dA+A\wedge A=dA+\tfrac12[A\wedge A]$, where $[A\wedge A]$ combines wedge and the commutator of $\mathfrak{gl}_k(\mathbb R)$; under $e'=eg$, $F'=g^{-1}Fg$; proof: source R2.1.7 full (the computation with $\nabla e\wedge(d\sigma+A\sigma)$), the frame-change "left to reader" — link RG I `Thm - Gauge Transformation Law for Connection 1-Forms` (restate; also give the two-line direct derivation from (14)); the identity $A\wedge A=\tfrac12[A\wedge A]$ proved from the bracket convention (forward-link the general statement in IV `Thm - Graded Antisymmetry and Jacobi Identity for Lie-Algebra-Valued Forms`; here prove the matrix case directly); spec: Convention callout: A's (18) has no $\tfrac12$ (matrix form) while (20) has it (bracket form); B's (2.7) and RG I's Cartan equation are the same identity; Bridges to RG I `Thm - Cartan's Second Structural Equation`. Targets: curvature is a first-order nonlinear operator in $A$; the Chern–Weil forms are gauge invariant because $F$ transforms by conjugation.
- `Def - Induced Connections on Dual, Hom, and Endomorphism Bundles` — type: definition (compound); source items: A-X2.2.7 (as the defining characterisation; the exercise page below proves the claims), A-T2.2.6 (statement; the associated-bundle proof is IV); prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle, Def - Operations on Vector Bundles and Pull-Back Bundles]; spec: the unique connections on $E^*$, $\operatorname{Hom}(E,F)$, $\operatorname{End}E$, $E\otimes F$, $E\oplus F$, $\Lambda^pE$ characterised by Leibniz rules against the natural pairings; existence and uniqueness proved on the page by writing the local formulas ($A^*=-A^t$; $\nabla\phi=d\phi+[A,\phi]$ for $\operatorname{End}E$; $A_E\otimes1+1\otimes A_F$) and checking frame independence; corollary: $d^\nabla$ on $\Omega^p(M;\operatorname{End}E)$ is $d a+A\wedge a-(-1)^pa\wedge A$ locally; links RG I `Def - Induced Connection on Tensor Bundles` (the $TM$ case). The identification with the associated-bundle induced connections (A-T2.2.6) is a bold forward reference to IV `Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles`. Calibration: the identity section of $\operatorname{End}E$ is parallel; $\nabla$ on $E^*\otimes E$ agrees with $\nabla$ on $\operatorname{End}E$.
- `Thm - Curvature of a Shifted Connection` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.1.4; prereqs: [Thm - Local Formula for the Curvature of a Connection, Def - Induced Connections on Dual, Hom, and Endomorphism Bundles, Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space]; statement: For $\nabla\in\mathcal A(E)$ and $a\in\Omega^1(M;\operatorname{End}E)$, $F_{\nabla+a}=F_\nabla+d^\nabla a+a\wedge a$, where $d^\nabla$ is the exterior covariant derivative of the induced connection on $\operatorname{End}E$; proof: source omitted ("immediately from (18)"); write: locally $A\mapsto A+a$, expand $d(A+a)+(A+a)\wedge(A+a)$, identify $da+A\wedge a+a\wedge A=d^\nabla a$ by the local formula of the induced $\operatorname{End}E$-connection, check that both sides are globally defined so the local identity globalises; spec: Targets: the curvature map is a quadratic polynomial on the affine space $\mathcal A(E)$; its differential at $\nabla$ is $d^\nabla$ (used for deformation complexes in IX/XI); the Chern–Weil homotopy argument (VI).
- `Ex - Curvature as the Failure of Partial Covariant Derivatives to Commute` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-R2.1.6; prereqs: [Def - Curvature of a Vector-Bundle Connection]; spec: derive $F(\partial_i,\partial_j)=[\nabla_i,\nabla_j]$ from $d^\nabla\nabla s$ in coordinates and then the general $[X,Y]$ formula.
- `Ex - Curvature of d plus A on the Trivial Bundle over the Plane` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-R2.1.7 drill; prereqs: [Thm - Local Formula for the Curvature of a Connection]; spec: for $A=x\,dy\,X_0$ with $X_0\in\mathfrak{gl}_2$ constant compute $F=dx\wedge dy\,X_0$ (the $A\wedge A$ term vanishes); for $A=x\,dy\,X_0+y\,dx\,Y_0$ compute the bracket term $[X_0,Y_0]$ contribution.
- `Ex - The Curvature of the Projected Connection on the Sphere` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-E2.1.3 (continued); prereqs: [Def - Curvature of a Vector-Bundle Connection, Ex - The Projected Connection on an Embedded Submanifold]; spec: for $TS^2\subset\underline{\mathbb R^3}$ prove $F(X,Y)Z=\langle Y,Z\rangle X-\langle X,Z\rangle Y$ by direct differentiation of $\operatorname{pr}=1-nn^t$ (no Gauss equation needed); conclude $K=1$ in the language of RG III (link `Def - Sectional Curvature` as bridge).
- `Ex - The Exterior Covariant Derivative Sequence is a Complex iff the Connection is Flat` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-D2.1.9 (last sentence); prereqs: [Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Vector Bundle, Def - Curvature of a Vector-Bundle Connection]; spec: $d^\nabla\circ d^\nabla=0$ on all degrees iff $F_\nabla=0$ (both directions: if $F\ne0$ at a point pick $s$ with $F s\ne0$).
- `Ex - Induced Connections on the Dual and Endomorphism Bundles` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X2.2.7 (both parts); prereqs: [Def - Induced Connections on Dual, Hom, and Endomorphism Bundles]; spec: prove uniqueness and existence from the Leibniz characterisations, compute the local matrices $-A^t$ and $\operatorname{ad}A$, and verify the identification with the associated-bundle construction is deferred (IV) — the exercise proves only the direct claims.
- `Exercise Index - §2.3 The Exterior Covariant Derivative and Curvature` — lists: [Ex - Curvature as the Failure of Partial Covariant Derivatives to Commute, Ex - Curvature of d plus A on the Trivial Bundle over the Plane, Ex - The Curvature of the Projected Connection on the Sphere, Ex - The Exterior Covariant Derivative Sequence is a Complex iff the Connection is Flat, Ex - Induced Connections on the Dual and Endomorphism Bundles]

### §2.4 The Gauge Group of a Vector Bundle
- `Def - Gauge Group of a Vector Bundle` **[backlink]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.1.12, A-D2.1.13, A-D2.1.14; prereqs: [Def - Operations on Vector Bundles and Pull-Back Bundles, Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle]; spec: $\mathcal G(E)=\{g\in\Gamma(\operatorname{End}E):g(m)\in GL(E_m)\}$ with the $C^\infty$ topology (defined: uniform convergence of all derivatives on compacta in local frames; a topological group — proved on the page: composition and inversion are continuous in this topology, using Leibniz for derivatives of products and the inverse-series argument), respecting extra structure (orientation: $\det g>0$; Euclidean: $g$ orthogonal; Hermitian: unitary — each a subgroup, verified); the right action $\nabla^gs:=g^{-1}\nabla(gs)$ (its properties are `Thm - Gauge Action on Connection Matrices`); gauge equivalence. Verified examples: for the trivial line bundle, $\mathcal G=C^\infty(M,\mathbb R^*)$ (or $C^\infty(M,U(1))$ with Hermitian structure); non-example: a bundle map covering a non-identity diffeomorphism of $M$ is not a gauge transformation (that is V's $\operatorname{Aut}(P)$). Convention callout: A's gauge group of $E$ is a subgroup of $\Gamma(\operatorname{End}E)$; B's gauge group (V) is $\operatorname{Aut}$ of the principal bundle; the two are identified in V `Thm - The Gauge Group is the Space of Sections of the Adjoint Group Bundle`. Calibration: $\mathcal G$ is not a vector space; the constant $-1$ acts trivially on connections; $\nabla^g=\nabla$ iff $g$ is parallel.
- `Thm - Gauge Action on Connection Matrices` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-R2.1.9, A-R2.1.10, A-D2.1.13 (claims); prereqs: [Def - Gauge Group of a Vector Bundle, Def - Connection Matrix and Local Form of a Connection, Thm - Local Formula for the Curvature of a Connection, Thm - Gauge Transformation Law for Connection 1-Forms]; statement: For $\nabla\in\mathcal A(E)$ and $g\in\mathcal G(E)$: (a) $\nabla^g$ is a connection; (b) $(\nabla^g)^h=\nabla^{gh}$ and $\nabla^{1}=\nabla$, so $(\nabla,g)\mapsto\nabla^g$ is a right action; (c) in a local frame $e$, with $g$ represented by $g(e)=e\cdot g$, $A(\nabla^g,e)=g^{-1}Ag+g^{-1}dg=A(\nabla,e\cdot g)$; (d) $F_{\nabla^g}=g^{-1}F_\nabla g$, and locally the curvature matrix transforms by conjugation; (e) $\nabla^g-\nabla=g^{-1}(\nabla g)\in\Omega^1(M;\operatorname{End}E)$; proof: source: (c) full, (a),(b) stated without proof — write all five: (a) Leibniz check; (b) associativity of composition; (c) A's computation; (d) from (c) and the local curvature formula, or directly from $d^{\nabla^g}=g^{-1}d^\nabla g$ (prove this operator identity on $\Omega^p(M;E)$ first — Lemma); (e) by the Leibniz rule on $\operatorname{End}E$; reference: Donaldson–Kronheimer §2.1.2; spec: Mechanism summary: "formula (14) is simultaneously a change of frame and a change of connection — a gauge transformation is a moving change of frame." Targets: the curvature of gauge-equivalent connections have the same characteristic forms (VI); the infinitesimal action $\xi\mapsto-\nabla\xi$ (V).
- `Ex - The Gauge Group of a Trivial Line Bundle and its Action on Connections` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-D2.1.13 drill; prereqs: [Thm - Gauge Action on Connection Matrices]; spec: on $M\times\mathbb C$ with Hermitian structure, $g=e^{if}$, $A\in i\Omega^1(M)$: $A^g=A+i\,df$, $F^g=F$; deduce that $\mathcal A/\mathcal G$ for a $U(1)$-bundle is an affine space over $i\Omega^1(M)/i\,dC^\infty(M)$ when $H^1$ is ignored — state precisely: the orbit of $A$ is $A+i\,d(C^\infty(M))$ plus the discrete part from non-null-homotopic $g$ (bold forward reference to V holonomy).
- `Ex - Gauge-Equivalent Connections have Conjugate Curvature` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-R2.1.8, A-D2.1.14 drill; prereqs: [Thm - Gauge Action on Connection Matrices]; spec: prove (d) from (c) and (18) by direct expansion, tracking $d(g^{-1})=-g^{-1}dg\,g^{-1}$.
- `Ex - Gauge Equivalence of Constant Connections on the Trivial Line Bundle over the Circle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: none (standard; foreshadows holonomy); prereqs: [Thm - Gauge Action on Connection Matrices, Thm - Complex Representations of U(1) and SU(2)]; spec: on $S^1\times\mathbb C$ with unitary gauge group $C^\infty(S^1,U(1))$, show $d+ia\,d\theta$ and $d+ia'\,d\theta$ ($a,a'\in\mathbb R$ constants) are gauge equivalent iff $a-a'\in\mathbb Z$; route: $g=e^{if}$ must satisfy $df=(a'-a)d\theta$, so $f(\theta)=(a'-a)\theta+c$, single-valued iff $a'-a\in\mathbb Z$; the invariant is $e^{2\pi ia}$ (the holonomy, V).
- `Ex - The Gauge Group Acts on the Right` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-R2.1.10; prereqs: [Def - Gauge Group of a Vector Bundle]; spec: verify $(\nabla^g)^h=\nabla^{gh}$ and that the naive left action $g\nabla g^{-1}$ is the right action by $g^{-1}$.
- `Exercise Index - §2.4 The Gauge Group of a Vector Bundle` — lists: [Ex - The Gauge Group of a Trivial Line Bundle and its Action on Connections, Ex - Gauge-Equivalent Connections have Conjugate Curvature, Ex - Gauge Equivalence of Constant Connections on the Trivial Line Bundle over the Circle, Ex - The Gauge Group Acts on the Right]

### §2.5 The Levi-Civita Connection as a Connection on the Tangent Bundle
- `Def - Euclidean Vector Bundle and Metric Connection` — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.2.4 (Euclidean structure), A §2.3 convention ($d\,g(v,w)=g(\nabla v,w)+g(v,\nabla w)$), A-D2.2.7 (Hermitian, statement only — the complex case is III); prereqs: [Def - Vector Bundle, Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle, Def - Riemannian Metric]; spec: a Euclidean structure is a smooth family of inner products (smoothness: $\langle s_1,s_2\rangle\in C^\infty$ for smooth sections; equivalently a smooth section of $\operatorname{Sym}^2E^*$ — equivalence proved); existence via partition of unity (corollary proved, same argument as DG XII's metric existence, linked); metric (compatible) connection; corollary proved: in an orthonormal frame the connection matrix is skew-symmetric, and conversely a skew connection matrix in an orthonormal frame gives a metric connection; corollary: the difference of two metric connections lies in $\Omega^1(M;\mathfrak{o}(E))$; existence of metric connections (project any connection: $\nabla^{met}:=\nabla-\tfrac12(\nabla g)^\sharp$ — written); the Levi-Civita connection is the example (§2.5 next page); non-example: $d+x\,dy\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\0&0\end{pmatrix}$ on $\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb R^2$ is not metric. Links RG I `Def - Metric-Compatible Connection` (the $TM$ case, bridge). Calibration: orthonormal frames exist locally (Gram–Schmidt, link LA); parallel transport preserves lengths (bold forward reference to V).
- `Def - The Levi-Civita Connection Viewed as a Connection on TM` — type: definition (compound; mostly links); source items: A-D2.3.1, A-D2.3.2 (→ link `Def - Torsion Tensor`), A-D2.3.3 (→ link `Def - Levi-Civita Connection`), A-T2.3.1, A-I2.3.1 (→ link `Thm - Fundamental Theorem of Riemannian Geometry (Full Proof)`), A-D2.3.4, A-R2.3.1, A-R2.3.2, A-R2.3.3, A §2.3 conventions; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle, Def - Euclidean Vector Bundle and Metric Connection, Thm - Tensoriality Lemma for C-Infinity-Linear Maps, Def - Torsion Tensor, Def - Levi-Civita Connection, Thm - Fundamental Theorem of Riemannian Geometry (Full Proof), Def - Riemann Curvature Tensor, Def - Ricci Tensor, Def - Scalar Curvature]; spec: This page places RG I/RG III's objects inside the vector-bundle language of the series: torsion $T(v,w)=\nabla_vw-\nabla_wv-[v,w]$ is tensorial (R2.3.1 proved on the page via the tensoriality lemma, all cases of $f_1,f_2$ written) hence $T\in\Omega^2(M;TM)$; torsion-free (65); the Levi-Civita connection is the unique metric torsion-free connection (linked full proof, restated); its curvature $F_{\nabla^{LC}}\in\Omega^2(M;\operatorname{End}TM)$ is the Riemann tensor $R(X,Y)Z$ of RG III (identification proved on the page from the commutator formula of §2.3); Ricci and scalar curvature with Haydys's convention $\operatorname{Ric}_g(v,w)=-\sum_ig(R(e_i,v)e_i,w)$ shown on the page to equal RG III's $\operatorname{Ric}(v,w)=\operatorname{tr}(Z\mapsto R(Z,v)w)$ (Convention callout with the antisymmetry $R(X,Y)=-R(Y,X)$ doing the work), $s_g=\operatorname{tr}_g\operatorname{Ric}$; R2.3.2 (why $TM$ is special) in Axiom Motivation; R2.3.3 (role of $s_g$ in the Weitzenböck formula) as a bold forward reference to VIII. Verified examples: flat $\mathbb R^n$; the round $S^2$ via the §2.3 exercise; non-example: $d$ in a chart is torsion-free but not metric for a non-flat metric.
- `Ex - Torsion-Freeness is the Alternation of the Covariant Derivative being the Exterior Derivative` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X2.3.1; prereqs: [Def - The Levi-Civita Connection Viewed as a Connection on TM, Def - Induced Connections on Dual, Hom, and Endomorphism Bundles]; spec: with the induced connection on $T^*M$, prove $\operatorname{Alt}\circ\nabla=d$ on $\Omega^1(M)$ iff $T=0$; route: $(\nabla\alpha)(X,Y)-(\nabla\alpha)(Y,X)=d\alpha(X,Y)+\alpha(T(X,Y))$.
- `Ex - The Connection Matrix of a Metric Connection in an Orthonormal Frame is Skew-Symmetric` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A §2.3 convention drill; prereqs: [Def - Euclidean Vector Bundle and Metric Connection]; spec: prove both directions; link RG I `Def - Connection 1-Forms (Cartan)` (orthonormal-frame paragraph).
- `Ex - Haydys's Ricci Convention Agrees with the Standard Ricci Tensor` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-D2.3.4; prereqs: [Def - The Levi-Civita Connection Viewed as a Connection on TM]; spec: sign bookkeeping in an orthonormal frame; compute both for the round $S^2$ ($\operatorname{Ric}=g$, $s=2$).
- `Ex - The Levi-Civita Connection of the Round Sphere as a Connection Matrix` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A §2.3 (worked instance), A-R2.1.7 drill; prereqs: [Def - The Levi-Civita Connection Viewed as a Connection on TM, Thm - Local Formula for the Curvature of a Connection]; spec: in the coordinate frame $(\partial_\theta,\partial_\varphi)$ write $A$ from the Christoffel symbols (link RG I exercise), compute $F=dA+A\wedge A$, and verify $F(\partial_\theta,\partial_\varphi)\partial_\varphi=\sin^2\theta\,\partial_\theta$, i.e. $K=1$; compare with the orthonormal-frame computation (link RG I `Ex - Cartan Structural Equations on S^2`).
- `Exercise Index - §2.5 The Levi-Civita Connection as a Connection on the Tangent Bundle` — lists: [Ex - Torsion-Freeness is the Alternation of the Covariant Derivative being the Exterior Derivative, Ex - The Connection Matrix of a Metric Connection in an Orthonormal Frame is Skew-Symmetric, Ex - Haydys's Ricci Convention Agrees with the Standard Ricci Tensor, Ex - The Levi-Civita Connection of the Round Sphere as a Connection Matrix]

### §3.1 Fibre Bundles
- `Def - Fibre Bundle` **[backlink]** — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D2.1.1, B-D2.1.2, B-D2.1.3, B-D2.1.7, B-D2.1.8 (statement: a vector bundle is a fibre bundle with fibre $\mathbb K^n$ and linear trivialisations — proved as corollary, both directions, linking DG VI `Def - Vector Bundle`), B-D2.1.9, B-D2.1.10, B-D2.1.11 (fibre-bundle pull-back, definition only), B-E2.1.1, B-E2.1.4, B-E2.1.5, B-R2.1.1, B §2.1 conventions; prereqs: [Def - Smooth Manifold, Def - Vector Bundle, Def - Diffeomorphism]; spec: A fibre bundle $(E,\pi,B)$ with typical fibre $F$: $\pi$ surjective smooth, local trivialisations $\psi_U\colon\pi^{-1}(U)\to U\times F$ diffeomorphisms over $U$; fibres $E_x$ (all diffeomorphic to $F$ — proved), $E|_U$; isomorphism (over the identity of $B$; also record the general notion over a diffeomorphism of bases) and triviality (iff a global trivialisation — proved); sections and the zero section of a vector bundle. Corollary proved: $\pi$ is a submersion and an open map. Verified examples: product bundle; $TB$ and its tensor constructions (E2.1.4, via II's operations page); the Möbius strip (forward to the next page); non-example: the map $\mathbb R\to S^1$, $t\mapsto e^{it}$ restricted to $(0,4\pi)$ is a surjective submersion that is not a fibre bundle (fibre cardinality jumps); the zero-section remark R2.1.1 with the Hopf bundle as the standing example of a fibre bundle without sections (bold forward reference to §3.3). Categorical: a fibre bundle is a locally trivial family; the category of bundles over $B$. Calibration: composition of bundle projections is a fibre bundle? (yes for compact fibres — not claimed; ask the reader to check the product case only); restriction of a bundle to an open set; a bundle over a point.
- `Thm - The Pull-Back of a Fibre Bundle is a Fibre Bundle` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.1.2, B-D2.1.11, A-D2.2.17, A-R2.2.9, B-D2.2.3; prereqs: [Def - Fibre Bundle, Thm - Regular Value Theorem on Manifolds]; statement: For a fibre bundle $(E,\pi,B)$ with fibre $F$ and smooth $\lambda\colon B'\to B$, $E':=\{(b',p):\lambda(b')=\pi(p)\}$ is an embedded submanifold of $B'\times E$, $(E',\operatorname{pr}_1,B')$ is a fibre bundle with fibre $F$, $\operatorname{pr}_2$ restricts to diffeomorphisms $E'_{b'}\to E_{\lambda(b')}$, and the square commutes. If $E=P$ is a principal $G$-bundle (with the right action $(b',p)\cdot g=(b',pg)$) then $\lambda^*P$ is a principal $G$-bundle and $\operatorname{pr}_2$ is $G$-equivariant; if $E$ is a vector bundle, $\lambda^*E$ is a vector bundle agreeing with II's fibrewise definition (isomorphism written); proof: source full modulo the submanifold claim — prove $E'$ is the preimage of the diagonal under $(\lambda\circ\operatorname{pr}_1,\pi\circ\operatorname{pr}_2)$, which is transverse to the diagonal because $\pi$ is a submersion (regular value theorem after composing with a local chart of $B$); local trivialisations as in B; the principal-bundle clauses (freeness, transitivity on fibres, equivariance of the induced trivialisations) written; reference: Husemoller Ch. 2 Prop 5.? / Kobayashi–Nomizu I Prop I.5.? ; spec: Targets: restriction to submanifolds, bundles along curves (horizontal lifts, V), naturality of characteristic classes (VI).
- `Def - Sphere Bundles and Mapping Tori` — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D2.1.4, B-D2.1.5, B-D2.1.6, B-E2.1.2, B-E2.1.3; prereqs: [Def - Fibre Bundle, Def - Euclidean Vector Bundle and Metric Connection, Def - Discrete Group and Properly Discontinuous Action]; spec: unit sphere bundle $S(E)$ of a Euclidean vector bundle (trivialisations induced from orthonormal frames — verified; fibre $S^{r-1}$); the mapping torus $E_\phi:=\mathbb Z\backslash(\mathbb R\times F)$ of a diffeomorphism $\phi$ with its projection to $S^1=\mathbb Z\backslash\mathbb R$; the Möbius strip as the mapping torus of $x\mapsto-x$ on $(-1,1)$; verified: the $\mathbb Z$-action is properly discontinuous (chapter I theorem) so $E_\phi$ is a manifold; that $E_\phi\to S^1$ is a fibre bundle is the next theorem. Non-example: the mapping torus of a non-injective map is not a bundle. Calibration: $E_{\operatorname{id}}=S^1\times F$; the mapping torus of a rotation of $S^1$ is a torus.
- `Thm - The Mapping Torus of a Diffeomorphism is a Fibre Bundle over the Circle` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.1.1; prereqs: [Def - Sphere Bundles and Mapping Tori, Thm - Quotient by a Properly Discontinuous Action is a Manifold and a Covering Map]; statement: $(E_\phi,\pi,S^1)$ is a fibre bundle with typical fibre $F$; it is trivial iff $\phi$ is isotopic to the identity through diffeomorphisms — only the "if" direction is stated and proved here (the "only if" needs the homotopy invariance of §3.5 and is placed there as a corollary of the clutching construction); proof: source sketch; write: over the arcs $U_1=S^1\setminus\{[0]\}$ and $U_2=S^1\setminus\{[1/2]\}$ the quotient map identifies $\pi^{-1}(U_i)$ with $(a_i,a_i+1)\times F$ (write the diffeomorphisms explicitly); transition on the overlap is $(t,f)\mapsto(t+1,\phi(f))$ on one component and the identity on the other; "if": an isotopy $\phi_t$ gives a global trivialisation by untwisting; spec: Targets: the Möbius strip (nontrivial — via DG VI's page); the Klein bottle.
- `Ex - The Möbius Strip as a Mapping Torus is a Nontrivial Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-E2.1.3; prereqs: [Thm - The Mapping Torus of a Diffeomorphism is a Fibre Bundle over the Circle]; spec: identify the mapping torus of $-\operatorname{id}$ with the Möbius line bundle of DG VI (link `Ex - The Möbius Bundle is Nontrivial`) and deduce nontriviality; route: a global trivialisation would give a nowhere-vanishing section, contradiction by the intermediate value theorem along the core circle.
- `Ex - The Unit Tangent Bundle of S^2 is Diffeomorphic to SO(3)` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: B-D2.1.4 (worked instance); prereqs: [Def - Sphere Bundles and Mapping Tori, Thm - Orbit-Stabilizer for Lie Group Actions]; spec: $SO(3)$ acts transitively on $S(TS^2)=\{(x,v):|x|=|v|=1,x\perp v\}$ with trivial stabiliser; conclude via the orbit–stabilizer theorem (DG XI) that the orbit map is a diffeomorphism; identify the bundle projection with $g\mapsto ge_3$.
- `Ex - Pull-Back of a Fibre Bundle along a Constant Map is Trivial` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-T2.1.2 drill; prereqs: [Thm - The Pull-Back of a Fibre Bundle is a Fibre Bundle]; spec: compute $\lambda^*E=B'\times E_{b_0}$; note this is the first instance of "homotopic maps pull back isomorphic bundles" (§3.5).
- `Ex - The Klein Bottle as a Mapping Torus` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: none (standard companion of B-D2.1.5); prereqs: [Thm - The Mapping Torus of a Diffeomorphism is a Fibre Bundle over the Circle]; spec: the mapping torus of the reflection $z\mapsto\bar z$ of $S^1$ is a circle bundle over $S^1$ that is non-orientable, hence nontrivial (use DG IX orientation: the total space of a trivial bundle $S^1\times S^1$ is orientable, while the Klein bottle is not — prove non-orientability via a nowhere-vanishing 2-form argument on the two charts).
- `Exercise Index - §3.1 Fibre Bundles` — lists: [Ex - The Möbius Strip as a Mapping Torus is a Nontrivial Bundle, Ex - The Unit Tangent Bundle of S^2 is Diffeomorphic to SO(3), Ex - Pull-Back of a Fibre Bundle along a Constant Map is Trivial, Ex - The Klein Bottle as a Mapping Torus]

### §3.2 Principal Bundles and the Frame Bundle
- `Def - Principal G-Bundle` **[anchor]** — type: definition; source items: A-D2.2.1, B-D2.2.1, B-R2.2.1, B-T2.2.1, B-D2.2.9, B-R2.2.3, B-E2.2.1 (statement; the Hopf bundle is `Def - The Hopf Bundle`), A §2.2 conventions (motivation "all frames at once"); prereqs: [Def - Fibre Bundle, Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions, Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action]; spec: A principal $G$-bundle is a fibre bundle $\pi\colon P\to M$ with a smooth free right $G$-action preserving fibres and transitive on them, admitting $G$-equivariant local trivialisations $\psi_U(pg)=\psi_U(p)\cdot g$; both sources' four-clause definitions are shown equivalent on the page (A's "surjective submersion + free transitive on fibres + equivariant trivialisations" and B's "fibre bundle + free + transitive + equivariant trivialisations"). Corollaries proved: the orbit map $\ell_p\colon G\to P_{\pi(p)}$ is a diffeomorphism (B-T2.2.1, with the injectivity of $d_e\ell_p$ from the freeness argument via fundamental vector fields); the fibre carries no natural group structure; local sections are exactly local gauges (B-D2.9, R2.2.3 in Relate to Other Fields: "a choice of units"). Per-clause failure analysis: drop freeness — $S^2\times SO(2)\to S^2$ with the rotation action; drop equivariance of trivialisations — a bundle with fibre $G$ but no structure (mapping torus of an inner automorphism, which is still principal, versus of an outer one); drop transitivity — a subbundle. Verified examples: $M\times G$; $G\to G/H$ (link DG XI theorem, restated); the Hopf bundle (link). Categorical: a $G$-torsor bundle; a principal bundle is a functor to $G$-torsors. Convention callout: right action throughout; B's occasional $G\backslash P$ notation. Calibration: a principal bundle is trivial iff it has a section (forward to the theorem below); the structure group of $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ is $GL_k$.
- `Thm - Free Proper Actions Give Principal Bundles` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.2.2, B-I2.2.2, B-I2.2.3, A-R2.2.3 (the manifold structure on $(P\times V)/G$), A-I2.2.2; prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Thm - Quotient Manifold Theorem for Free Proper Actions]; statement: If a Lie group $G$ acts smoothly, freely and properly on the right on a manifold $P$ (in particular if $G$ is compact and the action free), then $(P,\pi,P/G)$ with $\pi(p)=pG$ is a principal $G$-bundle. Moreover, if $P$ is a principal $G$-bundle and $G$ acts on a manifold $F$ on the left, then the diagonal action $(p,f)\cdot g=(pg,g^{-1}f)$ on $P\times F$ is free and proper and $P\times_GF:=(P\times F)/G\to M$ is a fibre bundle with fibre $F$ (the associated fibre bundle; the vector and principal cases are §3.4); proof: source sketch; write: the quotient manifold theorem gives the manifold structure, submersion, and local sections $s$; define $\psi_U^{-1}(x,g):=s(x)g$ and prove it is an equivariant diffeomorphism (injective by freeness, surjective by transitivity on fibres, smooth inverse via the slice charts); for the second part prove properness of the diagonal action from properness of the $G$-action on $P$ (a sequence argument) and construct the local trivialisations from local sections of $P$; reference: Lee ISM 2e Thm 21.10 + Kobayashi–Nomizu I Prop I.5.? ; spec: Convention callout: A calls the $P\times V$ action "properly discontinuous"; correct hypothesis is free and proper (properly discontinuous is the discrete case).
- `Def - Frame Bundle of a Vector Bundle` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.2.2, A-R2.2.1, B-D2.2.2, B-T2.2.3, B-E2.2.2, B-E2.2.3, B-E2.2.4 (table); prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - Vector Bundle, Def - Local Frame]; spec: $\operatorname{Fr}(E)=\bigsqcup_m\operatorname{Fr}(E_m)$, frames as isomorphisms $\mathbb R^k\to E_m$, right $GL_k(\mathbb R)$-action $e\cdot h$, charts $\Psi_U(m,h)=e(m)h$ (24) with transition $(m,h)\mapsto(m,g(m)h)$ — the full verification that these charts define a Hausdorff second-countable smooth structure and that all four principal-bundle clauses hold (R2.2.1's "clear from the construction" written out; B-T2.2.3's local triviality written out); the orthonormal frame bundle $O(E)$ of a Euclidean bundle and $U(E)$ of a Hermitian bundle (smooth structure via Gram–Schmidt — deferred to the reductions theorem); the table of structure groups $GL(n;\mathbb K)$, $O(n)$, $U(n)$, $GL^+(n;\mathbb R)$, $SO(n)$ for bases respecting extra structure (E2.2.4; each row verified after the reductions theorem). Non-example: the set of all bases of $TM$ with the *left* action $h\cdot e$ is not a right principal bundle without the inverse. Calibration: $\operatorname{Fr}(M\times\mathbb R^k)=M\times GL_k$; $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ has a global section iff $E$ is trivial (via the next theorem).
- `Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-X2.2.1 (all but the $TS^2$ clause, which is a §3.5 exercise), B-T2.2.8, B-R2.2.2, B-T2.2.5, B-E2.2.5; prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - Frame Bundle of a Vector Bundle]; statement: For a principal $G$-bundle $P\to M$ and open $U\subset M$ there is a bijection between equivariant local trivialisations over $U$ and smooth local sections over $U$, $\psi_U\mapsto s:=\psi_U^{-1}(\cdot,e)$, with inverse $s\mapsto(\psi_U(p):=(\pi(p),g(p))$ where $p=s(\pi(p))g(p))$; in particular $P$ is trivial iff it admits a global section; a vector bundle $E$ is trivial iff $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ admits a global section; and a principal $H$-bundle admits a reduction to the trivial group iff it is trivial (B-T2.2.5, after `Def - Reduction and Extension of the Structure Group` — state it there as a corollary and prove it here); proof: source full (both); write smoothness of $g(p)$ (from the slice/trivialisation) and equivariance; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Prop I.5.? / Husemoller Ch. 4 Thm 8.3; spec: Mechanism: "a section picks the identity element in each fibre, and freeness plus transitivity then coordinatises the fibre by $G$."
- `Def - Reduction and Extension of the Structure Group` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.2.3, A-R2.2.2, B-D2.2.4, B-T2.2.4, B-T2.2.5 (statement), B-E2.2.5, B-T2.2.14 (statement; proof on the cocycle theorem); prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - Frame Bundle of a Vector Bundle, Thm - Free Proper Actions Give Principal Bundles, Def - Lie Group Homomorphism]; spec: For a Lie group homomorphism $\varphi\colon G\to H$, the extension $P\times_\varphi H:=(P\times H)/G$ with $(p,h)g=(pg,\varphi(g)^{-1}h)$ and right $H$-action $[p,h]h'=[p,hh']$ — proved on the page to be a principal $H$-bundle (B-T2.2.4 in full for all $G$: freeness and properness of the $G$-action on $P\times H$, the $H$-action descends and is free and transitive on fibres, local trivialisations from local sections of $P$; B's misprint $P\times_\varphi G$ noted); a $G$-structure on $E$ (A-D2.2.3: a $G$-subbundle of $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ for $G\le GL_k$ closed) and a reduction of $Q$ to $G$ (a $G$-bundle $P$ with $P\times_GH\cong Q$); equivalence of the two notions for $G\le H$ closed proved on the page (a $G$-subbundle $P\subset Q$ gives $P\times_GH\cong Q$ via $[p,h]\mapsto ph$; conversely the image of $P\times\{e\}$). Verified examples: $O(E)\subset\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ (after the next theorem), $SO(E)$ for oriented Euclidean bundles, $\operatorname{Fr}(E)\subset\operatorname{Fr}(E\otimes\mathbb C)$; A-R2.2.2 in Relate to Other Fields; non-example: a $U(1)$-subbundle of $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ for a real rank-2 bundle need not exist (the Möbius bundle is non-orientable). Calibration: reduction to $\{e\}$ iff trivial (corollary, proved via the sections theorem).
- `Def - Complex Vector Bundle and Hermitian Structure` — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.2.6, A-D2.2.7, A-D2.2.5, B-D2.1.8 (complex case), A-X2.2.3(i) (as corollary: complex bundles are locally trivial families of complex vector spaces — proved); prereqs: [Def - Vector Bundle, Def - Euclidean Vector Bundle and Metric Connection]; spec: complex structure $I\in\Gamma(\operatorname{End}E)$, $I^2=-1$; complex vector bundle; the equivalence with "fibre bundle with fibre $\mathbb C^k$ and complex-linear trivialisations" proved (local complex frames from a real frame by the $I$-adapted Gram–Schmidt-free argument: choose $v_1,\dots,v_k$ with $v_j,Iv_j$ a real basis near $m$); Hermitian structure; fibrewise volume form (A-D2.2.5); complex line bundles and their tensor products, duals, and conjugates; existence of Hermitian structures (corollary via partition of unity). Verified examples: $\mathcal O(-1)$ over $\mathbb{CP}^n$ as a complex line bundle (via II's operations page and chapter I's $\mathbb{CP}^n$), $TS^2$ with $I=$ rotation by $90^\circ$; non-example: an odd-rank real bundle has no complex structure. Calibration: $E$ with $I$ and with $-I$ are conjugate bundles; a Hermitian structure restricts to a Euclidean one.
- `Thm - Euclidean and Hermitian Structures are Reductions of the Structure Group` — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.2.1, A-I2.2.1, A-X2.2.2, A-X2.2.3(ii), A-X2.2.4(ii), B-E2.2.3, B-E2.2.4; prereqs: [Def - Reduction and Extension of the Structure Group, Def - Euclidean Vector Bundle and Metric Connection, Def - Complex Vector Bundle and Hermitian Structure, Thm - Gram-Schmidt Procedure]; statement: For a real vector bundle $E$ of rank $k$ there are natural bijections: Euclidean structures $\leftrightarrow$ $O(k)$-structures $P\subset\operatorname{Fr}(E)$; fibrewise volume forms $\leftrightarrow$ $SL_k(\mathbb R)$-structures; complex structures (for $k=2m$) $\leftrightarrow$ $GL_m(\mathbb C)$-structures; orientations $\leftrightarrow$ $GL_k^+$-structures; and for a complex bundle, Hermitian structures $\leftrightarrow$ $U(m)$-structures; proof: source "full (informal)" for the Euclidean case only; write: Lemma 1 (smooth Gram–Schmidt: applying the Gram–Schmidt formulas to a smooth frame gives a smooth orthonormal frame — the formulas are smooth in the entries), so $O(E)$ is a smooth $O(k)$-subbundle; Lemma 2 (conversely $\langle p x_1,p x_2\rangle:=x_1^tx_2$ is independent of $p\in P_m$ by $O(k)$-invariance and smooth in $m$ via local sections); the other three cases by the same pattern with $\det$, $I_{st}$, $\det>0$; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. I §5 (reductions), Taubes Ch. 10; spec: Targets: every vector bundle has a Euclidean structure, so every $GL_k$-bundle reduces to $O(k)$; the $U(1)$-language for line bundles (A-R2.4.1).
- `Ex - Fibrewise Volume Forms Correspond to SL(k,R)-Structures` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X2.2.2; prereqs: [Thm - Euclidean and Hermitian Structures are Reductions of the Structure Group]; spec: the exercise proves the $SL$ row of the theorem in detail as a drill (unimodular frames).
- `Ex - Complex Structures Correspond to GL(k,C)-Structures` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X2.2.3; prereqs: [Def - Complex Vector Bundle and Hermitian Structure, Thm - Euclidean and Hermitian Structures are Reductions of the Structure Group]; spec: both parts of A's exercise, with $GL_k(\mathbb C)=\{A:AI_{st}=I_{st}A\}$ made explicit.
- `Ex - Every Complex Vector Bundle Admits a Hermitian Structure` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-X2.2.4(i); prereqs: [Def - Complex Vector Bundle and Hermitian Structure]; spec: partition of unity; uniqueness up to homotopy (convexity) as a remark used in VI.
- `Ex - G over H is a Principal H-Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B §2.2 (example of principal bundle; DG XI statement); prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Thm - Homogeneous Space is a Smooth Manifold]; spec: verify the four clauses for $G\to G/H$ from DG XI's theorem; special cases $SO(3)\to S^2$, $SU(2)\to S^2$ (the Hopf bundle again, forward).
- `Ex - Orientations Correspond to GL-plus Reductions` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-E2.2.4 (table row); prereqs: [Thm - Euclidean and Hermitian Structures are Reductions of the Structure Group, Def - Orientation of a Smooth Manifold]; spec: an orientation of $E$ is a $GL_k^+$-structure; $E$ orientable iff $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ has a two-component subbundle; relate to DG IX's orientation of $TM$.
- `Ex - The Frame Bundle of a Trivial Bundle and the Trivial Principal Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-D2.2.2 drill; prereqs: [Def - Frame Bundle of a Vector Bundle, Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality]; spec: $\operatorname{Fr}(M\times\mathbb R^k)\cong M\times GL_k$ equivariantly; a global frame is a global section.
