### §3.3 Transition Functions and the Cocycle Description
- `Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D2.2.6, B-D2.2.7, B-D2.2.8, B-T2.2.10, B-T2.2.11, B §2.2 conventions; prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality, Def - Transition Function of a Vector Bundle]; spec: for local sections $s_\alpha$ over a cover, $g_{\alpha\beta}\colon U_{\alpha\beta}\to G$ with $s_\beta=s_\alpha g_{\alpha\beta}$ (existence, uniqueness and smoothness proved via the sections theorem); cocycle conditions $g_{\alpha\alpha}=e$, $g_{\alpha\beta}=g_{\beta\alpha}^{-1}$, $g_{\alpha\beta}g_{\beta\gamma}g_{\gamma\alpha}=e$ (B-T2.2.10 proved as corollaries, all three); the coboundary relation (2.1) $g_{\alpha\beta}=h_\alpha\tilde g_{\alpha\beta}h_\beta^{-1}$ under $\tilde s_\alpha=s_\alpha h_\alpha$ (B-T2.2.11 proved); the reconstruction data $\bigsqcup U_\alpha\times G/\sim$ (definition; its properties are the next theorem); relation with DG VI's transition functions of a vector bundle (for $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ they coincide — proved). Convention callout: $s_\beta=s_\alpha g_{\alpha\beta}$ (B) versus $e=e'g$ (A, (6)): same convention after renaming. Calibration: the transition functions of a trivial bundle with global section are all $e$; of the frame bundle of $TM$ in two charts, the Jacobians.
- `Thm - Principal Bundles are Classified by Cocycles` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.2.12, B-D2.2.8, B-T2.2.14, B-R2.2.18? (none), A-E2.1? (none); prereqs: [Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition, Thm - Vector Bundle Construction Lemma, Def - Reduction and Extension of the Structure Group, Thm - Quotient by a Properly Discontinuous Action is a Manifold and a Covering Map]; statement: (a) Given an open cover $\{U_\alpha\}$ of $M$ and smooth $g_{\alpha\beta}\colon U_{\alpha\beta}\to G$ satisfying the cocycle conditions, $P:=\bigsqcup_\alpha U_\alpha\times G/\sim$ with $(x,g)\sim(x,g_{\alpha\beta}(x)g')$ is a principal $G$-bundle over $M$ with sections $s_\alpha(x)=[x,e]_\alpha$ whose transition functions are the $g_{\alpha\beta}$; (b) every principal $G$-bundle with transition functions $\{g_{\alpha\beta}\}$ is isomorphic to the bundle so constructed; (c) two cocycles on the same cover give isomorphic bundles iff they are cohomologous, $g_{\alpha\beta}=h_\alpha\tilde g_{\alpha\beta}h_\beta^{-1}$; (d) the transition functions of the extension $P\times_\varphi H$ with respect to $s'_\alpha=[s_\alpha,e]$ are $\varphi\circ g_{\alpha\beta}$, and those of an associated vector bundle $P\times_\rho V$ are $\rho\circ g_{\alpha\beta}$; proof: source: well-definedness of the map in (c) full, everything else asserted. Write: (a) $\sim$ is an equivalence relation (cocycle conditions used clause by clause), the quotient is Hausdorff and second countable (explicit: the quotient map is open and points in different fibres or in the same fibre separate), smooth structure by declaring $U_\alpha\times G\to P$ to be diffeomorphisms onto open sets (transition maps smooth), the right action and its freeness/transitivity, trivialisations; (b) the map $[x,g]_\alpha\mapsto s_\alpha(x)g$ is a well-defined equivariant diffeomorphism; (c) both directions (⇐ B's map; ⇒ an isomorphism $\phi$ gives $\phi(s_\alpha)=\tilde s_\alpha h_\alpha$); (d) B-T2.2.14's computation, and the vector-bundle case with DG VI's construction lemma; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Prop I.5.2, Steenrod §3.2–3.3, Husemoller Ch. 5 Thm 3.2; spec: Mechanism: "a principal bundle is a recipe for gluing trivial pieces, and the recipe is the cocycle; changing the local sections changes the recipe by a coboundary." Targets: Čech-style invariants (bold outlook), Bär's physics reading (transition functions are changes of gauge).
- `Def - The Hopf Bundle` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-E2.2.3 (bundle part; the connection is IV), B-E2.2.1, B-T2.2.13, B-E2.2.8, A-D2.4.6 (quaternionic Hopf bundles $S^{4n+3}\to\mathbb{HP}^n$, definitions; the $Sp(1)$ action), A-R2.4.2 (ladder of inclusions); prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - Complex Projective Space as a Quotient, Thm - Free Proper Actions Give Principal Bundles, Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition]; spec: The $U(1)$-bundle $\pi\colon S^{2n+1}\to\mathbb{CP}^n$, $z\mapsto[z]$, with the right action $z\cdot\lambda=z\lambda$ (principal by the free-compact theorem); for $n=1$ the identification of the base with $S^2$ via chapter I; B's explicit local sections $s_1,s_2$ over $U_1,U_2$ and the transition function $g_{12}(z,t)=z/|z|$ (B-T2.2.13 computed on the page in full, resolving B's "$=|z|/z$" misprint); the quaternionic Hopf bundles $S^{4n+3}\to\mathbb{HP}^n$ with $Sp(1)$ acting by right multiplication (freeness verified; $\mathbb{HP}^n$ defined as the quotient), and the ladders $S^3\subset S^5\subset\cdots$, $S^7\subset S^{11}\subset\cdots$ (R2.4.2) as commuting squares of bundle maps; the tautological line bundle $\mathcal O(-1)$ (bold forward reference to §3.4 where it is shown associated). Verified: the fibres are the $U(1)$-orbits (great circles); non-example: $S^{2n+1}\to\mathbb{CP}^n$ is not trivial (next theorem). Convention callout: B's stereographic normalisation of $S^2$ (south pole $(0,-1)$) versus AT III `Def - The Hopf Map`. Calibration: compute $g_{12}$ on the equator; check $g_{12}g_{21}=1$.
- `Thm - The Hopf Bundle is Nontrivial` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.2.9, B-E2.2.7, B-I2.2.1 (replaced); prereqs: [Def - The Hopf Bundle, Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality, Thm - The Mayer-Vietoris Sequence, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of de Rham Cohomology]; statement: The Hopf bundle $S^3\to S^2$ admits no global section; equivalently $S^3\not\cong S^2\times S^1$ as bundles (indeed not even diffeomorphic); more generally $S^{2n+1}\to\mathbb{CP}^n$ is nontrivial; proof: B uses $\pi_1$ facts (I2.2.1) that the vault does not prove; replace by de Rham cohomology: a global section would give a diffeomorphism $S^{2n+1}\cong\mathbb{CP}^n\times S^1$, but $H^1_{dR}(\mathbb{CP}^n\times S^1)\ne0$ (the form $\operatorname{pr}_2^*d\theta$ is closed and integrates to $2\pi$ over $\{pt\}\times S^1$, so it is not exact by Stokes) while $H^1_{dR}(S^{2n+1})=0$ (DG X's Mayer–Vietoris corollary for spheres, restated); a second proof via the transition function $z/|z|$ and the winding number is given after §3.5 as an exercise; spec: Targets: the first nontrivial principal bundle; the hairy-ball theorem (§3.5) gives a third proof for $n=1$.
- `Ex - Explicit Local Sections and the Transition Function of the Hopf Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-T2.2.13, B-E2.2.8; prereqs: [Def - The Hopf Bundle]; spec: verify $s_1,s_2$ are sections (norm one, correct image under the Hopf map) using $(1-t)(1+t)=|z|^2$, and compute $s_1\cdot(z/|z|)=s_2$.
- `Ex - The Cocycle of the Frame Bundle of the Sphere in Stereographic Charts` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-D2.2.6 drill; prereqs: [Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition, Def - Frame Bundle of a Vector Bundle]; spec: with the two stereographic charts, the transition function of $\operatorname{Fr}(TS^2)$ is the Jacobian of the chart change $z\mapsto1/z$ (stereographic charts from the two poles, orientation-compatible); compute $g_{12}(z)$ explicitly as a $GL_2(\mathbb R)$-valued map, show it is $|z|^{-2}$ times a rotation matrix, and reduce to $SO(2)=U(1)$ after normalising: $g_{12}=(z/|z|)^{\pm2}$ — foreshadowing $c_1(TS^2)[S^2]=2$ (VI).
- `Ex - Cohomologous Cocycles Give Isomorphic Bundles and Conversely` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-T2.2.12(c) drill; prereqs: [Thm - Principal Bundles are Classified by Cocycles]; spec: prove both directions on a two-set cover of $S^1$ and deduce that $U(1)$-bundles over $S^1$ are all trivial ($U(1)$ connected: the single transition function on the two arcs deforms to $e$ — write the coboundary explicitly using a logarithm on each component of the overlap).
- `Ex - Reconstructing the Möbius Bundle from a Z over 2 Cocycle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-D2.2.8 drill; prereqs: [Thm - Principal Bundles are Classified by Cocycles]; spec: the $O(1)=\{\pm1\}$-cocycle on the two-arc cover of $S^1$ with $g_{12}=+1$ on one component and $-1$ on the other reconstructs the frame bundle of the Möbius line bundle; show the cocycle is not a coboundary.
- `Exercise Index - §3.3 Transition Functions and the Cocycle Description` — lists: [Ex - Explicit Local Sections and the Transition Function of the Hopf Bundle, Ex - The Cocycle of the Frame Bundle of the Sphere in Stereographic Charts, Ex - Cohomologous Cocycles Give Isomorphic Bundles and Conversely, Ex - Reconstructing the Möbius Bundle from a Z over 2 Cocycle]

### §3.4 Associated Bundles
- `Def - Associated Bundle` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.2.8, A-R2.2.3, B-D2.2.5, B-D2.2.4 (restated), A-D2.2.9, A-R2.2.4, A §2.2 conventions ($[p,v]$, $(p,v)\cdot g=(pg,\rho(g^{-1})v)$, $\varpi$); prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Thm - Free Proper Actions Give Principal Bundles, Def - Representation of a Lie Group, Def - Vector Bundle]; spec: $E=P\times_\rho V:=(P\times V)/G$; manifold structure by the free-proper theorem; fibrewise vector space structure well defined ($[p,v]+[p,w]=[p,v+w]$ independent of the representative — proved); local trivialisations $(m,v)\mapsto[s(m),v]$ from local sections (proved to be smooth with smooth inverse); the space $C^\infty(P;V)^G$ of equivariant functions and the induced section $s$ with $s\circ\pi=\varpi\circ(\operatorname{id},\hat s)$ (R2.2.4 diagram; the bijection is the theorem below). Verified examples: $P\times_\rho V$ for $P=M\times G$ is $M\times V$; $\mathcal O(-1)=S^{2n+1}\times_{U(1)}\mathbb C$ (with $\rho_{-1}$? — fix: with $\rho(\lambda)v=\lambda v$, i.e. $\rho_1$, the class $[z,v]\mapsto vz$ lands in the line $[z]$; the exercise X2.2.6 verifies) ; $L^{\otimes k}=P\times_{\rho_k}\mathbb C$ for a $U(1)$-bundle (via chapter I's $\varrho_k$; proved as example); non-example: $P\times V$ with the action $(pg,\rho(g)v)$ does not descend to a bundle of the same type (it is $P\times_{\rho^{op}}V$ for the inverse-transpose convention — record). Convention callout: both sources use $\rho(g^{-1})$; the associated bundle of a left $G$-space via $g^{-1}$. Categorical: the functor $V\mapsto P\times_GV$ from $G$-representations to vector bundles; a principal bundle is the "universal frame". Calibration: rank of $P\times_\rho V$; $P\times_{\operatorname{triv}}V$ is trivial.
- `Thm - Vector Bundles are Associated to Their Frame Bundles` — type: theorem; source items: A-E2.2.1, A-E2.2.2, B-T2.2.6, B-T2.2.7, A-X2.2.5 (statement; exercise below); prereqs: [Def - Associated Bundle, Def - Frame Bundle of a Vector Bundle, Def - Constructions on Representations, Def - Operations on Vector Bundles and Pull-Back Bundles]; statement: For a rank-$k$ vector bundle $E$ with frame bundle $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$: (a) $\operatorname{Fr}(E)\times_{\operatorname{id}}\mathbb R^k\to E$, $[e,x]\mapsto e\cdot x$, is an isomorphism of vector bundles; (b) $\operatorname{Fr}(E)\times_{\Lambda^p\rho^*}\Lambda^p(\mathbb R^k)^*\cong\Lambda^pE^*$ via $[e,\alpha]\mapsto\alpha(e^{-1}\cdot,\dots,e^{-1}\cdot)$; (c) likewise $E^*$, $E\otimes F$, $E\oplus F$, $\operatorname{Hom}(E,F)$, $\operatorname{End}E$ are associated to $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ (or to $\operatorname{Fr}(E)\times_M\operatorname{Fr}(F)$) via the corresponding constructions on the standard representation; (d) if $P\subset\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ is a $G$-structure then $P\times_G\mathbb R^k\cong E$; proof: source: (a) well-definedness full (B), (b) map given, (c) stated; write for each: well-definedness on classes, fibrewise linear isomorphism, smoothness in local trivialisations (both directions), and equality of transition functions with those of II's operations page; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. I §5 Ex., Taubes Ch. 10; spec: Targets: connections on $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ versus on $E$ (IV), gauge groups (V).
- `Thm - Sections of an Associated Bundle are Equivariant Functions` — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.2.2, A-D2.2.9, A-R2.2.4; prereqs: [Def - Associated Bundle]; statement: $C^\infty(P;V)^G\to\Gamma(P\times_\rho V)$, $\hat s\mapsto s$, is a bijection (indeed a $C^\infty(M)$-module isomorphism); proof: source full modulo the equivariance and smoothness of the inverse — write: for a section $s$ and $p\in P$ the element $\hat s(p)$ is the unique $v$ with $s(\pi p)=[p,v]$ (freeness and transitivity), equivariance from $[pg,v]=[p,\rho(g)v]$, smoothness via local sections and trivialisations; spec: Targets: local connection forms as equivariant functions; the gauge group as sections of $\operatorname{Ad}P$ (V).
- `Def - Equivariant and Basic Forms on a Principal Bundle` — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.2.10 (vertical subspace $V_p=\ker d\pi_p=\{K_\xi(p)\}$ — proved: $d\pi(K_\xi)=0$ and dimension count using freeness), A-D2.2.11, A §2.2 conventions ($R_g^*\omega=\rho(g^{-1})\omega$), B-R2.3.1 (vertical vectors are exactly the fundamental fields); prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action, Def - Bundle-Valued Differential Forms, Def - Pullback of a Differential Form on a Manifold]; spec: $V$-valued forms on $P$; equivariant ($R_g^*\omega=\rho(g^{-1})\omega$) and basic (vanish on vertical vectors) forms; $\Omega^q_{\mathrm{bas}}(P;V)^G$; the invariant case ($\rho$ trivial); verified examples: $\pi^*\alpha$ for $\alpha\in\Omega^q(M)$ is basic and invariant; the Maurer–Cartan-type form $\omega(K_\xi)=\xi$ on $M\times G$ is equivariant but not basic; non-example: $d f$ for $f\in C^\infty(P)$ not invariant. Calibration: a basic 0-form is a function; a form that is both basic and equivariant of type $\operatorname{Ad}$ descends to $\Omega^q(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$ (next theorem).
- `Def - Adjoint Bundles ad P and Ad P` — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.2.12, A-D2.2.19 (the bundle $\operatorname{Ad}P=P\times_GG$ only), B-D2.7.4; prereqs: [Def - Associated Bundle, Def - Adjoint Representation]; spec: $\operatorname{ad}P:=P\times_{\operatorname{Ad}}\mathfrak g$ (a vector bundle; for $G\subset GL_k$ a subbundle of $\operatorname{End}E$ — proved), $\operatorname{Ad}P:=P\times_{\alpha}G$ with $G$ acting on itself by conjugation (a fibre bundle of groups: fibrewise multiplication $[p,g][p,g']=[p,gg']$ well defined — proved; each fibre isomorphic to $G$; not a principal bundle in general — B-D2.7.4; has the global section $[p,e]$; the Lie algebra bundle of $\operatorname{Ad}P$ is $\operatorname{ad}P$ — proved fibrewise). Verified examples: for $G$ abelian, $\operatorname{ad}P=M\times\mathfrak g$ and $\operatorname{Ad}P=M\times G$; for $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$, $\operatorname{ad}\operatorname{Fr}(E)=\operatorname{End}E$ and $\operatorname{Ad}\operatorname{Fr}(E)=\operatorname{Aut}E$; non-example: $P\times_{L}G$ with left multiplication is $P$ itself, not a group bundle. Calibration: $\Gamma(\operatorname{Ad}P)$ is a group under pointwise multiplication (proved).
- `Thm - Bundle-Valued Forms on the Base are Basic Equivariant Forms on the Total Space` — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.2.3; prereqs: [Def - Equivariant and Basic Forms on a Principal Bundle, Def - Associated Bundle, Thm - Sections of an Associated Bundle are Equivariant Functions]; statement: For a principal $G$-bundle $P$, a representation $\rho$ on $V$ and $q\ge0$, the map $\Omega^q(M;P\times_\rho V)\to\Omega^q_{\mathrm{bas}}(P;V)^G$, $a\mapsto\hat a$ with $a(d\pi\hat v_1,\dots,d\pi\hat v_q)=[p,\hat a_p(\hat v_1,\dots,\hat v_q)]$, is a bijection (of $C^\infty(M)$-modules); for $\rho$ trivial it is $a\mapsto\pi^*a$ onto basic invariant forms; proof: source full modulo the equivariance computation and independence of choices — write: (⇒) $\hat a$ is well defined because $[p,\cdot]\colon V\to E_{\pi(p)}$ is an isomorphism; basic because $d\pi$ kills vertical vectors; equivariant because $[pg,\rho(g^{-1})v]=[p,v]$ and $d\pi\circ dR_g=d\pi$; smooth via local sections; (⇐) independence of the lift (difference of lifts is vertical, killed by basic-ness) and of $p$ (equivariance), smoothness; the two constructions are inverse; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. II §5 (tensorial forms), Baum *Eichfeldtheorie* Satz 3.5; spec: Mechanism: "basic kills the fibre directions, equivariance identifies the fibres, together they leave exactly a form on the base with values in the associated bundle." Targets: curvature of a principal connection lives on $M$ (IV), Chern–Weil forms descend (VI).
- `Ex - End(E) as an Associated Bundle of the Frame Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-X2.2.5; prereqs: [Thm - Vector Bundles are Associated to Their Frame Bundles]; spec: $[e,A]\mapsto eAe^{-1}$; well-definedness under $(eh,h^{-1}Ah)$; smoothness; identification with $\operatorname{ad}\operatorname{Fr}(E)$.
- `Ex - The Tautological Line Bundle over CP^n is Associated to the Hopf Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X2.2.6; prereqs: [Def - Associated Bundle, Def - The Hopf Bundle, Def - Complex Vector Bundle and Hermitian Structure]; spec: $S^{2n+1}\times_{U(1)}\mathbb C\to\mathcal O(-1)$, $[z,w]\mapsto([z],wz)$ — determine the representation (with the series' $(pg,\rho(g^{-1})v)$ convention the map is well defined for $\rho(\lambda)w=\lambda w$? check: $[z\lambda,\lambda^{-1}w]\mapsto\lambda^{-1}w\,z\lambda=wz$ ✓ so $\rho=\varrho_1$); prove it is a smooth isomorphism of complex line bundles; note $\mathcal O(-1)$ is a subbundle of $\mathbb{CP}^n\times\mathbb C^{n+1}$.
- `Ex - Vertical Vectors are Exactly the Fundamental Vector Fields` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-D2.2.10, B-R2.3.1; prereqs: [Def - Equivariant and Basic Forms on a Principal Bundle]; spec: prove $\ker d\pi_p=\{K_\xi(p)\}$ with the dimension count and freeness; deduce $V_p\cong\mathfrak g$ canonically.
- `Ex - The Adjoint Bundle of an Abelian Principal Bundle is Trivial` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-D2.2.12 drill, B-T2.7.3 (bundle part); prereqs: [Def - Adjoint Bundles ad P and Ad P]; spec: for abelian $G$, $\operatorname{Ad}=\operatorname{id}$, so $\operatorname{ad}P=M\times\mathfrak g$, $\operatorname{Ad}P=M\times G$; sections are maps $M\to\mathfrak g$, $M\to G$.
- `Ex - Sections of the Tensor Powers of a Line Bundle as Equivariant Functions` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T2.2.2 drill with chapter I's $\varrho_k$; prereqs: [Thm - Sections of an Associated Bundle are Equivariant Functions, Thm - Complex Representations of U(1) and SU(2)]; spec: for a $U(1)$-bundle $P$, sections of $P\times_{\varrho_k}\mathbb C$ are functions $\hat s\colon P\to\mathbb C$ with $\hat s(p\lambda)=\lambda^{-k}\hat s(p)$; identify $P\times_{\varrho_k}\mathbb C\cong L^{\otimes k}$ where $L=P\times_{\varrho_1}\mathbb C$; for the Hopf bundle and $k=-1$ exhibit the coordinate functions $z_j$ as sections of $\mathcal O(1)=\mathcal O(-1)^*$.
- `Exercise Index - §3.4 Associated Bundles` — lists: [Ex - End(E) as an Associated Bundle of the Frame Bundle, Ex - The Tautological Line Bundle over CP^n is Associated to the Hopf Bundle, Ex - Vertical Vectors are Exactly the Fundamental Vector Fields, Ex - The Adjoint Bundle of an Abelian Principal Bundle is Trivial, Ex - Sections of the Tensor Powers of a Line Bundle as Equivariant Functions]

### §3.5 Smooth-Topological Tools — Generic Sections, Degree, and Homotopy Invariance of Bundles
(No connection theory is used in this section. It exists because the classification theorems of §3.6 — placed in III by the anchors — need degree theory, transversality, and the homotopy invariance of bundles, and the vault's pages for Sard and for the degree are flagged.)
- `Thm - Sard's Theorem when the Source Dimension does not Exceed the Target Dimension` — type: theorem; source items: none of the sources (prerequisite; replaces the flagged DG IV `Thm - Sard's Theorem` in the only case chapters I–VI need; the full theorem is chapter X's); prereqs: [Def - Set of Measure Zero on a Manifold, Def - Regular and Critical Points, Def - Smooth Map between Manifolds]; statement: Let $f\colon M^m\to N^n$ be smooth with $m\le n$. Then the set of critical values of $f$ has measure zero in $N$; in particular (a) if $m<n$ then $f(M)$ has measure zero and empty interior; (b) if $m=n$ then the regular values are dense in $N$; (c) if $M$ is compact and $y$ is a regular value then $f^{-1}(y)$ is finite; proof: (a) Lee ISM 2e Prop 6.5 (Lipschitz estimate on compact cubes, countably many charts); (b) the one-page argument for $m=n$: on a compact cube $K$ in a chart, cover the critical set by cubes of side $\delta$; on a cube containing a critical point $x$, Taylor's theorem places $f(\text{cube})$ inside a set of measure $\le C\delta^{n-1}\cdot\epsilon(\delta)\delta$ with $\epsilon(\delta)\to0$ because $df_x$ has rank $<n$ (its image lies in a hyperplane), so the total measure is $\le C'\epsilon(\delta)\to0$ (Guillemin–Pollack, App. 1, "the case $m=n$"; Milnor TDV §3 Step 1 for the general structure); (c) discreteness of $f^{-1}(y)$ by the inverse function theorem (DG IV `Thm - The Rank Theorem`) plus compactness; measure zero is chart-independent (link DG IV `Def - Set of Measure Zero on a Manifold`); reference: Lee ISM 2e Prop 6.5, Thm 6.10 (restricted); Guillemin–Pollack Appendix 1; spec: Convention: the page states in bold that the general Sard theorem ($m>n$) is proved in chapter X as a lemma of Sard–Smale and is not used before. Targets: generic sections, degree, hairy ball, classification arguments below.
- `Thm - Generic Sections are Transverse to the Zero Section` — type: theorem; source items: none of the sources (needed for A-T2.4.3, A-E2.4.1 and the clutching arguments); prereqs: [Thm - Sard's Theorem when the Source Dimension does not Exceed the Target Dimension, Thm - Regular Value Theorem on Manifolds, Thm - Existence of Smooth Partitions of Unity, Def - Vector Bundle]; statement: Let $E\to M$ be a real vector bundle of rank $r$ over a compact manifold of dimension $m$. (a) There exist finitely many sections $s_1,\dots,s_N$ spanning every fibre. (b) For almost every $a\in\mathbb R^N$ the section $s_a=\sum a_is_i$ is transverse to the zero section: at each zero $x$ of $s_a$ the vertical derivative $D_xs_a\colon T_xM\to E_x$ is surjective. (c) Consequently, if $r>m$ then $E$ has a nowhere-vanishing section; if $r=m$ then $E$ has a section with finitely many zeros, each nondegenerate ($D_xs_a$ an isomorphism), and when $M$ and $E$ are oriented each zero carries a sign $\epsilon(x)=\operatorname{sign}\det D_xs_a$; (d) the same holds for sections prescribed on a neighbourhood of a closed set $K$ where a given section is already transverse (relative version, needed for the clutching arguments); proof: (a) local frames times bump functions; (b) $F\colon M\times\mathbb R^N\to E$, $F(x,a)=s_a(x)$, is a submersion (surjective on fibres by (a)), so $W=F^{-1}(0_E)$ is a submanifold of dimension $m+N-r$ (regular value theorem), and by the easy Sard theorem applied to $\operatorname{pr}_2|_W\colon W\to\mathbb R^N$ almost every $a$ is a regular value; unwind: $a$ regular for $\operatorname{pr}_2|_W$ iff $s_a$ transverse to $0_E$ (the standard parametric transversality argument, written in full with the tangent-space identification); (c) dimension count; (d) multiply the perturbation by a cutoff vanishing near $K$ and use openness of transversality on compact sets; reference: Guillemin–Pollack Ch. 2 §3 (Transversality Theorem, parametric form), Bott–Tu Prop 11.? (generic sections), Hirsch Ch. 3 Thm 2.5; spec: Targets: triviality of $SU(2)$-bundles in dimension $\le3$; sections of line bundles over surfaces with finitely many signed zeros; "every $SU(2)$-bundle over a 4-manifold is trivial off a point".
- `Thm - Homogeneity Lemma for Connected Manifolds` — type: theorem; source items: none of the sources (needed for the clutching arguments); prereqs: [Thm - Fundamental Theorem on Flows, Ex - Compactly Supported Vector Fields are Complete, Thm - Existence of Smooth Bump Functions]; statement: Let $M$ be a connected manifold, $x,y\in M$ and $K\subset M\setminus\{x,y\}$ a closed set with $M\setminus K$ connected. Then there is a diffeomorphism $h\colon M\to M$, smoothly isotopic to the identity through diffeomorphisms fixing $K$ pointwise, with $h(x)=y$. Consequently any finite set of points of a connected manifold of dimension $\ge2$ can be moved into any prescribed coordinate disc by a diffeomorphism isotopic to the identity; proof: Milnor TDV §4 "Homogeneity Lemma": inside a coordinate ball, flow along a compactly supported vector field; the set of points reachable from $x$ by such diffeomorphisms is open and closed; for the finite-set clause use connectedness of $M$ minus finitely many points (proved: removing a finite set from a connected manifold of dimension $\ge2$ leaves it connected — a chart argument) and iterate; reference: Milnor, *Topology from the Differentiable Viewpoint*, §4, Lemma (Homogeneity) and its proof; spec: Targets: gathering the zeros of a section into one disc, so that a bundle is trivial off a disc.
- `Thm - The Top de Rham Cohomology of a Closed Connected Oriented Manifold is R` — type: theorem; source items: A-I3.1.3 (in its de Rham reading), needed for the degree; prereqs: [Def - de Rham Cohomology, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Thm - Integration is Well-Defined on Oriented Manifolds, Thm - The Poincaré Lemma on a Star-Shaped Region, Thm - Existence of Smooth Partitions of Unity]; statement: If $N$ is a closed connected oriented $n$-manifold then $\int_N\colon H^n_{dR}(N)\to\mathbb R$ is an isomorphism; equivalently an $n$-form is exact iff its integral vanishes; proof: Lee ISM 2e Thm 17.31 and Lemma 17.27 (compactly supported Poincaré lemma on $\mathbb R^n$: a compactly supported $n$-form with integral zero is $d$ of a compactly supported $(n-1)$-form — proved by induction on $n$ using integration in the last variable) and the "chain of balls" lemma (every $n$-form is cohomologous to one supported in a given coordinate ball, using a partition of unity and connectedness); write all steps; reference: Lee ISM 2e Lemma 17.27, Thm 17.30, Thm 17.31; Bott–Tu Prop 4.7 / Thm 5.? (compactly supported version); spec: Bridges: Hodge I `Thm - Poincare Duality via Hodge Star` gives the same statement analytically; RG IV `Def - Brouwer Degree of a Map` asserts exactly this exactness. Targets: the degree; the integral of the Chern form over a surface as the only invariant (VI).
- `Thm - The Brouwer Degree is an Integer and a Homotopy Invariant` — type: theorem; source items: none of the sources directly (replaces flagged RG IV theorem; needed for A-X3.2.1(d), the $SU(2)$ classification, the hairy-ball theorem, and B-D2.4.4's degree); prereqs: [Def - Brouwer Degree of a Map, Thm - The Top de Rham Cohomology of a Closed Connected Oriented Manifold is R, Thm - Sard's Theorem when the Source Dimension does not Exceed the Target Dimension, Thm - Change of Variables for Integration on Manifolds, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of de Rham Cohomology, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; statement: Let $M,N$ be closed oriented $n$-manifolds, $N$ connected, $f\colon M\to N$ smooth, and $\deg f:=\int_Mf^*\omega$ for any $\omega\in\Omega^n(N)$ with $\int_N\omega=1$ (RG IV's definition, linked). Then: (a) $\deg f$ is independent of $\omega$; (b) for every regular value $y$, $\deg f=\sum_{x\in f^{-1}(y)}\operatorname{sign}\det df_x$, hence $\deg f\in\mathbb Z$; (c) smoothly homotopic maps have equal degree; (d) if $\deg f\ne0$ then $f$ is surjective; (e) $\deg(g\circ f)=\deg g\cdot\deg f$; (f) if $M=\partial W$ for a compact oriented $(n+1)$-manifold $W$ and $f$ extends smoothly to $W$, then $\deg f=0$; (g) if $M$ is disconnected the degree is the sum over components; proof: (a) by the top-cohomology theorem; (b) choose $\omega$ supported in an evenly covered neighbourhood of the regular value $y$ (finitely many preimages by easy Sard (c)), apply change of variables on each sheet; (c) DG X's homotopy invariance (restated); (d) contrapositive: if $y\notin f(M)$ take $\omega$ supported near $y$; (e) pull-back functoriality; (f) Stokes; reference: Lee ISM 2e Thm 17.35 and Prop 17.36; Milnor TDV §5; spec: Convention: orientation conventions for $\partial W$ as in DG IX. Targets: winding numbers, the degree of $q\mapsto q^k$ on $S^3$, the Chern number of an $SU(2)$-bundle, the gauge variation of Chern–Simons (VI).
- `Thm - Hairy Ball Theorem` — type: theorem; source items: A-I2.1.1, B-T1.5.7 (obstruction clause), B-R1.5.2, B-I1.5.3; prereqs: [Thm - The Brouwer Degree is an Integer and a Homotopy Invariant, Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action]; statement: The sphere $S^{2n}$ admits no nowhere-vanishing tangent vector field. Corollaries: the antipodal map of $S^{2n}$ is not homotopic to the identity; $TS^{2n}$ is not trivial and $\operatorname{Fr}(TS^{2n})$ has no section; no Lie group of positive dimension acts freely on $S^{2n}$; proof: Milnor TDV §6: a nowhere-vanishing field $v$ gives the homotopy $x\mapsto\cos(t)x+\sin(t)v(x)/|v(x)|$ from the identity to the antipodal map, whose degree is $(-1)^{2n+1}=-1$ (computed via the regular-value formula, as a lemma: a reflection has degree $-1$, the antipodal map is a product of $2n+1$ reflections); contradiction with homotopy invariance; the free-action corollary via the fundamental vector field of a one-parameter subgroup (chapter I page); reference: Milnor TDV §6 Thm; spec: Bridges: RG IV `Thm - Poincare-Hopf Theorem for Surfaces` and `Ex - Hairy Ball Theorem from Poincare-Hopf` (the $S^2$ case by another route). This page discharges the "$\chi(M)=0$" claim of B Rem 1.5.19 in the only case the sources use ($S^{2n}$); the general Poincaré–Hopf statement is not claimed.
- `Thm - Winding Number of a Map from the Circle to U(1)` — type: theorem; source items: none of the sources directly (needed for B-T2.2.13's meaning, A-E2.4.1, A-R3.1.5, B-T2.5.8, and V's abelian holonomy); prereqs: [Thm - The Brouwer Degree is an Integer and a Homotopy Invariant, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; statement: For smooth $g\colon S^1\to U(1)$ define $w(g):=\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\int_{S^1}g^*d\theta=\tfrac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{S^1}g^{-1}dg$. Then (a) $w(g)\in\mathbb Z$ and $w(g)=\deg g$; (b) $w(g_1g_2)=w(g_1)+w(g_2)$ and $w(g^{-1})=-w(g)$ (pointwise product); (c) $w$ is invariant under smooth homotopy; (d) $w(g)=0$ iff $g=e^{iu}$ for a smooth $u\colon S^1\to\mathbb R$ iff $g$ extends to a smooth map $D^2\to U(1)$; (e) $w(z\mapsto z^k)=k$; proof: (a) via the degree theorem ($d\theta/2\pi$ is a normalised volume form); (b) $d\theta$ is bi-invariant and $(g_1g_2)^*d\theta=g_1^*d\theta+g_2^*d\theta$ exactly (since $U(1)$ is abelian); (c) homotopy invariance; (d) $u(t):=\int_0^tg^*d\theta$ descends iff $w=0$; extension over the disc by $e^{iu(r,\theta)}$ with a cutoff, and conversely by Stokes; (e) direct; reference: Lee ISM 2e Ex. 17-? / Bott–Tu §?; AT II `Thm - Pi_1 of S^1 is Z` is a bridge (the same integer via lifts); spec: Targets: the Hopf transition function $z/|z|$ has $w=1$, so the Hopf bundle is nontrivial (exercise below); the degree of a line bundle over a surface (§3.6); abelian holonomy (V).
- `Thm - Homotopic Maps Pull Back Isomorphic Principal Bundles` — type: theorem; source items: A-I3.3.2 (bundles over an interval are trivial — corollary), B-D2.1.7 (triviality, used), needed for A-T2.4.1, A-E2.4.1, A-D2.4.2 ("depend only on the isomorphism class"), B-T2.1.1 ("only if"); prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Thm - The Pull-Back of a Fibre Bundle is a Fibre Bundle, Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality, Thm - Existence of Smooth Partitions of Unity, Def - Smooth Homotopy of Maps]; statement: (a) Let $P\to M\times[0,1]$ be a principal $G$-bundle over a compact manifold $M$ (more generally over any manifold $M$). Then $P\cong\operatorname{pr}^*(P|_{M\times\{0\}})$; in particular the restrictions to $M\times\{0\}$ and $M\times\{1\}$ are isomorphic. (b) If $f_0,f_1\colon M'\to M$ are smoothly homotopic then $f_0^*P\cong f_1^*P$ for every principal bundle $P\to M$. (c) Every principal bundle over a contractible manifold, and every principal bundle over a closed disc $D^n$ or over $[0,1]$, is trivial. (d) If $h\colon M\to M$ is a diffeomorphism isotopic to the identity then $h^*P\cong P$. (e) The same statements hold for vector bundles and for fibre bundles with structure group $G$; proof: Hatcher, *Vector Bundles and K-Theory*, Thm 1.6 and Prop 1.7 (written for vector bundles; the principal case is identical using equivariant trivialisations): Lemma 1 (if $P$ over $M\times[a,b]$ is trivial over $M\times[a,c]$ and $M\times[c,b]$ then over $M\times[a,b]$ — glue the two trivialisations by adjusting the second with the transition function on $M\times\{c\}$ extended by the projection); Lemma 2 (for compact $M$, a finite cover by $U_i$ with $P$ trivial over $U_i\times[0,1]$ exists — Lebesgue number of the cover of $M\times[0,1]$ by trivialising sets); Lemma 3 (construct the isomorphism $P\to\operatorname{pr}^*P|_{M\times0}$ inductively over $U_1\cup\dots\cup U_i$ using a partition of unity to "slide" — Hatcher's argument with $\psi_i=\rho_1+\dots+\rho_i$); Lemma 4 (paracompact case: Hatcher's countable-union trick); (b) apply (a) to $H^*P$ over $M'\times[0,1]$; (c),(d) corollaries; reference: Hatcher VBKT Thm 1.6, Prop 1.7; Husemoller Ch. 4 Thm 9.9; Steenrod §11; spec: Mechanism: "a bundle over $M\times I$ can be slid along the $I$ direction because triviality over a strip lets us extend a trivialisation from one end." Targets: classification by homotopy classes of maps (§3.6); characteristic classes depend only on the isomorphism class (VI); the mapping torus of $\phi$ is trivial only if $\phi$ is isotopic to the identity (corollary via clutching).
- `Thm - Clutching Construction for Bundles over a Closed Manifold` — type: theorem; source items: none of the sources (mechanism behind A-E2.4.1, A-T2.4.3's downstream use and R3.1.6/(94)); prereqs: [Thm - Principal Bundles are Classified by Cocycles, Thm - Homotopic Maps Pull Back Isomorphic Principal Bundles]; statement: Let $X$ be a closed connected $n$-manifold, $D\subset X$ a closed coordinate disc with interior $D^\circ$ and boundary $S=\partial D\cong S^{n-1}$, and $G$ a Lie group. (a) For every smooth $g\colon S\to G$ there is a principal $G$-bundle $P_g$, trivial over $X\setminus D^\circ$ and over $D$, with transition function (the restriction to a collar of $S$ of) $g$ — constructed as the cocycle bundle on the cover $\{X\setminus D^\circ$-thickened, $D$-thickened$\}$ using a collar. (b) Every principal $G$-bundle $P$ that is trivial over $X\setminus D^\circ$ and over $D$ is isomorphic to some $P_g$; if $G$ is connected and $P$ is trivial over $X\setminus\{x_0\}$ for a point $x_0\in D^\circ$, then $P$ is trivial over $X\setminus D^\circ$ and over $D$ (by (c) of the previous theorem), hence of the form $P_g$. (c) $P_g\cong P_{g'}$ iff there are smooth $a\colon X\setminus D^\circ\to G$ and $b\colon D\to G$ with $g'=(a|_S)\,g\,(b|_S)$; in particular $P_g\cong P_{g'}$ whenever $g$ and $g'$ are smoothly homotopic (a homotopy of clutching maps extends over a collar) ; (d) $P_g$ is trivial iff $g=(a|_S)(b|_S)$ as in (c) with $g'=e$; proof: (a),(b) from the cocycle theorem and the collar neighbourhood of $S$ (existence of a collar for $\partial D$ in a coordinate disc is explicit: $D$ is a round ball in a chart); (c) both directions from the cocycle theorem's cohomology statement, plus the homotopy clause via the previous theorem applied to $S\times[0,1]$; reference: Hatcher VBKT §1.2 "Clutching functions" (Prop 1.11 and the discussion after it), Atiyah *K-Theory* §1.4; spec: Targets: the degree of a line bundle over a surface (§3.6); the Chern number of an $SU(2)$-bundle over a 4-manifold (§3.6, VI); the mapping-torus "only if" clause: $E_\phi$ trivial iff $\phi\simeq\operatorname{id}$ through diffeomorphisms — stated and proved here as corollary for the structure-group version (record that for general fibre bundles the same proof works with $\operatorname{Diff}(F)$ as structure "group" — only the principal case is claimed).
- `Ex - The Tangent Bundle of S^2 is Nontrivial` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-X2.1.2, A-I2.1.1; prereqs: [Thm - Hairy Ball Theorem, Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality]; spec: a trivialisation gives a nowhere-vanishing field; contradiction.
- `Ex - The Frame Bundle of TS^2 Admits No Global Section` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-X2.2.1 (last clause); prereqs: [Thm - Hairy Ball Theorem, Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality]; spec: a section is a global frame, in particular a nowhere-vanishing field.
- `Ex - The Antipodal Map of S^n has Degree Minus One to the Power n plus 1` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: standard (Milnor TDV §6); prereqs: [Thm - The Brouwer Degree is an Integer and a Homotopy Invariant]; spec: a reflection has degree $-1$ (regular value count with orientation), composition multiplicativity.
- `Ex - A Map Extending over a Bounding Manifold has Degree Zero` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: standard (Milnor TDV §5 Lemma 1); prereqs: [Thm - The Brouwer Degree is an Integer and a Homotopy Invariant]; spec: Stokes proof of (f), and the application: $\deg$ of the boundary value of $a\colon X\setminus D^\circ\to U(1)$ or $\to SU(2)$ is zero (used in §3.6).
- `Ex - Degree of the Power Maps on the Circle and on SU(2)` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: standard; prereqs: [Thm - The Brouwer Degree is an Integer and a Homotopy Invariant, Ex - SU(2) is the Group of Unit Quaternions]; spec: $z\mapsto z^k$ on $S^1$ has degree $k$ (regular value count); $q\mapsto q^k$ on $S^3=Sp(1)$ has degree $k$ (route: show $q_0=i$ is a regular value whose preimage consists of the $k$ points $e^{((\pi/2+2\pi j)/k)\,i}$, $j=0,\dots,k-1$, on the circle $\{e^{\theta i}\}$, using the quaternion-exponential formula of chapter I's exercise to compute the Jacobian and its sign; alternatively via the additivity of $W(g)=\tfrac1{24\pi^2}\int\operatorname{tr}((g^{-1}dg)^3)$ proved in §3.6).
- `Ex - Smooth Curves Cannot Fill a Surface` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: easy-Sard drill; prereqs: [Thm - Sard's Theorem when the Source Dimension does not Exceed the Target Dimension]; spec: the image of a smooth map $\mathbb R\to\mathbb R^2$ has measure zero and empty interior; contrast with continuous Peano curves.
- `Ex - Moving Finitely Many Points into a Disc by an Isotopy` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: homogeneity drill; prereqs: [Thm - Homogeneity Lemma for Connected Manifolds]; spec: explicit construction on $T^2$ of a diffeomorphism isotopic to the identity carrying three given points into a given small disc, with the vector fields written down.
- `Ex - The Hopf Bundle is Nontrivial via the Winding Number of its Transition Function` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-T2.2.13 (meaning), B-T2.2.9 (alternative proof); prereqs: [Thm - Winding Number of a Map from the Circle to U(1), Thm - Clutching Construction for Bundles over a Closed Manifold, Def - The Hopf Bundle]; spec: the Hopf bundle is $P_g$ for $g=z/|z|$ restricted to the equator, $w(g)=1\ne0$, while the triviality criterion (d) forces $w=0$ (boundary values of maps from the disc or from the complementary disc have winding number zero).
- `Exercise Index - §3.5 Smooth-Topological Tools — Generic Sections, Degree, and Homotopy Invariance of Bundles` — lists: [Ex - The Tangent Bundle of S^2 is Nontrivial, Ex - The Frame Bundle of TS^2 Admits No Global Section, Ex - The Antipodal Map of S^n has Degree Minus One to the Power n plus 1, Ex - A Map Extending over a Bounding Manifold has Degree Zero, Ex - Degree of the Power Maps on the Circle and on SU(2), Ex - Smooth Curves Cannot Fill a Surface, Ex - Moving Finitely Many Points into a Disc by an Isotopy, Ex - The Hopf Bundle is Nontrivial via the Winding Number of its Transition Function]

### §3.6 Classification of U(1)- and SU(2)-Bundles
- `Def - Classifying Bundle and Classifying Map` — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.4.1, A-D2.4.2, A-D2.4.3, A-D2.4.6 (classifying-bundle part), A-R2.4.2, A-R2.4.4, A-I2.4.1, A-I2.4.2, A-I2.4.3, A-I3.1.5, A §2.4 conventions ($[M;B]$, topological category); prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - The Hopf Bundle, Thm - Homotopic Maps Pull Back Isomorphic Principal Bundles, Def - Homotopy, Def - Homotopy Equivalence and Contractible Space, Def - Characteristic Class]; spec: A classifying bundle for $G$ is a free $G$-space $E$ that is contractible, $B=E/G$; the direct-limit spaces $S^\infty=\varinjlim S^{2N+1}$ with its free $U(1)$-action and $\mathbb{CP}^\infty=\varinjlim\mathbb{CP}^N$ (topologised as direct limits), likewise $S^\infty\to\mathbb{HP}^\infty$ for $Sp(1)$; contractibility of $S^\infty$ proved on the page (the shift map $(z_0,z_1,\dots)\mapsto(0,z_0,z_1,\dots)$ is homotopic to the identity by the straight-line homotopy normalised, and to a constant — the classical two-step argument, written with the direct-limit topology made explicit; this discharges the contractibility half of I2.4.3); the set $[M;\mathbb{CP}^\infty]:=\varinjlim_N[M;\mathbb{CP}^N]$ for compact $M$ (Convention callout: the series defines it as a direct limit so that no theorem about compact subsets of direct limits is needed; for CW-theoretic readers this agrees with the usual set, a fact not used); the classifying map of a bundle and the induced characteristic classes $f^*c$ for $c\in H^\bullet_{dR}(B)$ (A-D2.4.2, in de Rham form; naturality proved; independence of the classifying map by homotopy invariance of de Rham cohomology, DG X). Scope remarks (bold, not theorems, not used): the existence of classifying bundles for every compact Lie group (Milnor's join construction) and the general classification theorem (I2.4.1, I2.4.2), the CW structure of $\mathbb{CP}^\infty$, $\mathbb{HP}^\infty$ (rest of I2.4.3), and the Grassmannian $\operatorname{Gr}_k(\mathbb C^\infty)$ as $BU(k)$ (I3.1.5); R2.4.4 (why characteristic classes rather than $[M;B]$) in Axiom Motivation; link AT III `Def - Characteristic Class` for the axiomatic notion. Calibration: the classifying map of a trivial bundle is null-homotopic; $[pt;\mathbb{CP}^\infty]$ is a point.
- `Thm - Line Bundles over Compact Manifolds are Pulled Back from Projective Space` — type: theorem; source items: A-I3.1.2, A-T2.4.1 (the cases $G=U(1)$ and $G=Sp(1)$; the general statement is a scope remark), A-I2.4.2 (same), A-R2.4.1; prereqs: [Def - Classifying Bundle and Classifying Map, Def - The Hopf Bundle, Thm - Vector Bundles are Associated to Their Frame Bundles, Thm - Euclidean and Hermitian Structures are Reductions of the Structure Group, Thm - Homotopic Maps Pull Back Isomorphic Principal Bundles, Thm - Existence of Smooth Partitions of Unity]; statement: Let $M$ be a compact manifold. (a) For every complex line bundle $L\to M$ there are $N$ and a smooth $f\colon M\to\mathbb{CP}^N$ with $f^*\mathcal O(-1)\cong L$; equivalently every principal $U(1)$-bundle is $f^*(S^{2N+1}\to\mathbb{CP}^N)$. (b) Two such maps $f\colon M\to\mathbb{CP}^N$, $f'\colon M\to\mathbb{CP}^{N'}$ classify isomorphic bundles iff they become homotopic in $\mathbb{CP}^{N+N'+1}$ (after the standard inclusions); hence $P\mapsto[f_P]$ is a bijection from isomorphism classes of principal $U(1)$-bundles over $M$ onto $[M;\mathbb{CP}^\infty]$. (c) The same holds for principal $Sp(1)$-bundles (equivalently quaternionic line bundles) with $\mathbb{HP}^N$ and $\mathbb{HP}^\infty$; proof: A: "not too hard to show", "cited"; write Hatcher VBKT Thm 1.16's argument for rank one: (a) choose finitely many sections $s_0,\dots,s_N$ of $L$ spanning each fibre (partition of unity) and a Hermitian structure; the map $m\mapsto$ the line $\{(\langle s_0(m),v\rangle,\dots,\langle s_N(m),v\rangle):v\in L_m\}\subset\mathbb C^{N+1}$ is smooth into $\mathbb{CP}^N$ (its lift to $S^{2N+1}$ is a unitary frame of $L$) and $f^*\mathcal O(-1)\cong L$; (b) "⇐" is the homotopy-invariance theorem; "⇒": two embeddings $L\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathbb C^{N+1}}$, $L\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathbb C^{N'+1}}$ are joined, after the "even/odd coordinate" trick, by a linear homotopy through injective bundle maps into $\underline{\mathbb C^{N+N'+2}}$, whose induced maps to projective space give the homotopy; (c) identical with $\mathbb H$ (right $\mathbb H$-lines; $Sp(1)$ acting by right multiplication; state the conventions); reference: Hatcher VBKT Thm 1.16 and Prop 1.17, Milnor–Stasheff Lemma 5.3 and Thm 5.6; spec: Convention callout: A states the theorem for every compact $G$ with a general classifying bundle; the series proves it only for $U(1)$ and $Sp(1)$ (and remarks that the identical argument gives $U(n)$ with Grassmannians), and uses nothing else. Targets: definition of the topological first Chern class; the classification pages.
- `Thm - The de Rham Cohomology of Complex Projective Space` — type: theorem; source items: A-I3.1.3, A-I2.4.6 (de Rham form; the integral statement is a scope remark), the form $\omega_N$; prereqs: [Def - Complex Projective Space as a Quotient, Thm - The Mayer-Vietoris Sequence, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of de Rham Cohomology, Thm - Bundle-Valued Forms on the Base are Basic Equivariant Forms on the Total Space, Def - The Hopf Bundle]; statement: (a) There is a unique closed 2-form $\omega_N\in\Omega^2(\mathbb{CP}^N)$ with $\pi^*\omega_N=\tfrac1\pi\sum_{j=0}^N dx_j\wedge dy_j\big|_{S^{2N+1}}$ ($z_j=x_j+iy_j$); it is real, closed, satisfies $\iota_{N}^*\omega_{N+1}=\omega_N$ for the standard inclusion, and $\int_{\mathbb{CP}^1}\omega_1=1$ (the writer must verify the normalising constant $1/\pi$ against A's Example 51, where $\int_{\mathbb{CP}^1}F_a=2\pi i$ with $\pi^*F_a=2i\sum dx_j\wedge dy_j$; adjust the constant if the orientation convention of $\mathbb{CP}^1$ — the complex orientation — requires). (b) $H^k_{dR}(\mathbb{CP}^N)\cong\mathbb R$ for $k$ even, $0\le k\le2N$, generated by $[\omega_N]^{k/2}$, and $0$ for $k$ odd; (c) the restriction $H^2_{dR}(\mathbb{CP}^N)\to H^2_{dR}(\mathbb{CP}^1)$ is an isomorphism, and a closed 2-form on $\mathbb{CP}^N$ is exact iff its integral over $\mathbb{CP}^1$ vanishes; proof: (a) the form $\sum dx_j\wedge dy_j$ restricted to the sphere is basic (its contraction with the generator $v(z)=iz$ is $-\tfrac12d|z|^2|_{S}=0$) and $U(1)$-invariant, so it descends by the basic-forms theorem with trivial representation; closedness from $d$ commuting with $\pi^*$ and injectivity of $\pi^*$; the integral over $\mathbb{CP}^1$ by the explicit local section of the Hopf bundle and Stokes (or by the computation of A's Example 51 adapted); (b) induction on $N$ by Mayer–Vietoris for $U=\mathbb{CP}^N\setminus\mathbb{CP}^{N-1}\cong\mathbb C^N$ (chapter I's cell decomposition) and $V=\mathbb{CP}^N\setminus\{[0:\dots:0:1]\}$, which deformation-retracts onto $\mathbb{CP}^{N-1}$ (the explicit retraction $[z:w]\mapsto[z:tw]$), with $U\cap V\simeq S^{2N-1}$; the cohomology of spheres from DG X's Mayer–Vietoris corollary; the generator statement by evaluating $\omega_N^{k/2}$ on $\mathbb{CP}^{k/2}$ (nonzero, hence the class is nonzero); (c) from (b); reference: Bott–Tu §5 (Ex.) / Madsen–Tornehave Ch. 14 / Lee ISM 2e Problem 17-?; spec: Convention callout: the integral generator $a$ of A (73) corresponds to $[\omega_N]$ under any de Rham–singular comparison, a comparison not made in this series; scope remark for $H^\bullet(\mathbb{HP}^\infty;\mathbb Z)$ (not computed; the quaternionic Chern number is defined by clutching instead). Targets: $c_1^{top}$ below; $H^2(\mathbb{CP}^2)$ for chapter XIII (group 3 may link).
- `Def - First Chern Class via the Classifying Map` — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.4.5, A-D2.4.4, A-D2.4.7 (statement and the series' replacement), A-R2.4.1, A-R2.4.3 (scope), A §2.4 conventions ($c_1(P)=-f^*a$, the minus sign is a convention); prereqs: [Thm - Line Bundles over Compact Manifolds are Pulled Back from Projective Space, Thm - The de Rham Cohomology of Complex Projective Space, Thm - Clutching Construction for Bundles over a Closed Manifold, Thm - Winding Number of a Map from the Circle to U(1)]; spec: For a principal $U(1)$-bundle $P$ over a compact $M$ (equivalently a Hermitian line bundle $L$, R2.4.1: every complex line bundle admits a Hermitian structure, unique up to homotopy, so $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ = isomorphism classes of complex line bundles = of principal $U(1)$-bundles — proved), $c_1^{top}(P):=-f^*[\omega_N]\in H^2_{dR}(M)$ for any classifying $f\colon M\to\mathbb{CP}^N$ (well defined by (b) of the classification theorem, homotopy invariance, and $\iota^*\omega_{N+1}=\omega_N$ — proved); the degree of a line bundle over a closed connected oriented surface $\Sigma$: $\deg(P):=w(g)$ for a clutching function $g\colon\partial D\to U(1)$ of $P$ (well defined by the clutching theorem: the ambiguity $a|_Sgb|_S$ changes $w$ by degrees of boundary values of maps extending over $\Sigma\setminus D^\circ$ or $D$, which vanish by the degree theorem (f) — proved; that every $P$ over $\Sigma$ admits a clutching description is proved on the classification page below); the topological second Chern number of an $Sp(1)$-bundle over a closed oriented 4-manifold is defined on the $SU(2)$ classification page as the clutching degree (A-D2.4.7's $-f^*b$ recorded as a scope remark: it needs $H^4(\mathbb{HP}^\infty;\mathbb Z)$). Convention callout: the sign $c_1(P):=-f^*a$ makes $c_1(\mathcal O(-1))=-[\omega]$ and agrees with $[\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F]$ (proved in VI). Verified examples: $c_1^{top}$ of the trivial bundle is $0$; $c_1^{top}(\mathcal O(-1))=-[\omega_N]\ne0$; $\deg$ of the Hopf bundle over $S^2$ is $\pm1$ (sign fixed by the orientation convention; the exercise computes it). Calibration: $c_1^{top}$ is natural; $\deg$ of the trivial bundle is $0$.
- `Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-E2.4.1, A-T2.4.2 (i),(iii),(iv) and the surface case of (ii), A-I2.4.7, A-X2.4.1 (statement; exercise), A-I2.4.4, A-I2.4.5 (replaced), A-R2.4.1; prereqs: [Def - First Chern Class via the Classifying Map, Thm - Generic Sections are Transverse to the Zero Section, Thm - Homogeneity Lemma for Connected Manifolds, Thm - Homotopic Maps Pull Back Isomorphic Principal Bundles, Thm - Clutching Construction for Bundles over a Closed Manifold, Thm - Winding Number of a Map from the Circle to U(1), Def - Constructions on Representations]; statement: Let $M$ be a compact manifold and $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ the set of isomorphism classes of principal $U(1)$-bundles (= Hermitian line bundles) over $M$. (A) $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ is an abelian group under tensor product of line bundles, with inverse the dual (= conjugate) bundle and identity the trivial bundle, and $P\mapsto[f_P]$ is a bijection $\operatorname{Pic}(M)\to[M;\mathbb{CP}^\infty]$. (B) $c_1^{top}\colon\operatorname{Pic}(M)\to H^2_{dR}(M)$ satisfies $c_1^{top}(\underline{\mathbb C})=0$, $c_1^{top}(L^\vee)=-c_1^{top}(L)$, $c_1^{top}(h^*L)=h^*c_1^{top}(L)$ for smooth $h\colon M'\to M$, and $c_1^{top}$ depends only on the isomorphism class. (C) If $M=\Sigma$ is a closed connected oriented surface, then $\deg\colon\operatorname{Pic}(\Sigma)\to\mathbb Z$ (the clutching winding number) is a group isomorphism: every $P$ over $\Sigma$ is trivial over the complement of a point and hence given by a clutching map; $\deg(P_1\otimes P_2)=\deg P_1+\deg P_2$; for every $d\in\mathbb Z$ there is a bundle of degree $d$; and $\deg P=0$ implies $P$ trivial. (D) [proved in chapter VI on `Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature`; stated here in bold as a forward reference, not as part of the theorem] $\deg P=\int_\Sigma c_1^{top}(P)$ and $c_1^{top}(L_1\otimes L_2)=c_1^{top}(L_1)+c_1^{top}(L_2)$ for all $M$. Scope remark (bold, not a theorem): the identification $\operatorname{Pic}(M)\cong H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$ via $[M;\mathbb{CP}^\infty]\cong H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$ requires singular cohomology and is neither proved nor used in this series; A's $H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$-valued $c_1$ is recovered only in the forms above; proof: (A) group axioms via associated bundles of $\varrho_k$ (chapter I) and the frame-bundle theorem; bijection = previous theorem; (B) from the definition (constant classifying map; conjugation on $\mathbb{CP}^N$ pulls $\omega_N$ back to $-\omega_N$ — verified in coordinates; $f_{h^*L}=f_L\circ h$; homotopy invariance); (C) Step 1: $L=P\times_{U(1)}\mathbb C$ is an oriented real rank-2 bundle over the 2-manifold $\Sigma$, so a generic section has finitely many nondegenerate zeros; Step 2: gather the zeros into a coordinate disc $D$ by the homogeneity lemma and the isotopy-invariance clause (d) of the homotopy theorem; Step 3: $L$ (hence $P$) is trivial over $\Sigma\setminus D^\circ$ (the nowhere-vanishing section normalised) and over $D$, so $P\cong P_g$; Step 4: additivity of $\deg$ from $w(g_1g_2)=w(g_1)+w(g_2)$ (tensor product of clutching bundles has clutching function the product — proved via cocycles); Step 5: existence for each $d$ by clutching with $z\mapsto z^d$; Step 6: $\deg P=0\Rightarrow w(g)=0\Rightarrow g$ extends over $D$ $\Rightarrow P_g$ trivial (clutching theorem (d)); Step 7: injectivity from additivity and Step 6; reference: Hatcher VBKT §1.2 (clutching over spheres) adapted to surfaces; Milnor TDV §4–5 for the isotopy tools; spec: Convention callout: A's route via $[\Sigma;\mathbb{CP}^\infty]=[\Sigma;\mathbb{CP}^1]=[\Sigma;S^2]\cong\mathbb Z$ (cellular approximation I2.4.4 and the Hopf degree theorem I2.4.5) is recorded as the classical route and replaced by the clutching proof; both give the same integer. Mechanism: "a line bundle over a surface is trivial off a disc, and a map from the boundary circle to $U(1)$ has just one invariant." Targets: degree of $\mathcal O(-1)$ is $-1$; the Chern–Weil identification (VI); flat $U(1)$-bundles over surfaces (V).
- `Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.4.3, A-D2.4.6 ($Sp(1)$ part), A-D2.4.7 (replacement), A-R3.1.6 (the integer $k(P)$; its Chern–Weil identification is VI), A-I2.4.4 (replaced); prereqs: [Ex - SU(2) is the Group of Unit Quaternions, Thm - Generic Sections are Transverse to the Zero Section, Thm - Homogeneity Lemma for Connected Manifolds, Thm - Homotopic Maps Pull Back Isomorphic Principal Bundles, Thm - Clutching Construction for Bundles over a Closed Manifold, Thm - The Brouwer Degree is an Integer and a Homotopy Invariant, Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality, Thm - Vector Bundles are Associated to Their Frame Bundles]; statement: (A) Every principal $SU(2)$-bundle over a compact manifold of dimension $\le3$ is trivial. (B) Let $X$ be a closed connected oriented 4-manifold and $P\to X$ a principal $SU(2)$-bundle. Then $P$ is trivial over $X\setminus\{x_0\}$ for every $x_0$, hence $P\cong P_g$ for a clutching map $g\colon S^3\to SU(2)$ on the boundary of a coordinate disc $D\ni x_0$; the integer $k(P):=\deg g$ (the Chern number of $P$) is independent of all choices and is an isomorphism invariant; and $W(g):=\tfrac1{24\pi^2}\int_{S^3}\operatorname{tr}\big((g^{-1}dg)^{\wedge3}\big)=\deg g$ (sign to be fixed by the writer's orientation computation) for every smooth $g\colon S^3\to SU(2)$. (C) For every $k\in\mathbb Z$ there is a principal $SU(2)$-bundle over $X$ with $k(P)=k$, e.g. the clutching bundle of $q\mapsto q^k$ (unit quaternions), and $k(P_{g_1g_2})=k(P_{g_1})+k(P_{g_2})$. Scope remark (bold): that $k$ is a complete invariant (bundles with equal Chern number are isomorphic) requires $\pi_3(S^3)\cong\mathbb Z$ (the Hopf degree theorem in dimension 3) and is not proved in this series; nothing in I–VI or the later chapters' stated uses needs it beyond existence and invariance; proof: (A) $E:=P\times_{SU(2)}\mathbb C^2$ is a real rank-4 bundle; a nowhere-vanishing section exists by the generic-sections theorem (rank $4>\dim M$); normalise it by the Hermitian structure to a unit section $u$; since $SU(2)$ acts simply transitively on the unit sphere of $\mathbb C^2$, $P\cong$ the unit sphere bundle $S(E)$ as $SU(2)$-spaces (Lemma: the map $P\to S(E)$, $p\mapsto[p,e_1]$, is an equivariant diffeomorphism), so $u$ is a section of $P$ and $P$ is trivial; (B) a generic section of $E$ over $X^4$ has finitely many zeros, gathered into $D$ by the homogeneity lemma, so $P$ is trivial over $X\setminus D^\circ$ and over $D$ (contractible); the clutching theorem gives $P\cong P_g$; invariance of $\deg g$: the ambiguity $g\mapsto(a|_S)g(b|_S)$ changes the degree by $\deg(a|_S)+\deg(b|_S)=0$ (degree theorem (f) and additivity — Lemma A below); Lemma A (additivity of $W$ under pointwise products): with $\theta_i=g_i^{-1}dg_i$ and $\tilde\theta_1=g_2^{-1}\theta_1g_2$, $(g_1g_2)^{-1}d(g_1g_2)=\tilde\theta_1+\theta_2$ and $\operatorname{tr}((\tilde\theta_1+\theta_2)^3)=\operatorname{tr}(\theta_1^3)+\operatorname{tr}(\theta_2^3)-3\,d\operatorname{tr}(\tilde\theta_1\wedge\theta_2)$ (a direct computation using $d\theta=-\theta\wedge\theta$ and cyclicity of the trace; every sign displayed), so $W(g_1g_2)=W(g_1)+W(g_2)$ by Stokes; Lemma B ($W(g)=\deg g$): $\operatorname{tr}(\theta^3)$ is a bi-invariant 3-form on $SU(2)$, hence a constant multiple of the volume form of $S^3$; the constant is computed at the identity ($\operatorname{tr}(\theta^3)(X,Y,Z)=3\operatorname{tr}(X[Y,Z])$ on the basis $-i\sigma_a$) giving $\int_{SU(2)}\operatorname{tr}(\theta^3)=\pm24\pi^2$ (with $\operatorname{Vol}(S^3)=2\pi^2$ from DG IX's sphere-volume exercise); then $W(g)=\deg g$ by the degree theorem; (C) $\deg(q\mapsto q^k)=k$ (by Lemma A and $W(\operatorname{id})=1$, or by the §3.5 exercise) and the tensor/product rule for clutching functions; reference: Naber, *Topology, Geometry and Gauge Fields: Foundations* §6.? (clutching of $SU(2)$-bundles over $S^4$), Nakahara §10.5 (the $24\pi^2$ computation), Hatcher VBKT §1.2; spec: Convention callout: A proves (A) via the classifying space and cellular approximation (I2.4.4); the series' proof is the generic-section argument and needs compactness (A states the result for all manifolds of dimension $\le3$; the non-compact case is a scope remark). Targets: chapter VI proves $k(P)=\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\int_X\operatorname{tr}(F_A\wedge F_A)$, i.e. $c_2(P)[X]=k(P)$, and uses (A) to define the Chern–Simons functional; chapter VII/XIII use existence of the bundle with $k=1$.
- `Ex - The First Chern Class does not Depend on the Hermitian Structure` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-X2.4.1; prereqs: [Def - First Chern Class via the Classifying Map, Thm - Homotopic Maps Pull Back Isomorphic Principal Bundles]; spec: two Hermitian structures are joined by the convex path $h_t$; the $U(1)$-reductions form a bundle over $M\times[0,1]$ whose ends are the two reductions; conclude isomorphic, hence equal $c_1^{top}$.
- `Ex - Line Bundles of Every Degree on a Closed Oriented Surface` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-E2.4.1 (existence part), B-D2.4.4; prereqs: [Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class]; spec: construct $P_{z^d}$ explicitly on the torus and on $S^2$; show $P_{z^d}\cong P_{z}^{\otimes d}$.
- `Ex - The Tautological Bundle over CP^1 has Degree Minus One` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T3.1.2(iv) (topological half), A-D2.4.5 sign convention; prereqs: [Def - First Chern Class via the Classifying Map, Def - The Hopf Bundle, Thm - Winding Number of a Map from the Circle to U(1)]; spec: with the complex orientation of $\mathbb{CP}^1$ and B's sections $s_1,s_2$, compute the clutching function of $\mathcal O(-1)$ on the equator as $z/|z|$ or its inverse depending on the ordering of the two discs, and pin the sign so that $\deg\mathcal O(-1)=-1$, consistent with $c_1(\mathcal O(-1))=-[\omega_1]$; the writer must state explicitly which disc is "$D$" in the clutching convention.
- `Ex - SU(2)-Bundles over S^4 of Every Chern Number` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-D2.4.6, A-R2.4.2 (the bundle $S^7\to\mathbb{HP}^1=S^4$); prereqs: [Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds]; spec: the quaternionic Hopf bundle $S^7\to S^4$ is $P_g$ with $g(q)=q$ (or $\bar q$), so $k=\pm1$; $P_{q^k}$ realises every $k$; identify $\mathbb{HP}^1\cong S^4$ by the quaternionic stereographic map (analogue of chapter I's $\mathbb{CP}^1\cong S^2$, written out).
- `Ex - Every SU(2)-Bundle over the 3-Torus is Trivial` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T2.4.3 drill; prereqs: [Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds]; spec: run the generic-section proof concretely on $T^3$ and also give the cocycle-free argument that $P\cong S(E)$.
- `Ex - S^infinity is Contractible` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-I2.4.3 (contractibility half); prereqs: [Def - Classifying Bundle and Classifying Map]; spec: write the two homotopies (shift, then straight line to $(1,0,0,\dots)$) and verify continuity in the direct-limit topology.
- `Exercise Index - §3.6 Classification of U(1)- and SU(2)-Bundles` — lists: [Ex - The First Chern Class does not Depend on the Hermitian Structure, Ex - Line Bundles of Every Degree on a Closed Oriented Surface, Ex - The Tautological Bundle over CP^1 has Degree Minus One, Ex - SU(2)-Bundles over S^4 of Every Chern Number, Ex - Every SU(2)-Bundle over the 3-Torus is Trivial, Ex - S^infinity is Contractible]
