### §4.1 Lie-Algebra-Valued Forms and the Maurer-Cartan Form
- `Def - Lie-Algebra-Valued Differential Forms and Their Bracket` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D2.4.2, A §2.1/§2.2 conventions ($[A\wedge A]$, $A\wedge A$ for matrix groups), A-D2.2.14 (the action of $\mathfrak g$-valued forms on $V$-valued forms through $\rho_*$), conventions.md bracket paragraph; prereqs: [Def - Bundle-Valued Differential Forms, Def - Lie Algebra, Def - Representation of a Lie Algebra]; spec: $\Omega^p(M;\mathfrak g)=\Omega^p(M)\otimes\mathfrak g$; the bracket $[\alpha\wedge\beta]$ defined by $[\alpha\otimes\xi\wedge\beta\otimes\eta]:=(\alpha\wedge\beta)\otimes[\xi,\eta]$ and shown to agree, for 1-forms, with B's $[\eta,\varphi](X,Y)=[\eta(X),\varphi(Y)]-[\eta(Y),\varphi(X)]$, so $[\omega\wedge\omega](X,Y)=2[\omega(X),\omega(Y)]$; for matrix Lie algebras $[A\wedge A]=2A\wedge A$ (proved) — Convention callout: this is why A's (18) has no $\tfrac12$ and (20) has one; the wedge-action $\rho_*(\alpha)\wedge\phi$ of $\mathfrak g$-valued forms on $V$-valued forms and the notation $a\cdot\hat s$ (A (45)); $d$ on $\mathfrak g$-valued forms componentwise. Verified examples: for abelian $\mathfrak g$ all brackets vanish; $[A\wedge A]$ for $A=X\,dx+Y\,dy$ equals $2[X,Y]dx\wedge dy$; non-example: $[\alpha\wedge\beta]$ is not antisymmetric in the naive sense (see the theorem below). Calibration: $[\alpha\wedge\alpha]=0$ for an even-degree $\alpha$; $\operatorname{tr}[A\wedge B]=0$ for matrix-valued 1-forms.
- `Thm - Graded Antisymmetry and Jacobi Identity for Lie-Algebra-Valued Forms` — type: theorem; source items: A-I2.2.5, A-T2.2.8 (the identities it needs), B-T2.4.1 (the identity $[\omega,\omega](X,Y)=2[\omega(X),\omega(Y)]$); prereqs: [Def - Lie-Algebra-Valued Differential Forms and Their Bracket, Thm - Wedge Product Properties]; statement: For $\alpha\in\Omega^p(M;\mathfrak g)$, $\beta\in\Omega^q(M;\mathfrak g)$, $\gamma\in\Omega^r(M;\mathfrak g)$: (a) $[\alpha\wedge\beta]=-(-1)^{pq}[\beta\wedge\alpha]$; (b) $d[\alpha\wedge\beta]=[d\alpha\wedge\beta]+(-1)^p[\alpha\wedge d\beta]$; (c) $(-1)^{pr}[\alpha\wedge[\beta\wedge\gamma]]+(-1)^{pq}[\beta\wedge[\gamma\wedge\alpha]]+(-1)^{qr}[\gamma\wedge[\alpha\wedge\beta]]=0$; in particular for a 1-form $A$, $[A\wedge[A\wedge A]]=0$; (d) for matrix-valued forms $[\alpha\wedge\beta]=\alpha\wedge\beta-(-1)^{pq}\beta\wedge\alpha$; proof: source asserts (a) for $p=2,q=1$ and the Jacobi consequence; write all four by reduction to decomposable forms $\alpha=a\otimes\xi$ and the DG VIII wedge rules, with every sign displayed; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. II §5 (p. 36) / Baum §3.3; spec: Targets: the Bianchi identity (§4.3), the Chern–Weil closedness argument (VI).
- `Def - The Maurer-Cartan Form` **[anchor]** — type: definition; source items: B-R2.5.3 ($\phi_g=dL_{g^{-1}}$), A §2.2 conventions ($g^{-1}dg$ for matrix groups, $g^*\theta$ in the transformation law); prereqs: [Def - Left and Right Translations and Conjugation on a Lie Group, Def - Lie-Algebra-Valued Differential Forms and Their Bracket]; spec: $\theta\in\Omega^1(G;\mathfrak g)$, $\theta_g:=d_gL_{g^{-1}}\colon T_gG\to T_eG=\mathfrak g$; left-invariance $L_h^*\theta=\theta$ and $R_h^*\theta=\operatorname{Ad}_{h^{-1}}\theta$ (proved); $\theta(\tilde\xi)=\xi$ for the left-invariant field $\tilde\xi$; for matrix groups $\theta=g^{-1}dg$ (proved); the pull-back $g^*\theta$ for a map $g\colon U\to G$ and its product rule $(g_1g_2)^*\theta=\operatorname{Ad}_{g_2^{-1}}(g_1^*\theta)+g_2^*\theta$ (proved as corollary; it is the identity behind every local transformation law). Verified examples: $U(1)$: $\theta=z^{-1}dz=i\,d\varphi$; $SU(2)$ in quaternion coordinates. Non-example: $dg\,g^{-1}$ is the right-invariant form, not $\theta$. Calibration: $\theta$ on $\mathbb R^n$ is $dx$; $\theta$ evaluated on a curve $g(t)$ is $g^{-1}\dot g$.
- `Thm - The Maurer-Cartan Equation` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.5.6 (the computation $d\phi+\tfrac12[\phi,\phi]=0$, with B's sign slip corrected), B-R2.5.3; prereqs: [Def - The Maurer-Cartan Form, Thm - The Space of Vector Fields is a Lie Algebra under the Lie Bracket, Thm - Coordinate Expression for the Exterior Derivative]; statement: $d\theta+\tfrac12[\theta\wedge\theta]=0$; for matrix groups $d(g^{-1}dg)=-g^{-1}dg\wedge g^{-1}dg$; consequently for any smooth $g\colon U\to G$, $d(g^*\theta)+\tfrac12[g^*\theta\wedge g^*\theta]=0$; proof: evaluate on left-invariant fields $\tilde\xi,\tilde\eta$ using $d\theta(X,Y)=X\theta(Y)-Y\theta(X)-\theta([X,Y])$ (the invariant formula for $d$ on 1-forms, proved on the page from DG VIII's coordinate expression), $\theta(\tilde\xi)=\xi$ constant, and $[\tilde\xi,\tilde\eta]=\widetilde{[\xi,\eta]}$ (DG XI); B prints $d\phi(X,Y)=+[X,Y]$; the correct value is $-[X,Y]$; the matrix case by direct differentiation; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. I §4, Prop 4.1? / Sharpe Ch. 3; spec: Targets: the product connection on $M\times G$ is flat (VI), the local structure equation for the pure-gauge potential $g^{-1}dg$.
- `Ex - The Bracket of Matrix-Valued 1-Forms is Twice the Wedge Square` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-R2.1.8 (20) vs (18); prereqs: [Def - Lie-Algebra-Valued Differential Forms and Their Bracket]; spec: prove $[A\wedge A]=2A\wedge A$ and $[A\wedge B]=A\wedge B+B\wedge A$ for matrix-valued 1-forms; compute both sides for $A=\sigma_1dx+\sigma_2dy$.
- `Ex - The Maurer-Cartan Form of U(1) and of SU(2)` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-R2.5.3 drill; prereqs: [Def - The Maurer-Cartan Form]; spec: compute $\theta$ on $U(1)$ and on $SU(2)$ in the quaternion coordinates of chapter I, verify the Maurer–Cartan equation directly, and identify $\theta$ with the dual basis of left-invariant fields.
- `Ex - The Pull-Back of the Maurer-Cartan Form along a Product of Maps` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: none (identity used by B-T2.3.6 and A-T2.2.5 Step 3); prereqs: [Def - The Maurer-Cartan Form]; spec: prove $(g_1g_2)^*\theta=\operatorname{Ad}_{g_2^{-1}}(g_1^*\theta)+g_2^*\theta$ and $(g^{-1})^*\theta=-\operatorname{Ad}_g(g^*\theta)$; for matrix groups verify with $g^{-1}dg$.
- `Exercise Index - §4.1 Lie-Algebra-Valued Forms and the Maurer-Cartan Form` — lists: [Ex - The Bracket of Matrix-Valued 1-Forms is Twice the Wedge Square, Ex - The Maurer-Cartan Form of U(1) and of SU(2), Ex - The Pull-Back of the Maurer-Cartan Form along a Product of Maps]

### §4.2 Connections on Principal Bundles
- `Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle` **[anchor]** — type: definition; source items: A-D2.2.13, B-D2.3.1, B-R2.3.1, B-T2.3.1, B-D2.3.4, A-R2.2.6, A-D2.2.12 (link `Def - Adjoint Bundles ad P and Ad P`); prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action, Thm - The Fundamental Vector Field Map is a Lie Algebra Homomorphism for Right Actions, Def - Lie-Algebra-Valued Differential Forms and Their Bracket, Def - Equivariant and Basic Forms on a Principal Bundle]; spec: A connection form is $\omega\in\Omega^1(P;\mathfrak g)$ with (1) $R_g^*\omega=\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\omega$ and (2) $\omega(\xi_P)=\xi$; the space $\mathcal A(P)$ ($=\mathcal C(P)$ of B) is not a vector space ($0$ fails (2)); corollary proved on the page (B-T2.3.1): (1) and (2) are compatible because $dR_g(\xi_P(p))=(\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\xi)_P(pg)$ (chapter I theorem, restated), and replacing $\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}$ by $\operatorname{Ad}_g$ would be inconsistent (verified). Axiom Motivation from A-R2.2.6 (relate to vector-bundle connections) and B's figure (vertical directions are already coordinatised by $\mathfrak g$; a connection is a compatible choice of "horizontal"). Per-clause failure: drop (2) — $\omega=0$; drop (1) — a non-equivariant complement. Verified examples: the product connection $\operatorname{pr}_2^*\theta$ on $M\times G$ (both clauses checked, using $R_h^*\theta=\operatorname{Ad}_{h^{-1}}\theta$); the Hopf connection (next theorem page); non-example: $\operatorname{pr}_2^*\theta+\pi^*\alpha$ with $\alpha\in\Omega^1(M;\mathfrak g)$ is a connection iff $\alpha$ is $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariant pointwise (e.g. always for abelian $G$; for $SU(2)$ only $\alpha=0$) — verified. Convention callout: B misprints "$R_g^*=\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\circ\omega$"; A writes $a$ for the connection form and $\operatorname{ad}$ for $\operatorname{Ad}$. Categorical: a $G$-equivariant splitting of $0\to VP\to TP\to\pi^*TM\to0$. Calibration: $\omega$ restricted to a fibre is the Maurer–Cartan form under $\ell_p$; the difference of two connections vanishes on vertical vectors.
- `Def - Horizontal Subspace and Horizontal Lift` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D2.3.2, B-T2.3.4; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle]; spec: $H_p:=\ker\omega_p$; corollaries proved on the page (B-T2.3.4): $\omega_p|_{V_p}\colon V_p\to\mathfrak g$ is an isomorphism, $T_pP=H_p\oplus V_p$, $\dim H_p=\dim M$, $dR_g(H_p)=H_{pg}$, $d\pi_p|_{H_p}\colon H_p\to T_{\pi(p)}M$ is an isomorphism; the horizontal lift $\tilde v\in H_p$ of $v\in T_{\pi(p)}M$ and of a vector field ($\tilde X$ is $G$-invariant, $\pi$-related to $X$, and $\widetilde{fX}=(f\circ\pi)\tilde X$ — proved); the horizontal projection $\pi_H$; the horizontal lift of a curve (definition only; existence is V). Verified examples: on $M\times G$ with the product connection, $H_{(m,g)}=T_mM\times0$; non-example: the distribution $H$ of the Hopf connection is not integrable (exercise). Calibration: $\tilde X$ of a coordinate field in a local trivialisation; $[\tilde X,\tilde Y]-\widetilde{[X,Y]}$ is vertical (proved; it foreshadows curvature).
- `Thm - Connection Forms Correspond to Invariant Horizontal Distributions` — type: theorem; source items: conventions.md ("equivalently a $G$-invariant horizontal distribution $H=\ker\omega$"), B-T2.3.4 (converse direction); prereqs: [Def - Horizontal Subspace and Horizontal Lift, Def - Distribution on a Manifold]; statement: The map $\omega\mapsto H=\ker\omega$ is a bijection between connection forms on $P$ and smooth distributions $H\subset TP$ with $T_pP=H_p\oplus V_p$ and $dR_g(H_p)=H_{pg}$ for all $p,g$; the inverse sends $H$ to the form $\omega_H$ that vanishes on $H$ and equals the inverse of $\xi\mapsto\xi_P(p)$ on $V_p$; proof: both directions written, including smoothness of $\omega_H$ (local frames of $H$ and of $V$); reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Prop II.1.1; spec: Mechanism: "a connection is a choice of horizontal complement compatible with the group; the form is its projection to the vertical part read off in $\mathfrak g$."
- `Thm - Existence of Connections on Principal Bundles` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.2.4, A-R2.2.5, B-T2.3.8, B-T2.3.9, B-I2.3.1, B-D2.3.4; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle, Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality, Thm - Existence of Smooth Partitions of Unity, Thm - Bundle-Valued Forms on the Base are Basic Equivariant Forms on the Total Space, Def - Adjoint Bundles ad P and Ad P, Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space]; statement: For every principal $G$-bundle $P\to M$: (a) $\mathcal A(P)\ne\varnothing$; (b) for $\omega,\omega'\in\mathcal A(P)$, $\omega-\omega'$ is basic and $\operatorname{Ad}$-equivariant, hence corresponds to a unique $b\in\Omega^1(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$; (c) for $\omega\in\mathcal A(P)$ and $b\in\Omega^1(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$ with lift $\hat b$, $\omega+\hat b$ is a connection; hence $\mathcal A(P)$ is an affine space modelled on $\Omega^1(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$; (d) locally, $\omega_\beta-\omega'_\beta=\operatorname{Ad}_{g_{\alpha\beta}^{-1}}(\omega_\alpha-\omega'_\alpha)$ for the local forms; proof: A sketch, B sketch; write: (a) on a trivialising $U_\alpha$ with section $s_\alpha$ the form $\omega_\alpha$ defined by $\omega_\alpha(dR_g\,ds_\alpha(v)+\xi_P)=\xi$ ... i.e. transport the product connection $\operatorname{pr}_2^*\theta$ through the trivialisation (verify both clauses); glue $\omega:=\sum_\alpha(\rho_\alpha\circ\pi)\omega_\alpha$ and verify (1),(2) using $\sum\rho_\alpha=1$ and that (1),(2) are affine conditions; (b) the difference vanishes on $\xi_P$ (both take the value $\xi$) and is equivariant, so the basic-forms theorem applies; (c) direct check; (d) from (b) and the transformation of local sections; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Thm II.2.1; spec: Convention callout: A says "$G$-invariant" for "$\operatorname{Ad}$-equivariant". Targets: gauge orbits in an affine space (V), the Chern–Weil homotopy argument (VI).
- `Def - Local Connection Form and Gauge Potential` **[anchor]** — type: definition; source items: A-D2.2.16, B-D2.3.3, B-R2.3.2, B-D2.2.9 (gauge); prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle, Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality, Def - Pullback of a Differential Form on a Manifold]; spec: $A_s:=s^*\omega\in\Omega^1(U;\mathfrak g)$ for a local section $s$ (= local gauge); the physicists' gauge potential $A=A_\mu dx^\mu$; for the frame bundle and a local frame $e$, $e^*\omega$ is the connection matrix $A(\nabla,e)$ of II (this identification is the theorem in §4.4; here stated with a forward link); reconstruction of $\omega$ over $U$ from $A_s$: $\omega_{s(x)g}(dR_g\,ds(v)+\xi_P)=\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}A_s(v)+\xi$ (proved: this is B-T2.3.7's uniqueness half, written as a corollary). Verified examples: product connection has $A_s=0$ for the identity section and $A_{s\cdot g}=g^*\theta$ (pure gauge); the Hopf connection in B's two sections (exercise). Convention callout: A switches between $a$ and $A$; B writes $\omega_\alpha$. Calibration: $A_s$ is $\mathfrak g$-valued, not $\operatorname{ad}P$-valued; the family $(A_{s_\alpha})$ is not a global form.
- `Thm - Transformation of Local Connection and Curvature Forms` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.3.6, B-T2.3.7, B-R2.3.2, B-T2.4.6 (b),(c), B-D2.4.3, B-D2.4.4 (statement; definition on the curvature page), A-D2.2.16 (local curvature), A-T2.2.5 Step 3 (the same computation for frame bundles), RG I link for the matrix case; prereqs: [Def - Local Connection Form and Gauge Potential, Def - The Maurer-Cartan Form, Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition, Thm - Pull-Back Commutes with the Exterior Derivative, Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection]; statement: Let $s_\alpha,s_\beta$ be local sections with $s_\beta=s_\alpha g_{\alpha\beta}$. Then (a) $A_\beta=\operatorname{Ad}_{g_{\alpha\beta}^{-1}}A_\alpha+g_{\alpha\beta}^*\theta$, for matrix groups $A_\beta=g_{\alpha\beta}^{-1}A_\alpha g_{\alpha\beta}+g_{\alpha\beta}^{-1}dg_{\alpha\beta}$; (b) conversely a family $(A_\alpha)$ satisfying (a) on all overlaps is the family of local forms of a unique connection; (c) the local curvature forms $F_\alpha:=s_\alpha^*\Omega=dA_\alpha+\tfrac12[A_\alpha\wedge A_\alpha]$ satisfy $F_\beta=\operatorname{Ad}_{g_{\alpha\beta}^{-1}}F_\alpha$ (no inhomogeneous term), so $[s_\alpha,F_\alpha]$ glue to a global $F\in\Omega^2(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$ equal to the descended curvature; for abelian $G$, $F_\beta=F_\alpha$ and $F\in\Omega^2(M;\mathfrak g)$; proof: B full for (a) and (c), sketch for (b) — write: (a) differentiate $s_\beta(u)=s_\alpha(u)g_{\alpha\beta}(u_0)\cdot(g_{\alpha\beta}(u_0)^{-1}g_{\alpha\beta}(u))$ via the differential of the action map (chapter I / §4.4 Lemma $d\hat R_{(p,g)}(v,w)=dR_g(v)+(\theta_g(w))_P(pg)$, proved on this page as Lemma 1); (b) define $\omega$ on each $\pi^{-1}(U_\alpha)$ by the reconstruction formula and check the definitions agree on overlaps using (a) and the Maurer–Cartan product rule; (c) apply $\Omega$ to the same decomposition, the vertical term is killed (curvature is horizontal), and use $R_g^*\Omega=\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\Omega$; the identification with the descended form via the basic-forms theorem; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Prop II.1.4 and II.5.2; Bär's (2.2)/(2.3)/(2.7); spec: Bridges: RG I `Thm - Gauge Transformation Law for Connection 1-Forms` is the $GL_k$ frame-bundle case. Mechanism: "moving the section along the fibre by $g$ costs $g^*\theta$ (the pure-gauge term) and conjugates by $g$; the curvature has no pure-gauge term because it is horizontal."
- `Thm - The Standard Connection on the Hopf Bundle` — type: theorem; source items: A-E2.2.3 (connection part), B-T2.3.5, B-E2.3.3; prereqs: [Def - The Hopf Bundle, Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle, Def - Horizontal Subspace and Horizontal Lift]; statement: On $S^{2n+1}\to\mathbb{CP}^n$ with $\mathfrak u(1)=i\mathbb R$ and $v(z)=iz$ the fundamental field of $i$, the form $a_z(u):=\langle v(z),u\rangle\,i$ (real inner product of $\mathbb R^{2n+2}$) is a connection form; it is the unique connection with $\ker a=v^\perp$; proof: both sources full; write: equivariance = invariance because $U(1)$ is abelian and acts by isometries; $a(v)=|v|^2i=i$; uniqueness from the horizontal-distribution theorem; B's remark that his two checks are printed in swapped order; spec: Targets: the curvature computation (exercise in §4.3), $\mathcal O(-1)$'s connection (§4.4), the Chern number (VI).
- `Ex - The Product Connection on a Trivial Bundle and Pure Gauge Potentials` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-T2.5.6 (connection part), A-D2.2.16 drill; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle, Def - Local Connection Form and Gauge Potential]; spec: verify $\operatorname{pr}_2^*\theta$ is a connection on $M\times G$; compute $A_s=g^*\theta$ for the section $s=(\operatorname{id},g)$; show every connection on $M\times G$ is $\operatorname{pr}_2^*\theta+$ (lift of an element of $\Omega^1(M;\mathfrak g)$).
- `Ex - Connection Forms of the Hopf Connection in the Two Local Sections` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-T2.3.5, B-T2.2.13, B-T2.3.6 drill; prereqs: [Thm - The Standard Connection on the Hopf Bundle, Thm - Transformation of Local Connection and Curvature Forms]; spec: compute $A_1=s_1^*a$ and $A_2=s_2^*a$ in B's coordinates $(z,t)$ on $S^2$, and verify $A_2=A_1+g_{12}^{-1}dg_{12}=A_1+i\,d\arg z$.
- `Ex - Christoffel Symbols are the Local Connection Form of the Frame Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-T2.3.3, B-E2.3.2; prereqs: [Def - Local Connection Form and Gauge Potential, Thm - A Covariant Derivative Determines a Connection on the Frame Bundle]; spec: for $s=(\partial_1,\dots,\partial_n)$ show $(s^*\omega(\partial_k))^j{}_i=\Gamma^j_{ik}$; note this exercise uses the §4.4 theorem and is listed in §4.2 for thematic reasons.
- `Ex - The Horizontal Distribution of the Hopf Connection is Not Integrable` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-T2.4.7 (foreshadowed), A-E2.2.4; prereqs: [Thm - The Standard Connection on the Hopf Bundle, Thm - The Frobenius Theorem]; spec: exhibit horizontal fields $v_2,v_3$ on $S^3$ with $[v_2,v_3]=-2v_1$ vertical (A's frame); conclude non-involutive; interpret as nonzero curvature (forward).
- `Exercise Index - §4.2 Connections on Principal Bundles` — lists: [Ex - The Product Connection on a Trivial Bundle and Pure Gauge Potentials, Ex - Connection Forms of the Hopf Connection in the Two Local Sections, Ex - Christoffel Symbols are the Local Connection Form of the Frame Bundle, Ex - The Horizontal Distribution of the Hopf Connection is Not Integrable]

### §4.3 Curvature and the Structure Equation
- `Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D2.2.15, A-R2.2.7, B-D2.4.1, B-D2.4.3, B-D2.4.4, B-R2.4.1, B-R2.4.2; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle, Def - Horizontal Subspace and Horizontal Lift, Thm - Bundle-Valued Forms on the Base are Basic Equivariant Forms on the Total Space, Def - Adjoint Bundles ad P and Ad P]; spec: Primary definition (B): $\Omega(X,Y):=d\omega(\pi_HX,\pi_HY)$, a $\mathfrak g$-valued 2-form on $P$; secondary description (A): $d\omega+\tfrac12[\omega\wedge\omega]$ — the equality of the two is the structure equation theorem, and the page states it with a forward link; corollaries proved on the page: $\Omega$ is horizontal (by definition) and, granting the structure equation, A-R2.2.7's computation that $d\omega+\tfrac12[\omega\wedge\omega]$ is basic (Cartan's formula, $\mathcal L_{\xi_P}\omega=-[\xi,\omega]$ from equivariance) and equivariant; hence there is a unique $F_\omega\in\Omega^2(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$ with $\pi^*F_\omega=\Omega$ (through the basic-forms theorem), which is B's $\bar\Omega$; local curvature forms $F_\alpha=s_\alpha^*\Omega$; abelian case $F\in\Omega^2(M;\mathfrak g)$. Convention callout: A calls both $\Omega$ ("$\hat F_A$") and $F$ the curvature; B writes $\Omega$, $\Omega_\alpha$, $\bar\Omega$; the series writes $\Omega\in\Omega^2(P;\mathfrak g)$, $F_\omega\in\Omega^2(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$, $F_\alpha$ locally. Verified examples: product connection has $\Omega=0$ (via the Maurer–Cartan equation); the Hopf connection has $\Omega=da\ne0$ (exercise); non-example: $d\omega$ alone is not horizontal for non-abelian $G$. True name: "the vertical part of the bracket of horizontal lifts": $\Omega(\tilde X,\tilde Y)=-\omega([\tilde X,\tilde Y])$ (proved as corollary from the invariant formula for $d$). Calibration: $\Omega$ vanishes if either argument is vertical; $F_\omega$ is a genuine 2-form on $M$ with values in a vector bundle, not in $\mathfrak g$, unless $G$ is abelian.
- `Thm - Structure Equation for the Curvature` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.4.1, B-T2.4.2, B-T2.4.3, B-T2.4.6(a), B-I2.4.1, B-I2.4.2, A-D2.2.15 (the identity), A-D2.2.16 ($F_a=dA+\tfrac12[A\wedge A]$ locally), A-R2.2.7; prereqs: [Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Thm - Graded Antisymmetry and Jacobi Identity for Lie-Algebra-Valued Forms, Thm - The Fundamental Vector Field Map is a Lie Algebra Homomorphism for Right Actions, Thm - Cartan's Magic Formula, Thm - Coordinate Expression for the Exterior Derivative]; statement: (a) $\Omega=d\omega+\tfrac12[\omega\wedge\omega]$; (b) for $\xi\in\mathfrak g$ and a horizontal vector field $Y$, $[\xi_P,Y]$ is horizontal; (c) $R_g^*\Omega=\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\Omega$; (d) locally $F_\alpha=dA_\alpha+\tfrac12[A_\alpha\wedge A_\alpha]$, and for matrix groups $F_\alpha=dA_\alpha+A_\alpha\wedge A_\alpha$; (e) $d\omega+\tfrac12[\omega\wedge\omega]$ is basic and equivariant (A-R2.2.7); proof: B full in three cases with the silent identity $\omega([\bar X',\bar Y'])=[X',Y']$, which is chapter I's homomorphism theorem (restated); (b) via Cartan's formula and the flow $R_{\exp t\xi}$ of $\xi_P$ (chapter I); (c) $\pi_H$ commutes with $dR_g$; (d) pull back by $s_\alpha$ (II's pull-back theorem); (e) A's computation; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Thm II.5.2; spec: Convention callout: the $\tfrac12$ (bracket) versus no $\tfrac12$ (matrix wedge); B's $d$ convention without $1/(k+1)$ factors. Mechanism summary: "on two vertical vectors $d\omega$ is minus the bracket and $\tfrac12[\omega\wedge\omega]$ is plus the bracket; on mixed pairs both vanish because brackets of fundamental with horizontal fields are horizontal; on horizontal pairs only $d\omega$ survives." Targets: Bianchi; the vector-bundle curvature formula (II) as the $GL_k$ case; Chern–Weil.
- `Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Principal Bundle` **[anchor]** — type: definition; source items: A-T2.2.8 (the operator $d^{\nabla_a}$ on $\operatorname{ad}P$-valued forms), B-T2.4.4 (the operator "restrict $d$ to horizontal vectors"), Kobayashi–Nomizu's $D$; prereqs: [Def - Horizontal Subspace and Horizontal Lift, Def - Equivariant and Basic Forms on a Principal Bundle, Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection]; spec: $D^\omega\alpha:=(d\alpha)\circ\pi_H$ for $V$-valued forms on $P$; corollaries proved on the page: $D^\omega$ maps equivariant forms to basic equivariant forms; $\Omega=D^\omega\omega$ (restating the definition); for a basic equivariant form $\alpha$ of type $\rho$, $D^\omega\alpha=d\alpha+\rho_*(\omega)\wedge\alpha$ (the "tensorial formula"; proved: both sides are basic and agree on horizontal vectors and on vertical ones by equivariance); in particular $D^\omega\Omega=d\Omega+[\omega\wedge\Omega]$. Verified example: on $M\times G$ with the product connection $D^\omega=d$ on basic forms; non-example: $D^\omega\circ D^\omega\ne0$ in general ($=\rho_*(\Omega)\wedge$ — proved as corollary). Calibration: $D^\omega$ on functions is the horizontal part of $d$; $D^\omega\pi^*\alpha=\pi^*d\alpha$.
- `Thm - Exterior Covariant Derivatives on P and on Associated Bundles Agree` — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.2.8 (its operator), conventions.md ("Bianchi identity $d^\omega\Omega=0$, locally $dF_A+[A\wedge F_A]=0$"); prereqs: [Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Principal Bundle, Thm - Bundle-Valued Forms on the Base are Basic Equivariant Forms on the Total Space, Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Vector Bundle, Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles]; statement: Under the bijection $\Omega^q(M;P\times_\rho V)\cong\Omega^q_{\mathrm{bas}}(P;V)^G$, the exterior covariant derivative $d^\nabla$ of the induced connection on $P\times_\rho V$ corresponds to $D^\omega$; locally, with $A=s^*\omega$, $d^\nabla$ is $d+\rho_*(A)\wedge$; in particular $d^{\nabla_\omega}F_\omega$ corresponds to $D^\omega\Omega$; proof: check on $q=0$ (this is (47) of A) and extend by the Leibniz rule, both operators satisfying the same graded Leibniz rule against $\Omega^\bullet(M)$; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Prop II.5.? (comparison of $D$ and $\nabla$), Baum Satz 3.? ; spec: NOTE ordering: this page depends on §4.4's induced-connection theorem; the topic page orders §4.4's theorem before it (see the dependency order below).
- `Thm - Bianchi Identity for a Principal Connection` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.4.4, B-T2.4.5, A-T2.2.8, A-R2.2.8, A-I2.2.5; prereqs: [Thm - Structure Equation for the Curvature, Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Principal Bundle, Thm - Graded Antisymmetry and Jacobi Identity for Lie-Algebra-Valued Forms, Thm - Exterior Covariant Derivatives on P and on Associated Bundles Agree]; statement: (a) $D^\omega\Omega=0$, i.e. $d\Omega$ vanishes on triples of horizontal vectors; (b) $d\Omega=[\Omega\wedge\omega]$ on $P$ (A's (55) with $\hat F_A=\Omega$); (c) locally $dF_\alpha+[A_\alpha\wedge F_\alpha]=0$, i.e. $d^{\nabla_\omega}F_\omega=0$ for the induced connection on $\operatorname{ad}P$; (d) if $G$ is abelian, $\Omega=d\omega$ and $d\Omega=0$, and $F\in\Omega^2(M;\mathfrak g)$ is closed; proof: B full for (a) (six-term formula; every term has a horizontal-vanishing factor), A full for (c) (the graded computation with the Jacobi step, now justified by §4.1's theorem); (b) from $d\Omega=\tfrac12d[\omega\wedge\omega]=[d\omega\wedge\omega]=[\Omega\wedge\omega]-\tfrac12[[\omega\wedge\omega]\wedge\omega]$ and the Jacobi identity for 1-forms; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Thm II.5.4; spec: Mechanism: "$d$ of a structure equation is a Jacobi identity." Targets: Chern–Weil closedness (VI); the Yang–Mills equation's companion (VII).
- `Ex - Curvature of the Standard Hopf Connection` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-E2.2.4 (first half: $\pi^*F_a=da=2(dx_0\wedge dx_1+dx_2\wedge dx_3)i$, $F_a(\pi_*v_2,\pi_*v_3)=2i$), B-T2.4.7, B-E2.4.1; prereqs: [Thm - The Standard Connection on the Hopf Bundle, Thm - Structure Equation for the Curvature]; spec: compute $da$ from $a=(-x_1dx_0+x_0dx_1-x_3dx_2+x_2dx_3)i$; verify B's formula $\Omega_p(X,Y)=2i\langle iX,Y\rangle$ and its vanishing on vertical vectors; the metric identification with $2\,\mathrm{vol}_{S^2_{1/2}}i$ is the chapter VI exercise.
- `Ex - Curvature of a Connection on a Trivial Bundle and the Abelian Case` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-T2.4.5, B-R2.4.1; prereqs: [Thm - Structure Equation for the Curvature]; spec: for $\omega=\operatorname{pr}_2^*\theta+\pi^*A$ on $M\times G$ compute $F=dA+\tfrac12[A\wedge A]$; for abelian $G$ show $F=dA$ is closed and the map $A\mapsto F$ is linear; explain B's "semilinear first-order PDE" remark.
- `Ex - Bianchi Identity for an SU(2) Potential in Coordinates` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T2.2.8 drill; prereqs: [Thm - Bianchi Identity for a Principal Connection]; spec: with $A=A_\mu dx^\mu$, $A_\mu\colon\mathbb R^4\to\mathfrak{su}(2)$, write $F_{\mu\nu}=\partial_\mu A_\nu-\partial_\nu A_\mu+[A_\mu,A_\nu]$ and verify $\sum_{cyc}(\partial_\lambda F_{\mu\nu}+[A_\lambda,F_{\mu\nu}])=0$ by direct computation.
- `Ex - The Curvature is the Obstruction to Integrability of the Horizontal Distribution` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: none (standard; foreshadows V's flat connections); prereqs: [Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Thm - The Frobenius Theorem]; spec: prove $\Omega=0$ iff $H$ is involutive iff (Frobenius) $H$ is integrable, using $\Omega(\tilde X,\tilde Y)=-\omega([\tilde X,\tilde Y])$.
- `Ex - Curvature of the Connection Induced on the Frame Bundle by the Levi-Civita Connection` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-E2.3.1 continued; prereqs: [Thm - A Covariant Derivative Determines a Connection on the Frame Bundle, Thm - Structure Equation for the Curvature]; spec: show the local curvature form in the coordinate section is the matrix of RG I's curvature 2-forms, and that the Bianchi identity here is RG III's second Bianchi identity (link `Thm - First and Second Bianchi Identities` as a bridge; prove the identification, not the RG III theorem).
- `Exercise Index - §4.3 Curvature and the Structure Equation` — lists: [Ex - Curvature of the Standard Hopf Connection, Ex - Curvature of a Connection on a Trivial Bundle and the Abelian Case, Ex - Bianchi Identity for an SU(2) Potential in Coordinates, Ex - The Curvature is the Obstruction to Integrability of the Horizontal Distribution, Ex - Curvature of the Connection Induced on the Frame Bundle by the Levi-Civita Connection]

### §4.4 Connections on Associated Bundles and Induced Covariant Derivatives
- `Thm - A Covariant Derivative Determines a Connection on the Frame Bundle` — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.2.5(i) (Steps 1–3), A-X2.2.8 (Step 1, as Lemma), B-T2.3.2, B-T2.3.3, B-E2.3.1, B-E2.3.2; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle, Def - Connection Matrix and Local Form of a Connection, Def - Frame Bundle of a Vector Bundle, Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle, Def - Local Connection Form and Gauge Potential, Thm - Transformation of Local Connection and Curvature Forms, Thm - Gauge Transformation Law for Connection 1-Forms]; statement: Let $E\to M$ be a vector bundle with frame bundle $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$. (a) For every connection $\nabla$ on $E$ there is a unique connection $\omega$ on $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ with $e^*\omega=A(\nabla,e)$ for every local frame $e$; (b) equivalently (B's construction), for $X\in T_p\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ and any curve $p(t)$ of frames with $\dot p(0)=X$, $\omega(X)$ is the matrix defined by $\tfrac{\nabla}{dt}\big|_0p_j=(p(0)\cdot\omega(X))_j$, and this is well defined; (c) in the coordinate section $s=(\partial_1,\dots,\partial_n)$ of $\operatorname{Fr}(TM)$, $(s^*\omega(\partial_k))^j{}_i=\Gamma^j_{ik}$; (d) $\nabla\mapsto\omega$ is a bijection $\mathcal A(E)\to\mathcal A(\operatorname{Fr}(E))$ (with inverse the induced connection of the next theorem); proof: A full in Steps 1–3 with Step 1 left as exercise — write Lemma 1 ($d\hat R_{(p,g)}(v,w)=dR_g(v)+(\theta_g(w))_P(pg)$, the differential of the action map, by the product rule on $\hat R(p(t),g(t))$), Lemma 2 (over a trivialising $U$, for any $A\in\Omega^1(U;\mathfrak{gl}_k)$ there is a unique connection on $\operatorname{Fr}(E)|_U$ with $e^*\omega=A$ — the reconstruction formula, with the equivariance check using chapter I's $(R_g)_*\xi_P=(\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\xi)_P$), Step 3 (independence of the frame, via Lemma 1 and (14), which is exactly the local transformation law (a) of the previous theorem); (b) B's argument with the three checks (curve independence via the covariant derivative along a curve — link RG I `Def - Covariant Derivative along a Curve`; property (2) with $p(t)=p\exp(tX)$; property (1)); (c) unwinding; (d) after the next theorem; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. III §1 (Prop 1.? "linear connections"), Baum Satz 3.? ; spec: Convention callout: A writes $e^*a=A(\nabla,e)$; RG I's Cartan connection forms $\omega^a{}_b$ are the entries of $s^*\omega$.
- `Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.2.5(ii) (Step 4), A-D2.2.14, A-R2.2.6, A-T2.2.6, A-T2.2.7, A-X2.2.7 (identification clause), B-D2.3.5, B-T2.3.10, B-R2.3.9; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle, Def - Associated Bundle, Thm - Sections of an Associated Bundle are Equivariant Functions, Thm - Bundle-Valued Forms on the Base are Basic Equivariant Forms on the Total Space, Def - Representation of a Lie Algebra, Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle, Def - Curvature of a Vector-Bundle Connection, Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Thm - Structure Equation for the Curvature, Def - Induced Connections on Dual, Hom, and Endomorphism Bundles]; statement: Let $\omega$ be a connection on $P$ and $\rho\colon G\to GL(V)$ a representation with differential $\rho_*$. (a) There is a unique connection $\nabla=\nabla^\omega$ on $E=P\times_\rho V$ such that, for every section $s$ with equivariant lift $\hat s$, $\pi^*(\nabla s)$ corresponds to the basic equivariant $V$-valued 1-form $d\hat s+\rho_*(\omega)\hat s$ (A's (47)). (b) Locally, for a section $p$ of $P$ over $U$ and $v\colon U\to V$, $\nabla_X[p,v]=[p,\partial_Xv+\rho_*(p^*\omega(X))v]$ (B's formula), and this is independent of the representative (B-T2.3.10 completed: the computation with $(pg)^*\omega=\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}p^*\omega+g^*\theta$ and $\rho_*(g^*\theta(X))\rho(g^{-1})v=\partial_X(\rho(g^{-1})v)$... written in full). (c) The curvature of $\nabla^\omega$ is $\rho_*(F_\omega)\in\Omega^2(M;\operatorname{End}E)$, i.e. $\pi^*F_{\nabla^\omega}$ corresponds to $\rho_*(\Omega)$; locally $F_{\nabla^\omega}=\rho_*(F_\alpha)$ (A-T2.2.7). (d) For $E$ a vector bundle with frame bundle $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ and $\rho$ the standard representation, $\nabla^\omega$ has connection matrices $e^*\omega$, so the constructions of this and the previous theorem are mutually inverse; the induced connections on $E^*$, $\operatorname{End}E$, $\Lambda^pE$ (via the corresponding representations) coincide with II's direct constructions (A-T2.2.6, A-X2.2.7). (e) For $\rho=\operatorname{Ad}$ on $\operatorname{ad}P$, $\nabla^\omega$ is the connection $\nabla_a$ of A's Bianchi identity; proof: A full for (a) modulo equivariance of $d\hat s+a\cdot\hat s$ and uniqueness — write: $(d\hat s+\rho_*(\omega)\hat s)(\xi_P)=-\rho_*(\xi)\hat s+\rho_*(\xi)\hat s=0$ (differentiate $\hat s(p\exp t\xi)=\rho(\exp(-t\xi))\hat s(p)$), equivariance by $R_g^*d\hat s=d(\rho(g^{-1})\hat s)$ and $R_g^*\omega=\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\omega$ with $\rho_*\circ\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}=\rho(g^{-1})\rho_*\rho(g)$ (naturality of $\rho_*$, chapter I); Leibniz for $f\hat s$; uniqueness since $\nabla s$ is determined; (b) B's computation completed; (c) apply $d^\nabla$ twice to (47) and use the structure equation and $\rho_*[\xi,\eta]=[\rho_*\xi,\rho_*\eta]$; (d),(e) unwinding; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. III Prop 1.?; Baum Satz 3.? ; spec: Mechanism summary: "the covariant derivative of a section is the ordinary derivative of its equivariant lift corrected by the connection form so as to become horizontal." Targets: Levi-Civita as an $O(n)$-connection on the orthonormal frame bundle (RG I bridge), spinor connections (VIII), the Yang–Mills coupling to matter (VII).
- `Thm - Pull-Back of Connections and Curvature` — type: theorem; source items: A-T2.2.9, A-D2.2.17, A-R2.2.9, B-T2.5.4 (connection half: $\operatorname{pr}_2^*\omega$ is a connection on $f^*P$, $\Omega'=\operatorname{pr}_2^*\Omega$, $\Omega'_\alpha=f^*\Omega_\alpha$), B-R2.5.2; prereqs: [Thm - The Pull-Back of a Fibre Bundle is a Fibre Bundle, Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle, Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Thm - Pull-Back Commutes with the Exterior Derivative]; statement: For $f\colon N\to M$ and $\hat f\colon f^*P\to P$ the canonical equivariant map: (a) $f^*\omega:=\hat f^*\omega$ is a connection on $f^*P$; (b) its curvature is $\hat f^*\Omega$, and $F_{f^*\omega}=f^*F_\omega$ under the canonical identification $\operatorname{ad}(f^*P)=f^*\operatorname{ad}P$; (c) local forms: $A'_\alpha=f^*A_\alpha$ and $F'_\alpha=f^*F_\alpha$ for the pulled-back sections $s'_\alpha=\hat f^{-1}\circ s_\alpha\circ f$; (d) the induced covariant derivative on $f^*(P\times_\rho V)=f^*P\times_\rho V$ is the pull-back connection of the vector bundle; proof: A left as exercise, B full for (a),(c); write: equivariance of $\hat f$ gives (1), and $\hat f$ maps fundamental fields to fundamental fields gives (2); (b) by naturality of $d$ and the bracket under pull-back; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Prop II.6.2; spec: Targets: naturality of characteristic classes (VI), restriction of connections to curves (V: $\gamma^*\nabla$).
- `Ex - The Differential of the Right Action Map` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X2.2.8; prereqs: [Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action, Def - The Maurer-Cartan Form]; spec: prove $\hat R_*(v,w)=(R_g)_*v+(\theta_g(w))_P(pg)$ via $\hat R(p(t),g(t))=\hat R(\hat R(p(t),g),g^{-1}g(t))$.
- `Ex - Induced Connections on the Dual and Endomorphism Bundles Agree with the Direct Ones` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X2.2.7 (identification clause), A-T2.2.6; prereqs: [Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles, Def - Induced Connections on Dual, Hom, and Endomorphism Bundles]; spec: with $\rho^*$ and $\operatorname{Ad}$ on $\operatorname{End}(\mathbb R^k)$, compute $\rho_*$ and show the local matrices are $-A^t$ and $[A,\cdot]$, matching II.
- `Ex - The Induced Connection on the Tautological Bundle from the Hopf Connection` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T2.2.5 applied to A-E2.2.3 and A-X2.2.6; prereqs: [Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles, Thm - The Standard Connection on the Hopf Bundle, Ex - The Tautological Line Bundle over CP^n is Associated to the Hopf Bundle]; spec: show the induced connection on $\mathcal O(-1)\subset\underline{\mathbb C^{n+1}}$ is the projected connection $\nabla s=\operatorname{pr}(ds)$ (Hermitian projection onto the line), and that it is Hermitian; compute its curvature via (c).
- `Ex - Pull-Back of the Hopf Connection along the Inclusion of CP^1 into CP^n` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-T2.2.9 drill, A-R2.4.2; prereqs: [Thm - Pull-Back of Connections and Curvature, Def - The Hopf Bundle]; spec: the pull-back of $S^{2n+1}\to\mathbb{CP}^n$ along $\iota\colon\mathbb{CP}^1\hookrightarrow\mathbb{CP}^n$ is $S^3\to\mathbb{CP}^1$ with its own standard connection.
- `Exercise Index - §4.4 Connections on Associated Bundles and Induced Covariant Derivatives` — lists: [Ex - The Differential of the Right Action Map, Ex - Induced Connections on the Dual and Endomorphism Bundles Agree with the Direct Ones, Ex - The Induced Connection on the Tautological Bundle from the Hopf Connection, Ex - Pull-Back of the Hopf Connection along the Inclusion of CP^1 into CP^n]
