## Gauge Theory V — Parallel Transport, Holonomy, Gauge Transformations, and Flat Connections            (folder: Gauge Theory V/)
Sources: A §2.2.5, A §3.3 (and the gauge-group items of A §2.1.6 by link to II); B §2.6, §2.7; A-D3.2.3 (flat connection).
Existing vault pages to link: DG V — `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Integral Curves`, `Thm - Fundamental Theorem on Flows`; DG X — `Thm - The Frobenius Theorem`, `Def - Involutive Distribution`, `Def - Integral Manifold of a Distribution`; DG IX — `Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds`; DG VIII — `Thm - Cartan's Magic Formula`; RG I — `Def - Parallel Transport`, `Def - Covariant Derivative along a Curve`, `Thm - Parallel Transport is an Isometry for Metric-Compatible Connections`, `Ex - Parallel Transport around a Geodesic Triangle on the Sphere`; RG IV — `Ex - Holonomy around a Spherical Cap is the Solid Angle`; AT II — `Def - Covering Space`, `Def - Universal Cover` (definition only; existence is proved here), `Def - Deck Transformation Group` (definition only), `Def - Homotopy of Paths`, `Def - Path-Product and the Fundamental Group`, `Thm - The Fundamental Group is a Group`, `Def - Simply Connected Space`, `Thm - Path Lifting and Homotopy Lifting`, `Thm - Lifting Criterion for Continuous Maps`, `Thm - Pi_1 of S^1 is Z`, `Ex - Pi_1 of the Torus is Z Squared`; Topology IV — `Def - Homotopy`; chapters I–IV pages as named in the prereqs. Analysis: if `vault-index.txt` contains a proof-complete page on termwise differentiation of uniformly convergent series (search "Uniform Convergence" under Analysis), link it in the path-ordered-exponential theorem; otherwise that page proves the needed lemma itself (the manifest specifies the lemma).
Flagged (never link): AT II `Thm - Seifert-van Kampen Theorem (Statement)` (surface-group presentations are therefore never claimed); Topology III `Thm - Tychonoff Theorem` (the compactness theorem uses a countable-product diagonal argument instead); RG II `Thm - Hopf-Rinow Theorem (Statement)`.
Sections: §5.1 Horizontal Lifts and Parallel Transport | §5.2 Holonomy | §5.3 Gauge Transformations | §5.4 Flat Connections and Representations of the Fundamental Group

### §5.1 Horizontal Lifts and Parallel Transport
- `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Horizontal Lifts` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.6.1, B-T2.6.2, B-D2.6.1, B-I2.6.1, A-I3.3.1, A-R3.3.2 (the ODE reduction); prereqs: [Def - Horizontal Subspace and Horizontal Lift, Def - Local Connection Form and Gauge Potential, Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Integral Curves, Def - The Maurer-Cartan Form]; statement: Let $\omega$ be a connection on $P\to M$, $c\colon I\to M$ piecewise smooth, $t_0\in I$, $p\in P_{c(t_0)}$. There is a unique piecewise smooth $\tilde c\colon I\to P$ with $\pi\circ\tilde c=c$, $\dot{\tilde c}(t)\in H_{\tilde c(t)}$, $\tilde c(t_0)=p$; it is defined on all of $I$; and $\widetilde{c}\cdot g$ is the horizontal lift through $pg$; proof: B full except uniform global existence — write: Lemma 1 (local reduction: with $c(I')\subset U_\alpha$, $\tilde c=s_\alpha(c)h$ and horizontality $\iff$ $\dot h=-dR_h(A_\alpha(\dot c))$, B's (2.9), derived with the differential of the action map — IV §4.4 Lemma 1 / exercise); Lemma 2 (global existence for the ODE (2.9): for matrix groups it is linear, $\dot h=-A_\alpha(\dot c)h$, and linear ODEs with continuous coefficients have solutions on the whole interval — proved by the Grönwall/iteration argument, Lemma 2a; for general $G$ use B's Rem 2.6.2 argument: if the maximal solution stops at $t_1$, a lift near $c(t_1)$ shifted by $g$ extends it — written in full with the uniqueness step); Lemma 3 (piecewise smooth curves by concatenation; independence of the subdivision); Lemma 4 (equivariance $\widetilde{c}\cdot g$); reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Prop II.3.1; spec: Mechanism: "horizontality is a first-order ODE for the fibre coordinate, driven by the local connection form along the curve." Targets: parallel transport, holonomy, homotopy invariance of bundles via parallel transport (alternative proof; not needed), the reduced gauge group acting freely.
- `Def - Parallel Transport in a Principal Bundle` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D2.6.3, B-D2.6.2, B-T2.6.3 (corollary proved), B-E2.6.1, A-D3.3.2, A-D3.3.3, A-R3.3.2, A-R3.3.3, A-R3.3.5, A-I3.3.2 (link to III's homotopy theorem, corollary (c)); prereqs: [Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Horizontal Lifts, Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles, Def - Parallel Transport, Def - Covariant Derivative along a Curve, Thm - Pull-Back of Connections and Curvature]; spec: $\Gamma(c)\colon P_{c(t_0)}\to P_{c(t_1)}$, $\Gamma(c)(p)=\tilde c(t_1)$; parallel sections of an associated bundle along $c$ as $t\mapsto[\tilde c(t),v]$ (B-D2.6.2), with the corollary (B-T2.6.3) that these are exactly the sections with $\tfrac{\nabla}{dt}=0$ for the induced connection, so parallel transport in $E=P\times_\rho V$ is $[p,v]\mapsto[\Gamma(c)p,v]$, linear; for the frame bundle, a horizontal lift is a parallel frame (B-E2.6.1); A's vector-bundle definition (parallel sections of $\gamma^*E$, the linear ODE $\dot s+B(t)s=0$ in a trivialisation of $\gamma^*E$ — which is trivial because $[0,1]$ is contractible, III) and $PT_\gamma$; the two definitions agree (corollary proved: both solve the same ODE); A's typo $A=B$ noted; embedded curves (A-R3.3.3). Convention callout: RG I's `Def - Parallel Transport` is the same object for $E=TM$ and for vector bundles; A's $PT_\gamma$, B's $\Gamma(c)$, the series writes $\Gamma_c$ on $P$ and $PT_c$ on $E$. Verified examples: product connection: $\Gamma(c)(m,g)=(c(t_1),g)$; the trivial line bundle over $S^1$ with $A=ia\,d\theta$: $PT$ around the circle is $e^{-2\pi ia}$ (computed; II's exercise revisited); non-example: parallel transport is not defined for a continuous but nowhere-differentiable curve. Calibration: $\Gamma(c)$ for a constant curve is the identity; parallel transport in a Euclidean bundle with metric connection is an isometry (link RG I's theorem for $TM$; proved for vector bundles as corollary).
- `Thm - Properties of Parallel Transport` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.6.4, B-R2.6.1, A-X3.3.1 (isometry/unitary/complex-linear clauses); prereqs: [Def - Parallel Transport in a Principal Bundle, Def - Euclidean Vector Bundle and Metric Connection]; statement: (1) $c$ constant $\Rightarrow\Gamma(c)=\operatorname{id}$; (2) orientation-preserving reparametrisation leaves $\Gamma(c)$ unchanged; (3) orientation reversal gives $\Gamma(c)^{-1}$, so $\Gamma(c)$ is a diffeomorphism $P_{c(t_0)}\to P_{c(t_1)}$; (4) $\Gamma(c_2*c_1)=\Gamma(c_2)\circ\Gamma(c_1)$; (5) $\Gamma(c)\circ R_g=R_g\circ\Gamma(c)$; (6) in an associated vector bundle, $PT_c$ is a linear isomorphism, is an isometry when $\rho$ is orthogonal/unitary (equivalently when the connection is metric/Hermitian), and is complex-linear when $\rho$ is complex; (7) $\Gamma(c)$ depends on $c$, not only on its endpoints, in general (example: the Hopf connection, §5.2); proof: B brief but full for (1)–(5); (6) from the equivariance $\Gamma(c)\circ R_g=R_g\circ\Gamma(c)$ and the $G$-invariance of the inner product on $V$, or directly from $\tfrac{d}{dt}\langle s_1,s_2\rangle=\langle\nabla_ts_1,s_2\rangle+\langle s_1,\nabla_ts_2\rangle$; write all; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. II §3; spec: Targets: holonomy groups are groups; $\operatorname{Hol}\subset O(k),U(k)$ (§5.2).
- `Def - Path-Ordered Exponential` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D2.6.4, B-R2.6.2, B-T2.6.5, B-E2.6.2; prereqs: [Def - Parallel Transport in a Principal Bundle, Thm - The Exponential Map of a Matrix Group is the Matrix Exponential]; spec: for continuous $A\colon[0,L]\to\operatorname{Mat}(n\times n;\mathbb K)$, $\mathcal P\exp(-\int_0^tA)$ defined by the Dyson series (2.13) and, equivalently, the ordered product limit (2.14) (equivalence is the theorem below); the ordering problem (R2.6.2); corollary proved on the page (B-T2.6.5): if all $A(t)$ commute, $\mathcal P\exp(-\int A)=\exp(-\int_0^tA)$ (termwise differentiation justified by the Banach-algebra power series); the parallel-transport ODE $\dot v=-A(t)v$ as the matrix-group horizontal-lift equation. Verified examples: constant $A$; commuting diagonal $A(t)$; non-example: $A(t)=\sigma_1$ on $[0,1]$ and $\sigma_3$ on $[1,2]$ gives $e^{-\sigma_3}e^{-\sigma_1}\ne e^{-\sigma_1-\sigma_3}$ (computed). Calibration: $\mathcal P\exp$ is invertible with inverse the reversed-order exponential; the value at $t=0$ is $1$.
- `Thm - The Path-Ordered Exponential Solves the Linear Parallel-Transport Equation` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.6.6, B-I2.6.3; prereqs: [Def - Path-Ordered Exponential]; statement: For continuous $A\colon[0,L]\to\operatorname{Mat}(n\times n;\mathbb K)$ the unique solution of $\dot v=-Av$, $v(0)=v_0$, is $v(t)=\sum_{j\ge0}(-1)^j\int_0^t\!d\tau_j\int_0^{\tau_j}\!d\tau_{j-1}\cdots\int_0^{\tau_2}\!d\tau_1A(\tau_j)\cdots A(\tau_1)v_0$, the series converging absolutely and uniformly in $C^1([0,L])$; moreover $v(t)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\prod_{k=N-1}^{0}\big(1-\tfrac tNA(\tfrac kNt)\big)v_0$; proof: B full with the gap in (a) (uniform $O(\epsilon^2)$ needs a Lipschitz/uniform-continuity estimate) — write: (i) the bounds $\|j\text{-th term}\|\le L^j\|A\|^j_{C^0}/j!$ and the derivative bounds; (ii) termwise differentiation licensed by uniform convergence of the differentiated series (Lemma, proved on the page or linked to the vault's uniform-convergence page if proof-complete); (iii) uniqueness by Grönwall (Lemma); (iv) the product formula proved rigorously: compare the exact solution operator on $[k t/N,(k+1)t/N]$ with $1-\tfrac tNA(kt/N)$ using the integral equation and uniform continuity of $A$, giving an error $o(1/N)$ per step and $o(1)$ in total (write the telescoping estimate); reference: Reed–Simon II Thm X.69 (Dyson expansion) / Hall Thm 5.? ; spec: Mechanism: "Picard iteration for a linear equation converges everywhere, and its terms are the ordered integrals." Targets: holonomy expansion (§5.2), Wilson loops (bold outlook).
- `Ex - Horizontal Lifts in the Trivial Bundle with a Constant Connection` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-T2.6.1 drill; prereqs: [Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Horizontal Lifts]; spec: on $\mathbb R^2\times U(1)$ with $A=i(a\,dx+b\,dy)$ lift the segment from $0$ to $(x,y)$ and the unit square loop; compute $\Gamma=e^{-i(ax+by)}$ and the loop holonomy $1$ (flat), then repeat with $A=ix\,dy$ (holonomy $e^{-i}$ around the unit square).
- `Ex - Parallel Transport in the Hopf Bundle along a Great Circle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-E2.2.3 / B-T2.3.5 continued; prereqs: [Def - Parallel Transport in a Principal Bundle, Thm - The Standard Connection on the Hopf Bundle]; spec: lift the equator of $S^2$ (in B's coordinates) horizontally to $S^3$, using the local form $A_1$ from IV's exercise; find the holonomy $e^{-i\pi}=-1$ (half the sphere enclosed; sign per orientation), and compare with the curvature integral (§5.2).
- `Ex - The Dyson Series for a Two-Level System` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-T2.6.6 drill; prereqs: [Thm - The Path-Ordered Exponential Solves the Linear Parallel-Transport Equation]; spec: $A(t)=a(t)\sigma_1+b(t)\sigma_3$ with $a,b$ given; write the second-order term explicitly and show it is not $\tfrac12(\int A)^2$ unless $a$ or $b$ vanishes.
- `Ex - Global Existence for Linear ODEs via Gronwall` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-I2.6.1, A-I3.3.1; prereqs: [Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Horizontal Lifts]; spec: prove Grönwall's inequality and deduce that $\dot v=-A(t)v$ has a unique solution on all of $[0,L]$ for continuous $A$ (the Lemma 2a of the lifting theorem as a stand-alone drill).
- `Ex - Parallel Transport in an Associated Vector Bundle is Linear and Agrees with the ODE Definition` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-T2.6.3, A-D3.3.3; prereqs: [Def - Parallel Transport in a Principal Bundle]; spec: prove $[p,v]\mapsto[\Gamma(c)p,v]$ is well defined and linear and solves A's ODE in a trivialisation.
- `Exercise Index - §5.1 Horizontal Lifts and Parallel Transport` — lists: [Ex - Horizontal Lifts in the Trivial Bundle with a Constant Connection, Ex - Parallel Transport in the Hopf Bundle along a Great Circle, Ex - The Dyson Series for a Two-Level System, Ex - Global Existence for Linear ODEs via Gronwall, Ex - Parallel Transport in an Associated Vector Bundle is Linear and Agrees with the ODE Definition]

### §5.2 Holonomy
- `Def - Holonomy Group of a Connection` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D3.3.4, A-R3.3.4, A-X3.3.1 (statement; corollary and exercise), B-R2.6.1; prereqs: [Thm - Properties of Parallel Transport, Def - Parallel Transport in a Principal Bundle]; spec: for a principal connection and $p\in P_m$, the holonomy $\operatorname{hol}_p(c)\in G$ of a loop $c$ at $m$ defined by $\Gamma(c)(p)=p\cdot\operatorname{hol}_p(c)$; $\operatorname{Hol}_p(\omega):=\{\operatorname{hol}_p(c)\}$; for a vector-bundle connection, $\operatorname{Hol}_m(\nabla):=\{PT_c\}\subset GL(E_m)$ (A); corollaries proved on the page: $\operatorname{Hol}$ is a subgroup (concatenation and reversal, from the properties theorem); $\operatorname{hol}_{pg}(c)=g^{-1}\operatorname{hol}_p(c)g$ and $\operatorname{Hol}_{m'}=PT_\gamma\operatorname{Hol}_mPT_\gamma^{-1}$ for a path $\gamma$ from $m$ to $m'$ (A-R3.3.4, "standard argument" written); the holonomy of a metric/Hermitian connection lies in $O(k)$/$U(k)$ and of a complex connection in $GL_{k/2}(\mathbb C)$ (A-X3.3.1 as corollary of the properties theorem (6)); the restricted holonomy group (loops null-homotopic) defined for later use. Verified examples: product connection: trivial holonomy; the constant connection on the trivial line bundle over $S^1$: $\operatorname{Hol}=\{e^{-2\pi ika}\}$; the Hopf connection: $\operatorname{Hol}=U(1)$ (from the exercises); non-example: $\operatorname{Hol}$ is not in general a closed subgroup (record as a scope remark — not claimed either way). Convention callout: A drops the base point after fixing a basis; B does not name the holonomy group. Calibration: holonomy of a reversed loop is the inverse; holonomy is unchanged under reparametrisation.
- `Thm - Holonomy of an Abelian Connection is the Exponential of the Curvature Integral` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.6.7, B-E2.6.3, B-I2.6.2; prereqs: [Def - Holonomy Group of a Connection, Def - Path-Ordered Exponential, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Thm - Transformation of Local Connection and Curvature Forms]; statement: Let $G$ be abelian, $c$ a piecewise smooth loop in a trivialising set $U_\alpha$ bounding a compact oriented surface $S\subset U_\alpha$ with $\partial S=c$. Then $\operatorname{hol}(c)=\exp\big(-\int_c A_\alpha\big)=\exp\big(-\int_S F\big)$, where $F=dA_\alpha$ is the global curvature; more generally, for any loop $c$ in $U_\alpha$, $\operatorname{hol}(c)=\exp(-\int_cA_\alpha)$; in particular if $F\ne0$ then some loop has nontrivial holonomy; proof: B full: commuting case of the ODE (Def page corollary) and Stokes; write the independence of the trivialisation ($\int_c$ of $g^*\theta$ over a loop is in the kernel of $\exp$ — for $U(1)$ it is $2\pi i\cdot w(g)$, III's winding-number theorem); reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. II §7 (abelian case) ; spec: Targets: Dirac quantisation (VII), the Hopf holonomy, RG IV's spherical-cap exercise (bridge).
- `Thm - Curvature is the Infinitesimal Holonomy` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.6.8, B-R2.6.3, B §2.6 conventions (2.15); prereqs: [Thm - The Path-Ordered Exponential Solves the Linear Parallel-Transport Equation, Def - Holonomy Group of a Connection, Thm - Structure Equation for the Curvature, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; statement: Let $G\subset GL(n;\mathbb K)$ be a matrix group, $\omega$ a connection with local form $A=A_jdx^j$ in coordinates centred at $b_0$, and for $L\in(0,L_0)$ let $c_L$ be a piecewise smooth loop at $b_0$ of length at most $CL$, contained in the ball of radius $CL$, bounding an oriented surface $S_L$ of area at most $CL^2$, parametrised on $[0,1]$ proportionally to arc length. Then $\operatorname{hol}(c_L)=1-\int_{S_L}F+O(L^3)$ as $L\searrow0$, with $F=dA+A\wedge A$ the local curvature, where the $O(L^3)$ constant depends only on $C$ and the $C^1$-norm of $A$ on the ball; proof: B full modulo error bookkeeping — write: Step 1 (Dyson expansion to second order with the remainder bound $\le\|A\|^3_{C^0}(CL)^3/6$ from the path-ordered-exponential theorem); Step 2 (first-order term $=\int_{c_L}A=\int_{S_L}dA$ by Stokes, and $|\int_{S_L}dA|\le\|dA\|_{C^0}CL^2$); Step 3 (symmetric part $I_s=\tfrac12(\int_cA)^2=O(L^4)$); Step 4 (antisymmetric part: freeze $A_j$ at $b_0$ with error $\le\|A\|_{C^1}CL\cdot(CL)^2$, then $2I_a=\sum_{j,k}A_j(0)A_k(0)\int_c(x^kdx^j-x^jdx^k)=-2\sum_{j<k}[A_j(0),A_k(0)]\int_{S_L}dx^j\wedge dx^k+O(L^3)$ by Stokes; Step 5 (assemble: $-\int_S dA-\int_SA\wedge A+O(L^3)$, using $A\wedge A=\sum_{j<k}[A_j,A_k]dx^j\wedge dx^k$); reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. II §7 (Ambrose–Singer context; the expansion is B's own); spec: Mechanism summary: "to second order the holonomy of a small loop is $1$ minus the flux of the curvature through the loop; the $A\wedge A$ term is the non-commutativity of the ordered product." Targets: Ambrose–Singer (bold outlook), the Yang–Mills field strength as "infinitesimal holonomy" (VII), the physics reading of $F_{\mu\nu}$.
- `Ex - Holonomy of Euclidean and Hermitian Connections Lies in the Orthogonal and Unitary Groups` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-X3.3.1; prereqs: [Def - Holonomy Group of a Connection, Thm - Properties of Parallel Transport]; spec: all three clauses of A's exercise, using property (6).
- `Ex - Holonomy Groups at Different Base Points are Conjugate` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-R3.3.4; prereqs: [Def - Holonomy Group of a Connection]; spec: write the conjugation argument for $m,m'$ in the same component and show independence up to conjugacy of the base point and of the basis.
- `Ex - Holonomy of the Hopf Connection around a Latitude Circle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-T2.6.7 drill, A-E2.2.4; prereqs: [Thm - Holonomy of an Abelian Connection is the Exponential of the Curvature Integral, Ex - Curvature of the Standard Hopf Connection]; spec: for the latitude circle at height $t$ compute $\operatorname{hol}=\exp(-\int_{cap}F)=\exp(-i\cdot\text{(normalised area)})$ with the explicit $F$; compare with RG IV `Ex - Holonomy around a Spherical Cap is the Solid Angle` and with the great-circle exercise of §5.1.
- `Ex - Holonomy of a Constant Connection on the Trivial Bundle over the Torus` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-T2.6.7 drill (flat case), A-E3.3.1 foreshadowed; prereqs: [Def - Holonomy Group of a Connection]; spec: $A=i(a\,dx+b\,dy)$ on $T^2\times U(1)$: holonomies $e^{-2\pi ia}$, $e^{-2\pi ib}$ around the two generating loops, curvature zero, $\operatorname{Hol}$ the subgroup they generate.
- `Ex - Second-Order Holonomy Expansion for a Small Square in the Plane` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-T2.6.8 drill; prereqs: [Thm - Curvature is the Infinitesimal Holonomy, Thm - The Path-Ordered Exponential Solves the Linear Parallel-Transport Equation]; spec: for $A=x\,dy\,X+y\,dx\,Y$ with constant matrices $X,Y$ and the square of side $L$ at the origin, compute the Dyson series to second order by hand and verify $\operatorname{hol}=1-L^2([Y,X]+X-Y)\cdot(\ldots)$ — i.e. $1-\int_SF+O(L^3)$ with $F=(X-Y)dx\wedge dy+[Y,X]xy\,dx\wedge dy$ (the writer computes the exact coefficients).
- `Exercise Index - §5.2 Holonomy` — lists: [Ex - Holonomy of Euclidean and Hermitian Connections Lies in the Orthogonal and Unitary Groups, Ex - Holonomy Groups at Different Base Points are Conjugate, Ex - Holonomy of the Hopf Connection around a Latitude Circle, Ex - Holonomy of a Constant Connection on the Trivial Bundle over the Torus, Ex - Second-Order Holonomy Expansion for a Small Square in the Plane]

### §5.3 Gauge Transformations
- `Def - Gauge Transformation` **[backlink, anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D2.7.1, B-D2.7.2, B-D2.7.3, B-D2.7.5, B-T2.7.2 (corollary proved), B-R2.7.2, B-E2.7.2, A-D2.2.18, A-D2.2.20 (the action; its properties are the theorem below), A §2.2 conventions; prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - Gauge Group of a Vector Bundle, Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle]; spec: $\operatorname{Aut}(P)$ = equivariant diffeomorphisms $f(pg)=f(p)g$; the induced diffeomorphism $\bar f$ of the base (existence, uniqueness and smoothness proved on the page via local sections); the gauge group $\mathcal G(P):=\{f\in\operatorname{Aut}(P):\bar f=\operatorname{id}\}=\ker(\operatorname{Aut}(P)\to\operatorname{Diff}(M))$ (B-T2.7.2 proved as corollary), which is A's $\mathcal G(P)$; the reduced gauge group $\mathcal G_b(P)$; the right action $\omega\cdot f:=f^*\omega$ (A's (61)); B's diagram of groups. Convention callout: A's $\mathcal G(E)\subset\Gamma(\operatorname{End}E)$ (II) versus $\mathcal G(\operatorname{Fr}(E))$ — identified in the theorem `Thm - The Gauge Group is the Space of Sections of the Adjoint Group Bundle`; "gauge transformation" in physics = local change of section (III's local gauge) versus the global automorphism here; the two readings are reconciled on `Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature`. Verified examples: for $P=M\times G$, $f(m,h)=(m,g(m)h)$ for $g\colon M\to G$ (proved; note $g$ multiplies on the left); the identity; non-examples: $R_g$ for non-central $g$ is not equivariant (verified); a bundle map covering a rotation of $S^2$ is in $\operatorname{Aut}(P)\setminus\mathcal G(P)$. Categorical: $\mathcal G(P)$ is the automorphism group of $P$ in the category of bundles over $M$. Calibration: $\mathcal G(P)$ acts on $\Gamma(P\times_\rho V)$ by $[p,v]\mapsto[f(p),v]$ (well defined — check); $\mathcal G_b(P)$ is normal in $\mathcal G(P)$? (no in general — the reader checks only the subgroup property).
- `Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.7.1, A-D2.2.20, A-X2.2.10(iii), A-X2.2.11 (statement; exercise), A-R2.1.9 (link II), B-R2.7.6? (none); prereqs: [Def - Gauge Transformation, Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle, Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Thm - Transformation of Local Connection and Curvature Forms, Thm - Gauge Action on Connection Matrices, Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles, Def - The Maurer-Cartan Form]; statement: (a) $\operatorname{Aut}(P)$ is a subgroup of $\operatorname{Diff}(P)$, every $f\in\operatorname{Aut}(P)$ maps fibres to fibres and induces a unique smooth $\bar f$. (b) For $\omega\in\mathcal A(P)$ and $f\in\operatorname{Aut}(P)$, $f^*\omega\in\mathcal A(P)$, and $(\omega,f)\mapsto f^*\omega$ is a right action of $\operatorname{Aut}(P)$, hence of $\mathcal G(P)$, on $\mathcal A(P)$; it is affine: $f^*(\omega+\hat b)=f^*\omega+f^*\hat b$. (c) $\Omega_{f^*\omega}=f^*\Omega_\omega$; for $f\in\mathcal G(P)$ written as $f(p)=p\cdot\hat g(p)$ with $\hat g\colon P\to G$, $\hat g(pg)=g^{-1}\hat g(p)g$: $f^*\omega=\operatorname{Ad}_{\hat g^{-1}}\omega+\hat g^*\theta$ and $f^*\Omega=\operatorname{Ad}_{\hat g^{-1}}\Omega$; in a local section $s$ with $g:=\hat g\circ s\colon U\to G$: $A_{f^*\omega,s}=\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}A_{\omega,s}+g^*\theta$ (matrix case $g^{-1}Ag+g^{-1}dg$) and $F_{f^*\omega,s}=\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}F_{\omega,s}$. (d) On an associated bundle $E=P\times_\rho V$ with the induced gauge transformation $\gamma(f)\in\mathcal G(E)$, $\nabla^{f^*\omega}=\gamma(f)^{-1}\circ\nabla^\omega\circ\gamma(f)$ (A-X2.2.10(iii)), so the principal action and II's vector-bundle action agree. (e) The infinitesimal action of $\xi\in\Gamma(\operatorname{ad}P)$ (the Lie algebra of $\mathcal G(P)$, via $f_t(p)=p\exp(t\hat\xi(p))$) is $\tfrac{d}{dt}\big|_0f_t^*\omega=\widehat{d^{\nabla_\omega}\xi}$, i.e. $\xi\mapsto d^{\nabla_\omega}\xi\in\Omega^1(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$ (A-X2.2.11 with A's sign convention $a\cdot\psi=\psi^*a$ giving $+d_a\xi$ for the right action; A states $-d_a\xi$ for the left-action reading — record both in a Convention callout and fix the series' sign); proof: B full for (a),(b) modulo smoothness of $\bar f$; write all parts: (c) from the product rule for the Maurer–Cartan form and the differential of the action map; (d) via equivariant lifts; (e) differentiate (c) with $\hat g=\exp(t\hat\xi)$; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. II §6 (mappings of connections), Donaldson–Kronheimer §2.1.2, §2.3.1; spec: Mechanism: "a gauge transformation is a change of local section performed globally; it acts on potentials by the same formula as a change of trivialisation." Targets: the moduli spaces $\mathcal A/\mathcal G$ (VI, VII, XI), gauge invariance of curvature norms and of Chern–Weil forms.
- `Thm - The Gauge Group is the Space of Sections of the Adjoint Group Bundle` — type: theorem; source items: A-D2.2.19, A-R2.2.10, B-T2.7.4, B-T2.7.3, B-E2.7.1, B-R2.7.1, A-E2.2.5, A-X2.2.10(i),(ii) (statement; exercise); prereqs: [Def - Gauge Transformation, Def - Adjoint Bundles ad P and Ad P, Thm - Sections of an Associated Bundle are Equivariant Functions, Def - Gauge Group of a Vector Bundle]; statement: (a) $f\mapsto\hat f$ with $f(p)=p\hat f(p)$ is a bijection from $\mathcal G(P)$ onto $\{\hat f\in C^\infty(P;G):\hat f(pg)=g^{-1}\hat f(p)g\}$, hence (by III's equivariant-function theorem for the conjugation action) a group isomorphism $\mathcal G(P)\cong\Gamma(\operatorname{Ad}P)$ with pointwise multiplication; (b) if $G$ is abelian, $\mathcal G(P)\cong C^\infty(M;G)$, every $g\colon M\to G$ giving $f(p)=p\cdot g(\pi p)$; for non-abelian $G$ this recipe works exactly for $g$ with values in the centre (B-R2.7.1, R2.7.6); (c) for $P=\operatorname{Fr}(E)$, $\mathcal G(\operatorname{Fr}(E))\cong\mathcal G(E)$ (II), and a representation $\rho$ induces a homomorphism $\gamma\colon\mathcal G(P)\to\mathcal G(P\times_\rho V)$ with infinitesimal version $\Gamma(\operatorname{ad}P)\to\Gamma(\operatorname{End}E)$ induced by $\rho_*$ (A-X2.2.10(i),(ii)); proof: A/B full modulo smoothness and the group law — write all (the typo $\hat f(pg)=g^{-1}pg$ in A corrected); reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. II §6 ; spec: Targets: $\mathcal G(P)$ as an infinite-dimensional Lie group (exercise), Banach completions (IX, XI).
- `Thm - The Reduced Gauge Group Acts Freely on Connections` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.7.5, B-D2.7.5, B-R2.7.2; prereqs: [Def - Gauge Transformation, Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Horizontal Lifts]; statement: If $M$ is connected and $b\in M$, the action of $\mathcal G_b(P)$ on $\mathcal A(P)$ is free: $f^*\omega=\omega$ and $f|_{P_b}=\operatorname{id}$ imply $f=\operatorname{id}$. Corollary: the stabiliser of $\omega$ in $\mathcal G(P)$ is isomorphic to the centraliser of $\operatorname{Hol}_p(\omega)$ in $G$ (proved: $f$ fixing $\omega$ is determined by $f(p)=p\cdot z$ and commutes with parallel transport); proof: B full (horizontal lifts, $df$ preserves $H$) — write; add the corollary's proof; reference: Donaldson–Kronheimer §4.2.2 (stabilisers and reducible connections); spec: Targets: irreducible connections have stabiliser the centre (VII, XI: $\mathcal B^{irr}$), framed moduli spaces (A's §1 scheme).
- `Ex - Gauge Transformations of an Abelian Bundle are Maps to the Group` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E2.7.1, A-E2.2.5; prereqs: [Thm - The Gauge Group is the Space of Sections of the Adjoint Group Bundle]; spec: prove $f(p)=pg(\pi p)$ is a gauge transformation for abelian $G$ and that all are of this form; for $U(1)$ over $S^1$ describe $\mathcal G$ as $C^\infty(S^1,U(1))$ and its components by winding number (III).
- `Ex - The Gauge Group Homomorphism from a Representation and its Infinitesimal Version` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X2.2.10(i),(ii); prereqs: [Thm - The Gauge Group is the Space of Sections of the Adjoint Group Bundle]; spec: construct $\gamma$, prove it is a homomorphism, and prove the Lie algebra map is a homomorphism; note $\gamma$ need not be injective ($\operatorname{Ad}$ of $SU(2)$).
- `Ex - The Infinitesimal Gauge Action is the Covariant Derivative` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X2.2.11 (with the typo $\Omega^0\to\Omega^1$ corrected); prereqs: [Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature]; spec: differentiate $f_t^*\omega$ at $t=0$ and identify the result with $\pm d^{\nabla_\omega}\xi$ (sign fixed per the theorem's convention callout); check on the trivial $U(1)$-bundle: $\xi\mapsto d\xi$.
- `Ex - The C^k Gauge Group of a Matrix Group Bundle is a Banach Lie Group` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: A-X2.2.9; prereqs: [Thm - The Gauge Group is the Space of Sections of the Adjoint Group Bundle, Thm - The Exponential Map of a Matrix Group is the Matrix Exponential, Thm - The Exponential Map is a Local Diffeomorphism at the Origin]; spec: for $G\subset GL(N;\mathbb K)$ a closed subgroup and $M$ compact, prove that $C^k(M;\operatorname{Ad}P)$ is a Banach Lie group with Lie algebra $C^k(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$; route: $C^k(M;\operatorname{End}E)$ (with $E=P\times_G\mathbb K^N$, norm via a fixed connection) is a Banach algebra; $C^k(M;\operatorname{Ad}P)$ is a closed subgroup of its unit group; charts near $1$ via the matrix exponential/logarithm power series, which are analytic maps of the Banach algebra and restrict to $C^k(M;\operatorname{ad}P)\to C^k(M;\operatorname{Ad}P)$ by the closed subgroup characterisation $\mathfrak h=\{X:\exp tX\in H\}$ (DG XI, restated); translate charts by left multiplication; multiplication and inversion are analytic (Banach-algebra product and Neumann series); no $\omega$-lemma is needed. State clearly that only the matrix-group case is treated.
- `Ex - Stabiliser of a Connection is the Centraliser of its Holonomy` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-T2.7.5 corollary drill; prereqs: [Thm - The Reduced Gauge Group Acts Freely on Connections, Def - Holonomy Group of a Connection]; spec: prove the corollary in full for connected $M$; deduce that a $U(1)$-connection always has stabiliser $U(1)$ (constant gauge transformations) and that an $SU(2)$-connection with holonomy $SU(2)$ has stabiliser $\{\pm1\}$.
- `Exercise Index - §5.3 Gauge Transformations` — lists: [Ex - Gauge Transformations of an Abelian Bundle are Maps to the Group, Ex - The Gauge Group Homomorphism from a Representation and its Infinitesimal Version, Ex - The Infinitesimal Gauge Action is the Covariant Derivative, Ex - The C^k Gauge Group of a Matrix Group Bundle is a Banach Lie Group, Ex - Stabiliser of a Connection is the Centraliser of its Holonomy]

### §5.4 Flat Connections and Representations of the Fundamental Group
- `Def - Flat Connection` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D3.2.3, A-D3.3.1, A-R3.3.1, B-T2.5.6 (flatness of the product connection, as a verified example via the Maurer–Cartan equation); prereqs: [Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Def - Curvature of a Vector-Bundle Connection, Def - Gauge Transformation, Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature, Thm - The Maurer-Cartan Equation]; spec: $\omega$ (or $\nabla$) is flat iff $\Omega=0$ (iff $F=0$); $\mathcal A^\flat(P)$; corollaries proved on the page: flatness is gauge invariant (curvature transforms by conjugation), so $\mathcal G(P)$ acts on $\mathcal A^\flat(P)$ and the moduli space $\mathcal M^\flat(P):=\mathcal A^\flat(P)/\mathcal G(P)$ is defined; flat iff $H$ involutive (from IV's exercise, restated and proved here) ; the product connection $\operatorname{pr}_2^*\theta$ is flat (Maurer–Cartan); A-R3.3.1 (why study $\mathcal M^\flat$) in Axiom Motivation. Verified examples: product connection; the constant connections $i(a\,dx+b\,dy)$ on $T^2\times U(1)$ (flat, generally not gauge-trivial — the holonomy exercise); RG I `Ex - The Tangent Bundle of a Lie Group has a Canonical Flat Connection` (link); non-example: the Hopf connection. True name: "a connection with integrable horizontal distribution" / "locally the product connection" (the next theorem). Calibration: flat implies the exterior covariant derivative sequence is a complex (II exercise); a flat $U(1)$-connection over a simply connected base is gauge-trivial (forward to the monodromy theorem).
- `Thm - Local Triviality of Flat Connections` — type: theorem; source items: A-D3.3.6 (the mechanism $\pi^*\nabla s=d\hat s$), B-T2.5.6 (product connection), the classical "flat = locally product" (needed for A-I3.3.3); prereqs: [Def - Flat Connection, Thm - The Frobenius Theorem, Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality, Thm - Transformation of Local Connection and Curvature Forms, Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition]; statement: For a connection $\omega$ on $P\to M$ the following are equivalent: (a) $\omega$ is flat; (b) the horizontal distribution is integrable; (c) every point of $M$ has a neighbourhood $U$ with a local section $s$ such that $s^*\omega=0$ (a flat local gauge), equivalently $\omega|_{\pi^{-1}(U)}$ is the product connection in the trivialisation defined by $s$; (d) there is a trivialising atlas whose transition functions $g_{\alpha\beta}$ are locally constant; and then parallel transport along any curve inside a flat chart depends only on its endpoints; proof: (a)⇔(b) via $\Omega(\tilde X,\tilde Y)=-\omega([\tilde X,\tilde Y])$ and Frobenius (DG X, restated); (b)⇒(c): the integral manifold of $H$ through $p$ is transverse to the fibre and $\pi$ restricts to a local diffeomorphism onto a neighbourhood $U$ (inverse function theorem via DG IV's rank theorem), whose inverse is a horizontal section; (c)⇒(d): two flat gauges differ by $g$ with $g^*\theta=0$, hence $dg=0$ (matrix case: $g^{-1}dg=0$; general: $\theta_g\circ dg=0$ and $\theta_g$ is an isomorphism), so $g$ is locally constant; (d)⇒(a): $F_\alpha=0$ in each chart; the parallel-transport clause from (c); reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. II §9 (Thm 9.1), Taubes *Differential Geometry* Ch. 13; spec: Mechanism: "zero curvature makes the horizontal planes tangent to a foliation whose leaves are local horizontal sections." Targets: monodromy; flat bundles as bundles with discrete structure group (bold).
- `Thm - Holonomy of a Flat Connection Depends Only on the Homotopy Class of the Loop` — type: theorem; source items: A-I3.3.3, A-D3.3.5 (the monodromy representation, defined as corollary), A-R3.3.5; prereqs: [Thm - Local Triviality of Flat Connections, Def - Holonomy Group of a Connection, Def - Homotopy of Paths, Def - Path-Product and the Fundamental Group, Thm - The Fundamental Group is a Group]; statement: Let $\omega$ be flat. If $c_0,c_1$ are piecewise smooth paths from $m$ to $m'$ that are homotopic relative to the endpoints (through a continuous homotopy), then $\Gamma(c_0)=\Gamma(c_1)$. Consequently, for $p\in P_m$ the map $[c]\mapsto\operatorname{hol}_p(c)$ is a well-defined anti-homomorphism $\pi_1(M,m)\to G$ (a homomorphism after composing with inversion or after reading the path product in the opposite order — the page fixes the convention so that $\rho_\omega\colon\pi_1(M,m)\to G$ is a homomorphism), the monodromy representation of $\omega$ at $p$; changing $p$ conjugates it; for a vector-bundle connection one obtains $\rho_\nabla\colon\pi_1(M,m)\to GL(E_m)$; every element of $\pi_1$ is represented by a piecewise smooth loop (Lemma, via a smoothing argument in flat charts — or restrict the definition of $\pi_1$ on this page to piecewise smooth loops and prove the natural map to the continuous $\pi_1$ is a bijection using the Lebesgue-number subdivision and straight-line homotopies inside charts; write this lemma in full); proof: Lebesgue number of the homotopy square with respect to the cover by preimages of flat charts; within a flat chart parallel transport depends only on endpoints (previous theorem); move the path across the grid one square at a time; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. II §9, Cor 9.2? / Taubes Ch. 13; spec: Targets: the monodromy correspondence below; $\pi_1$ acting on fibres of flat bundles; the Aharonov–Bohm effect (bold outlook to VII).
- `Thm - Existence of the Universal Cover and the Deck Action of the Fundamental Group` — type: theorem; source items: A §3.3 conventions ($\tilde M$, $\pi_1(M)$ acting by deck transformations, $\tilde M$ as a principal $\pi_1(M)$-bundle), A-D3.3.6 (prerequisite); prereqs: [Def - Covering Space, Def - Universal Cover, Def - Deck Transformation Group, Thm - Path Lifting and Homotopy Lifting, Thm - Lifting Criterion for Continuous Maps, Thm - Quotient by a Properly Discontinuous Action is a Manifold and a Covering Map, Def - Discrete Group and Properly Discontinuous Action, Def - Principal G-Bundle]; statement: Let $M$ be a connected manifold with base point $m$. (a) There is a simply connected covering $q\colon\tilde M\to M$ (the universal cover), unique up to isomorphism of coverings over $M$; $\tilde M$ carries a unique smooth structure making $q$ a smooth covering map (a local diffeomorphism), and $\tilde M$ is second countable. (b) The deck group $\operatorname{Deck}(\tilde M/M)$ acts smoothly, freely and properly discontinuously on $\tilde M$, transitively on each fibre, and $\tilde M/\operatorname{Deck}\cong M$. (c) There is an isomorphism $\pi_1(M,m)\cong\operatorname{Deck}(\tilde M/M)$ (after choosing $\tilde m\in q^{-1}(m)$), so $q\colon\tilde M\to M$ is a principal $\pi_1(M,m)$-bundle with the discrete group $\pi_1(M,m)$ acting on the right (by $\tilde x\cdot[\gamma]:=\varphi_{[\gamma]}^{-1}(\tilde x)$, or with the convention fixed on the page). (d) $\pi_1(M,m)$ is countable; proof: (a) Hatcher §1.3 construction (points are homotopy classes of paths from $m$; manifolds are locally path-connected and semi-locally simply connected since locally contractible), with the smooth structure lifted through the local homeomorphisms (Lee ISM 2e Prop 4.40 argument); second countability from countability of $\pi_1$ (d) and second countability of $M$; (b) AT II's Def page states these; prove them here: freeness and proper discontinuity via the unique-lifting property (linked AT II theorems), transitivity via the lifting criterion; (c) the standard bijection via endpoints of lifts, homomorphism property checked; (d) Lee, *Introduction to Topological Manifolds* 2e, Thm 7.21 (countable cover by coordinate balls, countably many components of intersections, loops generated by finitely many "junction" segments) — written in full; reference: Hatcher, *Algebraic Topology*, §1.3 (pp. 63–65, Prop 1.39, Prop 1.40); Lee ISM 2e Prop 4.40; Lee TM 2e Thm 7.21; spec: NOTE for the coordinator: chapter XII (group 3) owns `Def - Covering and the Universal Covering` and `Thm - Lifting Criterion for Coverings`; this page proves the smooth-manifold existence statement because chapter V cannot depend on XII; XII should link this page for the manifold case rather than re-prove it, or the coordinator merges. Targets: flat bundles from representations; $T^n=\mathbb R^n/\mathbb Z^n$; the orientation double cover (bold).
- `Thm - Flat Connections and Monodromy Representations of the Fundamental Group` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-T3.3.1, A-D3.3.5, A-D3.3.6, A-D3.3.7, A-X3.3.2 (as Lemma), A-R3.3.6, A §3.3 conventions ($\mathcal R(M;G)$); prereqs: [Thm - Holonomy of a Flat Connection Depends Only on the Homotopy Class of the Loop, Thm - Existence of the Universal Cover and the Deck Action of the Fundamental Group, Def - Associated Bundle, Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles, Thm - Local Triviality of Flat Connections, Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature, Def - Representation of a Lie Group]; statement: Let $M$ be a connected manifold with base point $m$, $G$ a Lie group, $\tilde M\to M$ the universal cover with the right action of $\Gamma:=\pi_1(M,m)$. (a) For every homomorphism $\rho\colon\Gamma\to G$ the associated bundle $P_\rho:=\tilde M\times_\rho G$ (extension of the structure group of the principal $\Gamma$-bundle $\tilde M$ along $\rho$) is a principal $G$-bundle over $M$ carrying a canonical flat connection $\omega_\rho$ (the one whose horizontal lifts are the images of paths in $\tilde M$; equivalently, in the induced vector bundle $\tilde M\times_\rho V$ for a representation $V$, $\pi^*\nabla s=d\hat s$ for $\Gamma$-equivariant $\hat s\colon\tilde M\to V$ — A's (47) with $a=0$), whose monodromy representation at the base point is $\rho$. (b) Conversely a flat connection $\omega$ on a principal $G$-bundle $P$ with monodromy $\rho_\omega$ at $p\in P_m$ is gauge equivalent to $(P_{\rho_\omega},\omega_{\rho_\omega})$ via an isomorphism of bundles with connection. (c) Gauge equivalent flat connections have conjugate monodromy representations (A-X3.3.2), and conjugate representations give isomorphic flat bundles. (d) Hence the assignment $\omega\mapsto[\rho_\omega]$ induces a bijection $\mathcal M^\flat_G(M):=\{(P,\omega):P$ a principal $G$-bundle over $M$, $\omega$ flat$\}/\text{isomorphism}\;\longrightarrow\;\mathcal R(M;G):=\operatorname{Hom}(\pi_1(M,m),G)/\text{conjugation}$; in particular for $G=GL_k(\mathbb R)$ this is A's correspondence between flat rank-$k$ vector bundles (all bundles at once) and $\mathcal R(M;GL_k(\mathbb R))$; and for a fixed bundle $P$, $\mathcal M^\flat(P)=\mathcal A^\flat(P)/\mathcal G(P)$ is the subset of $\mathcal R(M;G)$ of representations whose associated bundle is isomorphic to $P$ (with the gauge group $\mathcal G(P)$, versus $\operatorname{Aut}(P)$ over the identity: both give the same quotient — proved). The variants for $O(k)$, $U(k)$, $SO(k)$, $SU(k)$ (A-R3.3.6) are the cases of general $G$; proof: A gives the two constructions only; write: (a) $P_\rho$ is a principal $G$-bundle (III's extension theorem, with the discrete-group principal bundle $\tilde M$); the flat connection: on $\tilde M\times G$ take the product connection, which is invariant under the diagonal $\Gamma$-action (Lemma) and descends to $P_\rho$; flatness by local triviality; monodromy computed by lifting a loop to $\tilde M$; (b) the developing map: define $\Phi\colon\tilde M\times G\to P$, $\Phi(\tilde x,g):=\Gamma(\tilde c)(p)\cdot g$ where $\tilde c$ is any path from $\tilde m$ to $\tilde x$ projected to $M$ and $\Gamma$ its parallel transport — well defined by the homotopy-invariance theorem, $\Gamma$-invariant by definition of the monodromy, descends to an isomorphism $P_{\rho_\omega}\to P$ carrying $\omega_{\rho_\omega}$ to $\omega$ (horizontal lifts correspond); (c) both directions written; (d) assemble; reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu I Ch. II §9 (Prop 9.3), Taubes Ch. 13, Morita *Geometry of Characteristic Classes* §2.? ; spec: Mechanism summary: "a flat connection is the same as a way of transporting fibres along paths that only sees homotopy classes; the universal cover is the space of homotopy classes of paths, so a representation of $\pi_1$ is exactly the transport data." Targets: the representation variety as a "moduli space of solutions of $F=0$" (A-R3.3.1), Chern–Simons critical points (VI), flat $U(1)$-bundles and $H^1$ (bold outlook), Casson-type invariants (bold).
- `Thm - The Moduli Space of Flat Connections with Compact Structure Group is Compact` — type: theorem; source items: A-T3.3.2, A-R3.3.7, A-I3.3.4 (replaced by countability), A-E3.3.1 (example), B-? none; prereqs: [Thm - Flat Connections and Monodromy Representations of the Fundamental Group, Thm - Existence of the Universal Cover and the Deck Action of the Fundamental Group]; statement: Let $M$ be a connected manifold and $G$ a compact Lie group. Give $\operatorname{Hom}(\pi_1(M,m),G)$ the topology of pointwise convergence (as a subspace of $G^{\pi_1(M,m)}$ with the product topology) and $\mathcal R(M;G)$ the quotient topology. Then $\mathcal R(M;G)$ is compact; if moreover $\pi_1(M,m)$ is generated by $\gamma_1,\dots,\gamma_N$ then $\rho\mapsto(\rho(\gamma_i))_i$ embeds $\operatorname{Hom}(\pi_1,G)$ as a closed subset of $G^N$ and $\mathcal R(M;G)$ as a closed subset of $G^N/G$ (diagonal conjugation), a compact Hausdorff space (A-R3.3.7 as a conditional clause; finite generation of $\pi_1$ of a compact manifold is a scope remark, not claimed); proof: (i) $\pi_1(M,m)$ is countable (universal-cover theorem (d)); (ii) a countable product of compact metrisable spaces is sequentially compact, hence compact (diagonal argument, written; this avoids the flagged Tychonoff page); (iii) $\operatorname{Hom}$ is closed in the product (the conditions $\rho(ab)=\rho(a)\rho(b)$ are closed); (iv) the quotient by the continuous action of the compact group $G$ is compact and Hausdorff (compact group acting on a Hausdorff space: the orbit relation is closed — proved); (v) the finitely generated clause; A's Example 107 (torus) as worked corollary: $\mathcal R(T^n;U(1))=\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Z^n,U(1))=U(1)^n$, using $\pi_1(T^n)=\mathbb Z^n$ (AT II's torus exercise and $\pi_1(S^1)$, with the product formula $\pi_1(X\times Y)=\pi_1(X)\times\pi_1(Y)$ proved as a lemma on the page); reference: Goldman, *The symplectic nature of fundamental groups of surfaces* §1 (for the topology on representation varieties), Bourbaki GT for the quotient; spec: Convention callout: A topologises $\mathcal R$ via a finite presentation; the series uses pointwise convergence, which agrees with A's when $\pi_1$ is finitely generated (proved in clause (v)). Targets: compactness of moduli of flat connections as the first "compactness theorem" of the series (contrast with XI).
- `Ex - Flat U(1)-Connections on the Torus` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-E3.3.1 (with the typo $\operatorname{Hom}(T^n,U(1))$ corrected); prereqs: [Thm - The Moduli Space of Flat Connections with Compact Structure Group is Compact, Thm - Flat Connections and Monodromy Representations of the Fundamental Group]; spec: identify $\mathcal M^\flat_{U(1)}(T^n)\cong U(1)^n$ both via the monodromy correspondence and directly via the constant connections $i\sum a_jdx^j$ modulo gauge ($a_j$ modulo $\mathbb Z$), and show the two identifications agree.
- `Ex - Representation Varieties of Surface Groups` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-E3.3.2, A-I3.3.5 (recorded as a scope remark: the identification of $\pi_1(\Sigma_g)$ with the presented group is not proved in this series); prereqs: [Thm - The Moduli Space of Flat Connections with Compact Structure Group is Compact]; spec: for an abstract group $\Gamma_g=\langle a_i,b_i\mid\prod_i[a_i,b_i]=1\rangle$ prove $\operatorname{Hom}(\Gamma_g,G)/G\cong\{(A_i,B_i)\in G^{2g}:\prod[A_i,B_i]=1\}/G$; for $G=U(1)$ obtain $U(1)^{2g}$; for $G=SU(2)$, $g=1$, describe the set of commuting pairs up to conjugation (the pillowcase, $T^2/\pm$); the $SL(n;\mathbb C)$ remark of A is a bold outlook.
- `Ex - Gauge-Equivalent Flat Connections have Conjugate Monodromy` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-X3.3.2; prereqs: [Thm - Flat Connections and Monodromy Representations of the Fundamental Group]; spec: the Lemma (c) as a stand-alone drill, both for principal and for vector bundles.
- `Ex - A Flat Connection on the Möbius Line Bundle with Holonomy Minus One` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-D3.3.6 drill; prereqs: [Thm - Flat Connections and Monodromy Representations of the Fundamental Group, Thm - Existence of the Universal Cover and the Deck Action of the Fundamental Group]; spec: with $\tilde S^1=\mathbb R$ and $\rho\colon\mathbb Z\to O(1)$, $1\mapsto-1$, show $\mathbb R\times_\rho\mathbb R$ is the Möbius bundle, that its canonical flat connection has holonomy $-1$, and that the bundle admits no flat connection with trivial holonomy (nontriviality).
- `Ex - Parallel Sections of the Flat Bundle of a Representation` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-D3.3.6 drill; prereqs: [Thm - Flat Connections and Monodromy Representations of the Fundamental Group]; spec: prove that global parallel sections of $\tilde M\times_\rho V$ correspond to $\rho(\pi_1)$-invariant vectors of $V$, and that the bundle is trivial as a flat bundle iff $\rho$ is trivial.
- `Ex - The Universal Cover of the Circle and of the Torus` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: universal-cover theorem drill; prereqs: [Thm - Existence of the Universal Cover and the Deck Action of the Fundamental Group]; spec: verify $\mathbb R\to S^1$ and $\mathbb R^2\to T^2$ are universal covers with deck groups $\mathbb Z$, $\mathbb Z^2$ acting by translation, and that they are the principal bundles of chapter I's quotient theorem.
- `Exercise Index - §5.4 Flat Connections and Representations of the Fundamental Group` — lists: [Ex - Flat U(1)-Connections on the Torus, Ex - Representation Varieties of Surface Groups, Ex - Gauge-Equivalent Flat Connections have Conjugate Monodromy, Ex - A Flat Connection on the Möbius Line Bundle with Holonomy Minus One, Ex - Parallel Sections of the Flat Bundle of a Representation, Ex - The Universal Cover of the Circle and of the Torus]
