### §6.1 Invariant Polynomials and the Chern-Weil Homomorphism
- `Def - Ad-Invariant Polynomial` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D3.1.1, B-D2.5.1, A-E3.1.1 (Appendix B item 4: $p_d(\xi)=\operatorname{tr}\xi^d$, not $i\operatorname{tr}\xi^d$), A-T3.1.1 Step 1 (infinitesimal invariance (83), proved as corollary), A §3.1 conventions (polarisation); prereqs: [Def - Adjoint Representation, Def - Multilinear Form, Thm - Symmetrization and Alternation Projectors, Thm - Ad is a Smooth Representation and its Differential is ad]; spec: an $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariant homogeneous polynomial $p\colon\mathfrak g\to\mathbb K$ of degree $d$ (A's three conditions) and the equivalent symmetric $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariant $d$-linear form $\lambda$ (B's definition), with the polarisation identity proved on the page (both directions, the standard formula $\lambda(\xi_1,\dots,\xi_d)=\tfrac1{d!}\sum_{S}(-1)^{d-|S|}p(\sum_{i\in S}\xi_i)$); the ring $I(G)$ of invariant polynomials; corollary proved: infinitesimal invariance $\sum_jp(\xi_1,\dots,[\xi,\xi_j],\dots,\xi_d)=0$ for connected $G$ (differentiate along $\operatorname{Ad}_{\exp t\xi}$), and conversely for connected $G$ (via the exponential-image argument of chapter I). Verified examples: $\operatorname{tr}(\xi^d)$ on $\mathfrak{gl}_n$, $\mathfrak u(n)$ (real-valued after multiplying by $i^d$), the coefficients $c_j$ of $\det(\lambda1+\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\xi)$ on $\mathfrak u(r)$ (real-valued: A's conjugation argument written), $\det$; non-example: $\xi\mapsto\xi_{11}$ on $\mathfrak{gl}_2$ (not invariant, verified with a specific $g$); non-example: a polynomial invariant under $\operatorname{Ad}$ of $SO(2m)$ but not of $O(2m)$ — the Pfaffian (forward). Calibration: on an abelian $\mathfrak g$ every polynomial is invariant; $I(SU(2))$ is generated by $\det$ (stated as an exercise-level claim with proof deferred to the exercise on the characteristic polynomial).
- `Def - Chern-Weil Form of an Invariant Polynomial` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D3.1.2, A-R3.1.1, B-D2.5.2, B-T2.5.1, B-D2.5.3 (→ link `Def - de Rham Cohomology`, `Def - Betti Numbers`), B-D2.5.4 (the class $c_\lambda(P)$; its independence of $\omega$ is the theorem), B §2.5 conventions (the antisymmetrised formula and the basis formula); prereqs: [Def - Ad-Invariant Polynomial, Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Thm - Bundle-Valued Forms on the Base are Basic Equivariant Forms on the Total Space, Def - Lie-Algebra-Valued Differential Forms and Their Bracket, Def - Adjoint Bundles ad P and Ad P]; spec: for a principal $G$-bundle with connection $\omega$ and $p\in I(G)$ of degree $d$, the form $p(\Omega)\in\Omega^{2d}(P)$ (A: evaluate the polynomial on the matrix of 2-forms; B: $\lambda(\Omega,\dots,\Omega)$ with the antisymmetrisation normalisation $\tfrac1{d!}\sum_\sigma\operatorname{sign}\sigma\,\lambda(\Omega(X_{\sigma1},X_{\sigma2}),\dots)$ — the page proves the two agree and equal the basis formula $\sum\Omega^{j_1}\wedge\dots\wedge\Omega^{j_d}\lambda(Y_{j_1},\dots,Y_{j_d})$, using that even forms commute (Lemma)); $p(\Omega)$ is basic and invariant (entries of $\Omega$ basic; $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariance of $p$ and equivariance of $\Omega$), so it descends to $p(F_\omega)\in\Omega^{2d}(M)$ — B's $\lambda\circ\bar\Omega$, defined through the fibrewise descent of $\lambda$ to $\operatorname{ad}P$ (B-T2.5.1 proved); the vector-bundle version $p(F_\nabla)$ for a connection on $E$ via $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ (identical by IV's correspondence); the class $c_p(P):=[p(F_\omega)]\in H^{2d}_{dR}(M)$ (well defined by the Chern–Weil theorem; Convention callout on the coefficient field $\mathbb R$ vs $\mathbb C$ and A's "for definiteness $\mathbb C$"). Verified examples: $p=\operatorname{tr}$ on a $U(1)$-bundle gives $F$ itself; $p=\det$ on $\mathfrak{su}(2)$; non-example: $p(\xi)=\operatorname{tr}(\xi)^2$ vs $\operatorname{tr}(\xi^2)$ give different forms (computed on a rank-2 example). Calibration: $p(F)$ for a flat connection is $0$ in positive degree; $p(F)$ is gauge invariant (from V's curvature transformation).
- `Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-T3.1.1, A-I3.1.1 (replaced by DG X link), B-T2.5.2, B-T2.5.3, B-I2.5.1, B-R2.5.1, A-R3.1.1; prereqs: [Def - Chern-Weil Form of an Invariant Polynomial, Thm - Bianchi Identity for a Principal Connection, Thm - Graded Antisymmetry and Jacobi Identity for Lie-Algebra-Valued Forms, Thm - Existence of Connections on Principal Bundles, Thm - Pull-Back of Connections and Curvature, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of de Rham Cohomology, Thm - Structure Equation for the Curvature]; statement: For a principal $G$-bundle $P\to M$, a connection $\omega$ and $p\in I(G)$ of degree $d$: (i) $d\,p(F_\omega)=0$; (ii) for two connections $\omega_0,\omega_1$ the forms $p(F_{\omega_1})-p(F_{\omega_0})$ are exact; hence $c_p(P)=[p(F_\omega)]\in H^{2d}_{dR}(M)$ is independent of the connection, and $p\mapsto c_p(P)$ is a ring homomorphism $I(G)\to H^{even}_{dR}(M)$ (the Chern–Weil homomorphism); proof: A: (i) full modulo the passage from (83) to $\mathfrak g$-valued forms; (ii) full modulo the cited homotopy operator and the unproved claim that $A_t$ is a connection on $\varpi^*P$; B: (i) full modulo the horizontal-at-a-point section; (ii) omitted. Write: Lemma 1 (invariance identity for forms: for $\alpha\in\Omega^1(P;\mathfrak g)$ and even-degree $\beta_j$, $\sum_jp(\beta_1,\dots,[\alpha\wedge\beta_j],\dots,\beta_d)=0$, from (83) by multilinearity, with the sign bookkeeping for even forms — this is A's asserted step); Lemma 2 (closedness on $P$: $d\,p(\Omega)=\sum_jp(\Omega,\dots,d\Omega,\dots,\Omega)=\sum_jp(\Omega,\dots,[\Omega\wedge\omega],\dots)=0$ by Bianchi (b) and Lemma 1); Lemma 3 ($\pi^*$ is injective on forms and commutes with $d$, so $p(F_\omega)$ is closed on $M$); Lemma 4 (B's alternative proof of (i) via a local section horizontal at a point: existence of such a section proved — take any section and modify by $g$ with $g(b)=e$, $dg_b$ chosen to kill the vertical part); Lemma 5 (the connection $\tilde\omega:=(1-t)\varpi^*\omega_0+t\varpi^*\omega_1$ on $\varpi^*P\to M\times[0,1]$ is a connection — convexity of $\mathcal A$ — with $\iota_j^*\tilde\omega=\omega_j$); Lemma 6 ($p(F_{\tilde\omega})$ is closed on $M\times[0,1]$, so $\iota_1^*p(F_{\tilde\omega})-\iota_0^*p(F_{\tilde\omega})$ is exact by DG X's homotopy invariance (restated; the explicit transgression form is the next theorem)); ring homomorphism from $(pq)(F)=p(F)\wedge q(F)$ (even forms commute); reference: Kobayashi–Nomizu II Ch. XII Thm 1.1; Milnor–Stasheff Appendix C; Morita, *Geometry of Characteristic Classes* Thm 5.? ; spec: this page replaces the flagged AT III pages; Bridges to AT III `Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem (Statement)` are written as "the statement there is proved here" (no link to the flagged page; bold plain text). Mechanism summary: "the Bianchi identity says $d\Omega$ is a bracket, and invariance says brackets are invisible to $p$; the space of connections is convex, so any two curvatures are ends of one closed form on $M\times I$." Targets: Chern classes, Euler class, Pontryagin classes, Chern–Simons transgression, Yang–Mills topological bound (VII).
- `Thm - Transgression Formula and the Chern-Simons Form` — type: theorem; source items: A-X3.2.1(a) (the identity (97)), A-R3.2.3, A-D3.2.2 (the integrand), the explicit form of Chern–Weil (ii); prereqs: [Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem, Thm - Curvature of a Shifted Connection, Def - Lie-Algebra-Valued Differential Forms and Their Bracket, Thm - Graded Antisymmetry and Jacobi Identity for Lie-Algebra-Valued Forms]; statement: Let $\omega_0,\omega_1$ be connections on $P$, $b:=\omega_1-\omega_0$ (basic, $\operatorname{ad}$-valued), $\omega_t=\omega_0+tb$, $F_t$ its curvature, and $p\in I(G)$ of degree $d$. Then $p(F_1)-p(F_0)=d\,Tp(\omega_0,\omega_1)$ with the transgression form $Tp(\omega_0,\omega_1):=d\int_0^1p(b,F_t,\dots,F_t)\,dt\in\Omega^{2d-1}(M)$ (descended from $P$). In particular, on a trivial bundle with $\omega_0$ the product connection and $\omega_1$ given by $A\in\Omega^1(M;\mathfrak g)$: for $p(\xi)=\operatorname{tr}(\xi^2)$ on a matrix group, $\operatorname{tr}(F_A\wedge F_A)=d\operatorname{tr}\big(A\wedge dA+\tfrac23A\wedge A\wedge A\big)$ (A's (97)), and the 3-form $\operatorname{cs}(A):=\operatorname{tr}(A\wedge dA+\tfrac23A^{\wedge3})$ is the Chern–Simons form; proof: differentiate $p(F_t)$ in $t$ using $\tfrac{d}{dt}F_t=d^{\omega_t}b$ (II's shifted-curvature theorem in the principal setting), the invariance identity, and Bianchi, to get $\tfrac{d}{dt}p(F_t)=d\,\big(d\,p(b,F_t,\dots,F_t)\big)$; integrate in $t$; the special case by direct expansion with $F_A=dA+A\wedge A$; reference: Chern–Simons, *Characteristic forms and geometric invariants* (1974) Prop 3.2; Kobayashi–Nomizu II Ch. XII Thm 1.? ; Freed, *Classical Chern–Simons theory I* §1; spec: Targets: the Chern–Simons functional (§6.4); integrality of the second Chern number via clutching (§6.4); the Yang–Mills energy identity (VII).
- `Thm - Naturality and Isomorphism Invariance of Characteristic Classes` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.5.4, B-T2.5.5, B-R2.5.2, A-T3.1.2(ii), A-D2.4.2 ("depend only on the isomorphism class"); prereqs: [Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem, Thm - Pull-Back of Connections and Curvature, Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature]; statement: (a) For smooth $f\colon N\to M$ and $p\in I(G)$, $c_p(f^*P)=f^*c_p(P)$; (b) if $\phi\colon P\to P'$ is an isomorphism of principal $G$-bundles over $M$ then $c_p(P)=c_p(P')$; (c) $c_p(P)$ depends only on the isomorphism class of $P$ and is invariant under $\operatorname{Aut}(P)$; (d) for homotopic $f_0\simeq f_1$, $c_p(f_0^*P)=c_p(f_1^*P)$; proof: B full for (a),(b) (with the misprints $\phi^*$ vs $\phi_*$ and $\phi^{-1}\circ s_\alpha$ corrected); (d) from III's homotopy theorem or from DG X's homotopy invariance; spec: Targets: characteristic classes are invariants of bundles; $c_p$ of a trivial bundle vanishes (next theorem).
- `Thm - Trivial Bundles Have Vanishing Characteristic Classes` — type: theorem; source items: B-T2.5.6, B-T2.5.7, B-R2.5.3; prereqs: [Thm - Naturality and Isomorphism Invariance of Characteristic Classes, Thm - The Maurer-Cartan Equation, Def - Flat Connection]; statement: If $P$ is trivial and $\deg p=d\ge1$ then $c_p(P)=0$; equivalently, if $c_p(P)\ne0$ for some $p$ of positive degree then $P$ is not trivial; more generally $c_p(P)=0$ for every $P$ admitting a flat connection; proof: B full with the sign slip in $d\phi(X,Y)$ corrected via IV's Maurer–Cartan equation: the product connection is flat, so $p(F)=0$; the flat case identically; reference: B Rem 2.5.9; spec: Targets: Chern–Weil obstructions to triviality (e.g. $TS^2$, $\mathcal O(-1)$).
- `Ex - The Coefficients of the Characteristic Polynomial are Ad-Invariant` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-E3.1.1(b); prereqs: [Def - Ad-Invariant Polynomial]; spec: invariance of $\det(\lambda1+\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\xi)$ under conjugation, homogeneity of each $c_j$, reality on $\mathfrak u(r)$ via $\overline{\det(\bar\lambda 1+\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\xi)}$; compute $c_1,c_2$ for $r=2$ and show $c_2=\tfrac{1}{8\pi^2}\operatorname{tr}\xi^2$ on $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ (A-R3.1.6's identity $\operatorname{tr}\xi^2=-2\det\xi$).
- `Ex - Polarisation of an Invariant Polynomial` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A §3.1 conventions, B-D2.5.1; prereqs: [Def - Ad-Invariant Polynomial]; spec: prove the polarisation formula and that invariance of $p$ is equivalent to invariance of $\lambda$; compute $\lambda$ for $p=\det$ on $\mathfrak{gl}_2$.
- `Ex - Even-Degree Forms Commute and Determinants of Matrices of Even Forms are Multiplicative` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-R3.1.1, A-T3.1.2(iii) gap; prereqs: [Def - Chern-Weil Form of an Invariant Polynomial, Thm - Determinant is Multiplicative]; spec: the even subalgebra $\Omega^{even}(M)$ is commutative; for square matrices with entries in a commutative ring, $\det$ is multiplicative and $\det$ of a block-diagonal matrix is the product; deduce that $\det(1+\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F)$ is well defined and multiplicative on direct sums.
- `Ex - The Chern-Weil Form of the Trace on a U(1)-Bundle is the Curvature` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E2.5.1 drill; prereqs: [Def - Chern-Weil Form of an Invariant Polynomial]; spec: for $G=U(1)$ and $p=\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\operatorname{tr}$ (i.e. $\tfrac{1}{2\pi i}\operatorname{tr}$ in B's convention — record both), $p(F)=\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F$ is a real closed 2-form.
- `Ex - Chern-Weil Forms of the Flat Product Connection Vanish` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-T2.5.6 drill; prereqs: [Thm - Trivial Bundles Have Vanishing Characteristic Classes]; spec: compute $\Omega=\operatorname{pr}_2^*(d\theta+\tfrac12[\theta\wedge\theta])=0$ explicitly for $G=SU(2)$ in quaternion coordinates.
- `Exercise Index - §6.1 Invariant Polynomials and the Chern-Weil Homomorphism` — lists: [Ex - The Coefficients of the Characteristic Polynomial are Ad-Invariant, Ex - Polarisation of an Invariant Polynomial, Ex - Even-Degree Forms Commute and Determinants of Matrices of Even Forms are Multiplicative, Ex - The Chern-Weil Form of the Trace on a U(1)-Bundle is the Curvature, Ex - Chern-Weil Forms of the Flat Product Connection Vanish]

### §6.2 Chern Classes and the First Chern Class from Curvature
- `Def - Chern Classes` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D3.1.3, A-D3.1.4, A-R3.1.2 (scope remark), A-R2.4.3, B-D2.5.5, B-D2.5.6, B-E2.5.1, B-E2.5.3, A-E3.1.1(b), conventions.md ($c(E)=\det(1+\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F)$); prereqs: [Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem, Def - Complex Vector Bundle and Hermitian Structure, Thm - Euclidean and Hermitian Structures are Reductions of the Structure Group, Thm - Naturality and Isomorphism Invariance of Characteristic Classes, Thm - Homotopic Maps Pull Back Isomorphic Principal Bundles, Ex - Every Complex Vector Bundle Admits a Hermitian Structure]; spec: For a principal $U(r)$-bundle $P$ with connection $A$: $c_j(P):=[c_j(F_A)]\in H^{2j}_{dR}(M)$, $c(P)=1+c_1+\dots+c_r=[\det(1+\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F_A)]$; for a complex vector bundle $E$: $c_j(E):=c_j(\operatorname{Fr}_U(E))$ for any Hermitian structure — independence proved on the page (two Hermitian structures are joined by a convex path, the unitary frame bundles form a bundle over $M\times[0,1]$, III's homotopy theorem gives an isomorphism, then isomorphism invariance); the same class is obtained from any connection on $E$ compatible with some Hermitian structure, and in fact from any $GL_r(\mathbb C)$-connection (corollary: $c_j$ extends to $I(GL_r(\mathbb C))$ and the Chern–Weil class does not depend on the reduction — proved via the Chern–Weil theorem on $\operatorname{Fr}(E)$ with $p$ the coefficient of $\det(\lambda1+\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\xi)$, which is $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariant on $\mathfrak{gl}_r(\mathbb C)$); B's normalisations $c_1$ from $\tfrac{1}{2\pi i}\operatorname{tr}$ and $c_n$ from $\tfrac1{(2\pi i)^n}A_1\wedge\dots\wedge A_n$ (proved equal to the top coefficient, i.e. $c_r(F)=\det(\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F)$; Convention callout on $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}$ versus $\tfrac1{2\pi i}$ — they differ by $(-1)^j$ and B's stated formulas are consistent with the series' after this sign is tracked; the series fixes $c(E)=\det(1+\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F)$ so that $c_1(\mathcal O(-1))[\mathbb{CP}^1]=-1$); explicit $c_1=[\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\operatorname{tr}F]$, $c_2=[\tfrac{1}{8\pi^2}(\operatorname{tr}F\wedge\operatorname{tr}F-\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F))]$, for $SU(r)$: $c_1=0$, $c_2=[-\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)]$ (sign: the writer must reconcile with A-R3.1.6, which prints $c_2=\tfrac1{8\pi^2}[\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)]$ — the discrepancy is the sign of $\operatorname{tr}\xi^2$ for skew-Hermitian $\xi$: $\operatorname{tr}(\xi^2)\le0$; A's $c_2=\tfrac{i^2}{(2\pi)^2}\det\xi=-\tfrac1{4\pi^2}\det\xi=\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\operatorname{tr}\xi^2$ using $\operatorname{tr}\xi^2=-2\det\xi$ on $\mathfrak{su}(2)$; so with $F$ skew-Hermitian $c_2(F)=\tfrac{1}{8\pi^2}\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)$ on $\mathfrak{su}(2)$-valued forms, and AT III's "$-\tfrac1{8\pi^2}$" convention differs by treating $\theta$ differently — record the resolution in the Convention callout and state which formula the series uses: $c_2(P)=\tfrac1{8\pi^2}[\operatorname{tr}(F_A\wedge F_A)]$ for $SU(2)$-bundles, agreeing with A's (94)); scope remark (R3.1.2, A-I3.1.4): the integral lift $c_j\in H^{2j}(M;\mathbb Z)$ is not constructed; integrality of periods is proved for $c_1$ over surfaces and $c_2$ over 4-manifolds (this chapter). Verified examples: trivial bundle $c=1$; line bundles $c=1+c_1$; the Hopf/tautological bundle (next theorem); non-example: a real bundle has no Chern classes without a complex structure (but see Pontryagin). Calibration: $c_j(E)=0$ for $j>\operatorname{rk}E$; $c(E)$ is a unit in $H^{even}$.
- `Thm - Axioms and Properties of Chern Classes` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-T3.1.2, A-R3.1.3, A-X3.1.1 (parts (a)–(d) as corollaries; exercises), A-T2.4.2(ii) (general additivity), B-T2.5.8; prereqs: [Def - Chern Classes, Thm - Naturality and Isomorphism Invariance of Characteristic Classes, Thm - Trivial Bundles Have Vanishing Characteristic Classes, Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature, Def - Induced Connections on Dual, Hom, and Endomorphism Bundles, Ex - Even-Degree Forms Commute and Determinants of Matrices of Even Forms are Multiplicative]; statement: The Chern classes satisfy: (i) $c_0(E)=1$; (ii) naturality $c(f^*E)=f^*c(E)$; (iii) Whitney sum $c(E_1\oplus E_2)=c(E_1)\smile c(E_2)$ (wedge of representatives); (iv) normalisation $c(\mathcal O(-1))=1-[\omega_1]$ on $\mathbb{CP}^1$, i.e. $\int_{\mathbb{CP}^1}c_1(\mathcal O(-1))=-1$; and consequently (v) $c(E)$ depends only on the isomorphism class; (vi) $c_j(E^\vee)=(-1)^jc_j(E)$; (vii) $c(E)=1$ for trivial $E$ and $c_j(E)=0$ for $j>\operatorname{rk}E-k$ if $E\cong E_1\oplus\underline{\mathbb C}^k$; (viii) $c_1(L_1\otimes L_2)=c_1(L_1)+c_1(L_2)$ and $c_1(E)=c_1(\Lambda^rE)$; (ix) (B-T2.5.8) for a $U(1)$-bundle over a closed oriented surface, $\int_\Sigma F\ne0$ implies nontriviality; proof: A full for (i)–(iv) modulo the determinant identity (exercise) and the normalisation computation (next theorem); (vi) via the dual connection $-A^t$ and $\det(1+\tfrac{i}{2\pi}(-F^t))=\det(1-\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F)$; (viii) via the tensor-product connection $A_1\otimes1+1\otimes A_2$ with curvature $F_1+F_2$, and $\Lambda^r$ via the trace; (ix) Stokes; reference: Milnor–Stasheff §14 (axioms), Kobayashi–Nomizu II Ch. XII §3; spec: Convention callout: A's generator $a$ vs the series' $[\omega_1]$; the sign in (iv). Mechanism: "block-diagonal curvature has block-diagonal $\det$, and $\det$ is multiplicative on commuting entries." Targets: Chern classes of $T\mathbb{CP}^n$ (bold outlook), $c_2$ of $SU(2)$-bundles (§6.4), the Euler class comparison (§6.3).
- `Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature` **[anchor]** — type: theorem; source items: A-T3.1.3, A-R3.1.5 (integrality and degree), A-E2.2.4 ($\int_{S^2}F_a=2\pi i$, re-derived by Stokes here; the metric route is the exercise), B-T2.5.8, B-E2.5.2, A-T3.1.2(iv) (normalisation), A-I3.1.2 (link III), A-I3.1.3 (link III), the injectivity clause for simply connected bases (new; needed downstream); prereqs: [Def - Chern Classes, Def - First Chern Class via the Classifying Map, Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class, Thm - The Standard Connection on the Hopf Bundle, Ex - Curvature of the Standard Hopf Connection, Thm - The de Rham Cohomology of Complex Projective Space, Thm - Winding Number of a Map from the Circle to U(1), Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Thm - Flat Connections and Monodromy Representations of the Fundamental Group, Thm - The Top de Rham Cohomology of a Closed Connected Oriented Manifold is R]; statement: Let $L\to M$ be a complex line bundle over a compact manifold with Hermitian structure and unitary connection $A$ (curvature $F_A\in\Omega^2(M;i\mathbb R)$). (a) $c_1(L)=[\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F_A]$ equals the topological first Chern class $c_1^{top}(L)$ of chapter III; in particular for the tautological bundle $\mathcal O(-1)\to\mathbb{CP}^N$ with the connection induced by the Hopf connection, $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F=-\omega_N$ as forms, and $\int_{\mathbb{CP}^1}\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F=-1$. (b) For every smooth $h\colon\Sigma\to M$ from a closed oriented surface, $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\int_\Sigma h^*F_A\in\mathbb Z$, and equals $\deg(h^*L)$, the clutching degree of chapter III; in particular for $M=\Sigma$ itself $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\int_\Sigma F_A=\deg L$, so $\deg\colon\operatorname{Pic}(\Sigma)\to\mathbb Z$ is $L\mapsto\int_\Sigma c_1(L)$ and $c_1$ is a complete invariant of line bundles over closed oriented surfaces. (c) $c_1(L)\ne0$ implies $L$ nontrivial; if $\int_\Sigma F_A\ne0$ then $L|_\Sigma$ is nontrivial. (d) If $M$ is connected and simply connected, then $c_1(L)=0$ implies $L$ trivial, so $c_1\colon\operatorname{Pic}(M)\to H^2_{dR}(M)$ is injective; proof: (a) naturality of both classes (VI theorem and III's page) reduces to $\mathcal O(-1)$; there IV's Hopf curvature exercise gives $\pi^*F=2i\sum dx_j\wedge dy_j$ and III's $\omega_N$ has $\pi^*\omega_N=\tfrac1\pi\sum dx_j\wedge dy_j$, so $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\pi^*F=-\pi^*\omega_N$ and injectivity of $\pi^*$ gives the form identity; the integral over $\mathbb{CP}^1$ is III's normalisation; (b) trivialise $L$ over $\Sigma\setminus D^\circ$ and $D$ as in III's classification proof, write $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F=\tfrac{i}{2\pi}dA_j$ on each piece, apply Stokes to both pieces, and identify $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\oint(A_2-A_1)=\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\oint g^{-1}dg=-w(g)$ or $+w(g)$ according to orientation — the writer fixes the sign so that (a) holds for $\mathcal O(-1)$ ($\deg=-1$); (c) from the trivial-bundle theorem and Stokes; (d) $c_1(L)=0$ means $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F_A=d\eta$ for a real 1-form (by definition of exactness in $H^2_{dR}$), so $A':=A-2\pi i\eta$ (check: $F_{A'}=F_A-2\pi i\,d\eta=0$) is a flat unitary connection; its monodromy $\pi_1(M)\to U(1)$ is trivial since $\pi_1=1$, so by V's monodromy theorem $L$ with $A'$ is isomorphic to the trivial flat bundle, hence trivial; reference: Bott–Tu §6 (Ex.) for (b), Griffiths–Harris Ch. 1 §1 for (a), Donaldson–Kronheimer §2.1.4; spec: Convention callout: A's Remark 92 integrates over $\Sigma$, not $M$ (Appendix B item 5). Mechanism summary: "the curvature of a line bundle is $d$ of the potential on each trivialising piece, and Stokes turns the failure of the potentials to match into the winding number of the transition function." Targets: Dirac quantisation (VII), $\operatorname{Pic}$ of simply connected 4-manifolds detected by $c_1$ (XI, XIII), the degree–Chern number dictionary.
- `Ex - Chern Classes Depend only on the Isomorphism Class and Vanish for Trivial Bundles` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-X3.1.1(a),(c); prereqs: [Thm - Axioms and Properties of Chern Classes]; spec: drill of (v),(vii) with full proofs from naturality and the trivial-bundle theorem.
- `Ex - Chern Classes of the Dual Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X3.1.1(b); prereqs: [Thm - Axioms and Properties of Chern Classes, Def - Induced Connections on Dual, Hom, and Endomorphism Bundles]; spec: prove $c_j(E^\vee)=(-1)^jc_j(E)$ via the dual connection; also $c_j(\bar E)=(-1)^jc_j(E)$ and $E^\vee\cong\bar E$ for Hermitian $E$.
- `Ex - Chern Classes Vanish above the Rank of the Non-Trivial Summand` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X3.1.1(d); prereqs: [Thm - Axioms and Properties of Chern Classes]; spec: Whitney sum with a trivial summand.
- `Ex - The Tangent Bundle of S^2 is Nontrivial via Chern Classes` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X3.1.2; prereqs: [Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature, Def - The Levi-Civita Connection Viewed as a Connection on TM, Ex - The Levi-Civita Connection of the Round Sphere as a Connection Matrix]; spec: $TS^2$ with $I=$ rotation by $90^\circ$ is a Hermitian line bundle whose Levi-Civita connection is unitary with curvature $F=iK\,dA$; $\int_{S^2}\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F=-\tfrac1{2\pi}\int K\,dA=\mp2$ (sign per orientation convention: the writer fixes it so that $c_1(TS^2)[S^2]=2$, which is $e(TS^2)[S^2]$ per conventions.md), nonzero, so $TS^2$ is nontrivial; compare with RG IV Gauss–Bonnet (bridge only).
- `Ex - Curvature of the Hopf Connection and the Round Metric of Radius One Half` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: A-E2.2.4 (second half), A-I2.2.4; prereqs: [Ex - Curvature of the Standard Hopf Connection, Def - Isometry of Riemannian Manifolds, Thm - CP^1 is Diffeomorphic to S^2 via the Hopf Map]; spec: prove that $\Phi\colon S^3\to S^2_{1/2}\subset\mathbb C\times\mathbb R$, $(z_0,z_1)\mapsto(z_0\bar z_1,\tfrac12(|z_0|^2-|z_1|^2))$, is constant on Hopf fibres, induces a diffeomorphism $\mathbb{CP}^1\to S^2_{1/2}$, and is a Riemannian submersion for the round metrics (compute $d\Phi$ on the horizontal frame $v_2,v_3$ and show it is orthonormal in $S^2_{1/2}$), so the quotient metric is the round metric of radius $\tfrac12$; conclude $F_a=2\,\mathrm{vol}_{S^2_{1/2}}i$ and $\int F_a=2\pi i$, recovering (a) of the theorem by a second route.
- `Ex - The Chern Number of the Hopf Line Bundle over CP^1 is Minus One` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T3.1.2(iv), B-T2.5.8; prereqs: [Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature, Def - The Hopf Bundle]; spec: compute $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{CP}^1}F$ via the two local potentials $A_1,A_2$ of IV's exercise and Stokes, obtaining $-w(z/|z|)=-1$; link AT III `Ex - The Chern Number of the Hopf Line Bundle over CP^1` as a companion (with its normalisation compared).
- `Exercise Index - §6.2 Chern Classes and the First Chern Class from Curvature` — lists: [Ex - Chern Classes Depend only on the Isomorphism Class and Vanish for Trivial Bundles, Ex - Chern Classes of the Dual Bundle, Ex - Chern Classes Vanish above the Rank of the Non-Trivial Summand, Ex - The Tangent Bundle of S^2 is Nontrivial via Chern Classes, Ex - Curvature of the Hopf Connection and the Round Metric of Radius One Half, Ex - The Chern Number of the Hopf Line Bundle over CP^1 is Minus One]

### §6.3 Pontryagin Classes, the Pfaffian, and the Euler Class
- `Def - Pfaffian` **[backlink, anchor]** — type: definition; source items: B-D2.5.7 (Pfaffian half), B-I2.5.2 (proved as corollary: $\mathfrak{so}(2m)\cong\Lambda^2\mathbb R^{2m}$ via $A\mapsto\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}e_i\wedge e_j$, and $\lambda(\sigma_1,\dots,\sigma_m)=\sigma_1\wedge\dots\wedge\sigma_m\in\Lambda^{2m}\mathbb R^{2m}\cong\mathbb R$ is symmetric multilinear); prereqs: [Def - Alternating Multilinear Form, Def - Determinant, Thm - Lie Algebras and Dimensions of the Classical Matrix Groups, Def - Ad-Invariant Polynomial]; spec: for $A\in\mathfrak{so}(2m)$, $\operatorname{Pf}(A):=\tfrac{1}{2^mm!}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2m}}\operatorname{sign}(\sigma)\prod_{i=1}^mA_{\sigma(2i-1)\sigma(2i)}$; equivalently $\tfrac1{m!}\sigma_A^{\wedge m}=\operatorname{Pf}(A)e_1\wedge\dots\wedge e_{2m}$ for $\sigma_A=\sum_{i<j}A_{ij}e_i\wedge e_j$ (equivalence proved); B's $\lambda(\sigma,\dots,\sigma)$ equals $m!\operatorname{Pf}$ (Convention callout explaining B's $(2\pi)^mm!$ normalisation); $\operatorname{Pf}$ for $m=1$: $\operatorname{Pf}\begin{pmatrix}0&a\\-a&0\end{pmatrix}=a$; verified examples: block-diagonal $A$ with $2\times2$ blocks $a_i$: $\operatorname{Pf}=\prod a_i$; non-example: $\operatorname{Pf}$ is not defined on symmetric or odd-size matrices. Calibration: $\operatorname{Pf}(-A)=(-1)^m\operatorname{Pf}(A)$; $\operatorname{Pf}(A)^2=\det A$ for $m=1,2$ by hand.
- `Thm - Properties of the Pfaffian` — type: theorem; source items: B-I2.5.2 (the Pfaffian is an invariant polynomial on $\mathfrak{so}(2m)$), B-E2.5.4; prereqs: [Def - Pfaffian, Thm - Determinant is Multiplicative, Def - Ad-Invariant Polynomial]; statement: For $A\in\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ and $B\in\operatorname{Mat}(2m\times2m;\mathbb R)$: (a) $\operatorname{Pf}(B^tAB)=\det(B)\operatorname{Pf}(A)$; (b) $\operatorname{Pf}(A)^2=\det A$; (c) $\operatorname{Pf}$ is $\operatorname{Ad}(SO(2m))$-invariant and homogeneous of degree $m$, hence in $I(SO(2m))$, but changes sign under $\operatorname{Ad}$ of $O(2m)\setminus SO(2m)$; (d) $\operatorname{Pf}$ of a block-diagonal matrix is the product of the Pfaffians of the blocks; (e) the polynomial $\det$ on $\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ equals $\operatorname{Pf}^2$, so $\operatorname{Pf}$ is not in the subring generated by the coefficients of the characteristic polynomial; proof: (a) via the $\Lambda^{2m}$ description: $B^t$ acts on $\sigma_A$ by $\Lambda^2(B^t)$ and on $\Lambda^{2m}$ by $\det B$; (b) every skew matrix is $B^tJB$ with $J$ block-diagonal standard (spectral theorem for skew-symmetric matrices — write the elementary reduction), then (a) and (d); (c) from (a) with $B=g^{-1}=g^t$; (d) direct; reference: Milnor–Stasheff Appendix C (Lemma C.? and the discussion of the Pfaffian), Kobayashi–Nomizu II Ch. XII §5 ; spec: Targets: the Euler class; $e^2=p_m$ (corollary of (b), proved in the Euler-class page).
- `Def - Euler Class of an Oriented Vector Bundle` **[anchor]** — type: definition; source items: B-D2.5.7 (Euler class half), B-E2.5.4, B-E2.5.5, B-T2.5.9, conventions.md ($e(E)=[\operatorname{Pf}(F/2\pi)]$, $e(TS^2)[S^2]=2$); prereqs: [Def - Pfaffian, Thm - Properties of the Pfaffian, Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem, Thm - Euclidean and Hermitian Structures are Reductions of the Structure Group, Def - Chern Classes, Ex - The Levi-Civita Connection of the Round Sphere as a Connection Matrix]; spec: for an oriented real vector bundle $E$ of rank $2m$, choose a Euclidean structure, so $\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}(E)$ is an $SO(2m)$-bundle, and a metric connection with curvature $F$ (skew-symmetric in oriented orthonormal frames): $e(E):=[\operatorname{Pf}(\tfrac{F}{2\pi})]\in H^{2m}_{dR}(M)$; well defined by Chern–Weil and independent of the Euclidean structure (convexity + III's homotopy theorem, as for Chern classes; proved); B's normalisation $\operatorname{Pf}(\bar\Omega)/((2\pi)^mm!)$ with B's $\operatorname{Pf}=\lambda(\sigma,\dots,\sigma)=m!\operatorname{Pf}_{series}$ agrees (Convention callout, computed); corollaries proved on the page: $e(E)=c_1(E)$ for an oriented rank-2 bundle viewed as a Hermitian line bundle (B-T2.5.9: $\mathfrak{so}(2)\cong\mathfrak u(1)$, $\operatorname{Pf}(\sigma)=\sigma$, and the identification of $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}\operatorname{tr}$ with $\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\operatorname{Pf}$ — the sign is where the orientation convention enters; the writer fixes orientations so that $e=c_1$ with the series' $c_1$); $e(TS^2)[S^2]=2$ verified from the round-sphere curvature ($\operatorname{Pf}(F/2\pi)=K\,dA/2\pi$); $e(E)=0$ if $E$ has a nowhere-vanishing section (proved: reduce the structure group to $SO(2m-1)$ and the Pfaffian vanishes on $\mathfrak{so}(2m-1)\subset\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ — written); $e(E)^2=p_m(E)$ (from the Pfaffian theorem (b), after the Pontryagin page); reversing the orientation negates $e$. Verified examples: $TS^2$; trivial bundles ($e=0$); non-example: a non-orientable bundle has no Euler class in this sense. Bridges: RG IV `Thm - Gauss-Bonnet Theorem for Surfaces` (the identity $\int_\Sigma e(T\Sigma)=\chi(\Sigma)$ is not proved here; the series proves only $\int_{S^2}e(TS^2)=2$; a bold scope remark). Calibration: $e$ of a rank-$2m$ bundle vanishes if $2m>\dim M$; $e(E_1\oplus E_2)=e(E_1)\smile e(E_2)$ (proved via block-diagonal Pfaffians).
- `Def - Pontryagin Classes` **[anchor]** — type: definition; source items: none in A/B §1–3 (conventions.md: $p_1(E)=-c_2(E\otimes\mathbb C)$; required by the anchors for VII/XIII); prereqs: [Def - Chern Classes, Def - Constructions on Representations, Def - Euclidean Vector Bundle and Metric Connection, Def - Euler Class of an Oriented Vector Bundle]; spec: for a real vector bundle $E$ of rank $n$, $p_k(E):=(-1)^kc_{2k}(E\otimes\mathbb C)\in H^{4k}_{dR}(M)$; corollaries proved on the page: $c_j(E\otimes\mathbb C)=0$ for odd $j$ (the complexification is isomorphic to its conjugate, and $c_j(\bar E)=(-1)^jc_j(E)$ from the dual-bundle exercise, so $2c_j=0$ in real cohomology); the Chern–Weil formula: for a metric connection with skew curvature $F$, $\det(1-\tfrac{F}{2\pi})=1+p_1(F)+p_2(F)+\dots$ (the odd terms vanish pointwise as forms because $\operatorname{tr}$ of odd powers of skew matrices vanish — proved), so $p_1(E)=[-\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)]$; naturality and Whitney formula modulo 2-torsion (the de Rham version is exact: $p(E_1\oplus E_2)=p(E_1)\smile p(E_2)$, proved by block-diagonal determinants); $e(E)^2=p_m(E)$ for oriented rank $2m$ (via $\operatorname{Pf}^2=\det$); for an $SU(2)$-bundle $P$ and the underlying real rank-4 bundle $E$ of $P\times_{SU(2)}\mathbb C^2$: $E\otimes\mathbb C\cong\mathbb C^2\oplus\overline{\mathbb C^2}\cong2\cdot\mathbb C^2$ as $SU(2)$-representations (the quaternionic structure gives $\overline{\mathbb C^2}\cong\mathbb C^2$), so $c(E\otimes\mathbb C)=c(P)^2=(1+c_2(P))^2$ and $p_1(E)=-c_2(E\otimes\mathbb C)=-2c_2(P)$ — recorded as a verified example with every step proved. Convention callout: the sign $(-1)^k$ makes $p_1$ of the tangent bundle of $\mathbb{CP}^2$ positive (stated as a scope remark; not computed here). Non-example: $p_k$ of a bundle with a flat connection vanishes. Calibration: $p_k(E)=0$ for $4k>\dim M$; $p_1$ of a trivial bundle is $0$. Reference: Milnor–Stasheff §15, Kobayashi–Nomizu II Ch. XII §4.
- `Ex - Pfaffian of a 4 by 4 Skew Matrix and the Identity Pf Squared Equals det` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-D2.5.7 drill; prereqs: [Def - Pfaffian]; spec: $\operatorname{Pf}(A)=a_{12}a_{34}-a_{13}a_{24}+a_{14}a_{23}$; verify $\operatorname{Pf}^2=\det$ by expansion.
- `Ex - The Euler Class of the Tangent Bundle of the Round Sphere Integrates to Two` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: conventions.md sign check, B-E2.5.4; prereqs: [Def - Euler Class of an Oriented Vector Bundle, Ex - The Levi-Civita Connection of the Round Sphere as a Connection Matrix]; spec: in an oriented orthonormal frame compute the $\mathfrak{so}(2)$-valued curvature matrix $F=K\,dA\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\1&0\end{pmatrix}$ (up to the orientation sign) with RG I's Cartan structure equations (link `Ex - Cartan Structural Equations on S^2`), obtains $\operatorname{Pf}(F/2\pi)=dA/2\pi$ and $\int=2$; discuss the sign convention.
- `Ex - The Euler Class of an Oriented Rank-2 Bundle is its First Chern Class` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-T2.5.9, B-E2.5.5; prereqs: [Def - Euler Class of an Oriented Vector Bundle, Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature]; spec: the identification $SO(2)=U(1)$ of an oriented Euclidean plane bundle with a Hermitian line bundle ($I$ = rotation by $+90^\circ$), and $e=c_1$ including the sign; deduce $e(TS^2)[S^2]=c_1(TS^2)[S^2]=2$ consistently with the §6.2 exercise.
- `Ex - Odd Chern Classes of a Complexified Real Bundle Vanish` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: Pontryagin page corollary drill; prereqs: [Def - Pontryagin Classes, Ex - Chern Classes of the Dual Bundle]; spec: two proofs: via $E\otimes\mathbb C\cong\overline{E\otimes\mathbb C}$ and via the vanishing of $\operatorname{tr}$ of odd powers of skew-symmetric matrices of 2-forms.
- `Exercise Index - §6.3 Pontryagin Classes, the Pfaffian, and the Euler Class` — lists: [Ex - Pfaffian of a 4 by 4 Skew Matrix and the Identity Pf Squared Equals det, Ex - The Euler Class of the Tangent Bundle of the Round Sphere Integrates to Two, Ex - The Euler Class of an Oriented Rank-2 Bundle is its First Chern Class, Ex - Odd Chern Classes of a Complexified Real Bundle Vanish]

### §6.4 The Chern-Simons Functional and Flat Connections
- `Thm - Second Chern Number of an SU(2)-Bundle over a Closed Four-Manifold is the Clutching Degree` — type: theorem; source items: A-R3.1.6 (with (94)), A-I3.1.4 (for $c_2$), A-I3.2.3, A §3.1 conventions; prereqs: [Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds, Thm - Transgression Formula and the Chern-Simons Form, Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Def - Chern Classes, Ex - The Coefficients of the Characteristic Polynomial are Ad-Invariant]; statement: Let $X$ be a closed connected oriented 4-manifold, $P\to X$ a principal $SU(2)$-bundle with connection $A$. Then $c_2(P)=\tfrac1{8\pi^2}[\operatorname{tr}(F_A\wedge F_A)]$ (using $\operatorname{tr}\xi^2=-2\det\xi$ on $\mathfrak{su}(2)$) and $\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\int_X\operatorname{tr}(F_A\wedge F_A)=k(P)\in\mathbb Z$, the clutching degree of chapter III; in particular the integral is an integer independent of $A$, and every integer occurs; proof: choose $D$ and trivialisations of $P$ over $X\setminus D^\circ$ and over $D$ with clutching map $g$ (III); in each trivialisation $\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)=d\operatorname{cs}(A_i)$ (transgression theorem); Stokes on the two pieces (with the induced orientations of $S^3=\partial D$) gives $\int_X\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)=\int_{S^3}(\operatorname{cs}(A_2)-\operatorname{cs}(A_1))$; on $S^3$, $A_2=g^{-1}A_1g+g^{-1}dg$ (V's gauge action, local form), and Lemma (gauge variation of the Chern–Simons form): $\operatorname{cs}(g^{-1}Ag+g^{-1}dg)-\operatorname{cs}(A)=-\tfrac13\operatorname{tr}((g^{-1}dg)^3)-d\operatorname{tr}(dg\,g^{-1}\wedge A)$ (every term displayed; sign conventions tracked); so $\int_X\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)=-\tfrac13\int_{S^3}\operatorname{tr}((g^{-1}dg)^3)=\mp8\pi^2\deg g$ by III's $W(g)=\deg g$ with $\int_{SU(2)}\operatorname{tr}(\theta^3)=\pm24\pi^2$; the writer fixes all signs so that the final statement reads $\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\int_X\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)=k(P)$ with $k$ as defined in III (if the natural orientation conventions give $-k$, the definition of $k$ in III is adjusted by the coordinator to match — flag this in the return value); reference: Naber, *Topology, Geometry and Gauge Fields: Foundations* Ch. 6 / Freed–Uhlenbeck App. ; Donaldson–Kronheimer §2.1.4 (the local formula); spec: Mechanism summary: "$\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)$ is locally exact, and gluing two local primitives across a 3-sphere produces the winding number of the clutching map." Targets: the Chern–Simons functional is well defined modulo $\mathbb Z$; Yang–Mills topological bound (VII); Donaldson's moduli spaces on the bundle with $k=1$ (XIII).
- `Def - Chern-Simons Functional` **[anchor]** — type: definition (compound); source items: A-D3.2.2 (primary definition), A-D3.2.1 (4-dimensional formula; stated as the theorem below), A-R3.2.1, A-R3.2.3, A-I3.2.1 (scope remark), A-I3.2.2 (not needed), A-I3.2.3 (→ the previous theorem), A §3.2 conventions; prereqs: [Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds, Thm - Transgression Formula and the Chern-Simons Form, Thm - Gauge Variation of the Chern-Simons Functional]; spec: Let $M$ be a closed oriented 3-manifold and $P\to M$ a principal $SU(2)$-bundle — trivial by III(A). Choose a trivialisation, so connections are $A\in\Omega^1(M;\mathfrak{su}(2))$, and set $\vartheta(A):=\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\int_M\operatorname{tr}\big(A\wedge dA+\tfrac23A\wedge A\wedge A\big)\in\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$; well-definedness modulo $\mathbb Z$ under change of trivialisation is the corollary of the gauge-variation theorem (two trivialisations differ by a gauge transformation $g\colon M\to SU(2)$, and $\vartheta$ changes by $\deg g\in\mathbb Z$); $\vartheta$ is gauge invariant modulo $\mathbb Z$ and descends to $\mathcal A(P)/\mathcal G(P)\to\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$; A's 4-dimensional formula (95) is recorded as the characterisation proved on `Thm - The Four-Dimensional Formula for the Chern-Simons Functional`. Convention callout: A defines $\vartheta$ four-dimensionally and needs (I3.2.1) that every closed oriented 3-manifold bounds a compact oriented 4-manifold (Thom, Rokhlin; Lickorish–Wallace) — the series does not prove or use this fact; it is recorded as a scope remark in bold, and the 3-dimensional definition is primary; A's Appendix B item 6 (missing $\tfrac1{8\pi^2}$) noted. Verified examples: $\vartheta(0)=0$; $\vartheta$ of a pure-gauge connection $g^{-1}dg$ is $\deg g\equiv0$; for a connection with $A\wedge A\wedge A=0$ (e.g. abelian, $A$ valued in a line) $\vartheta=\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\int\operatorname{tr}(A\wedge dA)$; non-example: for $U(1)$-bundles over a 3-manifold the same formula is not well defined modulo $\mathbb Z$ without a further normalisation (record as a remark, not a claim). Calibration: $\vartheta$ is a cubic polynomial on the affine space of connections; its differential is computed in the critical-point theorem.
- `Thm - Gauge Variation of the Chern-Simons Functional` — type: theorem; source items: A-X3.2.1(c), A-X3.2.1(d), A §3.2 conventions ($\deg g$ for $g\colon M\to SU(2)\cong S^3$); prereqs: [Thm - Transgression Formula and the Chern-Simons Form, Thm - Gauge Action on Connection Matrices, Thm - The Brouwer Degree is an Integer and a Homotopy Invariant, Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; statement: For $A\in\Omega^1(M;\mathfrak{su}(2))$ on a closed oriented 3-manifold and $g\colon M\to SU(2)$ smooth, with $A^g=g^{-1}Ag+g^{-1}dg$: $\operatorname{cs}(A^g)=\operatorname{cs}(A)-\tfrac13\operatorname{tr}((g^{-1}dg)^3)-d\operatorname{tr}(dg\,g^{-1}\wedge A)$ (signs tracked), hence $\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\int_M\operatorname{cs}(A^g)=\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\int_M\operatorname{cs}(A)-\tfrac{1}{24\pi^2}\int_M\operatorname{tr}((g^{-1}dg)^3)=\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\int_M\operatorname{cs}(A)\mp\deg g$; consequently $\vartheta(A\cdot g)=\vartheta(A)+\deg g$ (mod the sign fixed by orientation conventions; the writer states the sign) in $\mathbb R$, and $\vartheta$ is well defined in $\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$ independently of the trivialisation; proof: the algebraic identity by direct expansion (every term displayed); Stokes kills the exact term; $W(g)=\deg g$ from III (restated with the value $24\pi^2$); reference: Freed, *Classical Chern–Simons theory I* Prop 2.? ; Donaldson–Kronheimer §2.1.4 ; spec: Mechanism: "the Chern–Simons form is not gauge invariant; its variation is an exact term plus the pull-back of the bi-invariant 3-form of $SU(2)$, whose integral counts the degree."
- `Thm - The Four-Dimensional Formula for the Chern-Simons Functional` — type: theorem; source items: A-D3.2.1, A-X3.2.1(b), A-X3.2.1(a) (the $M\times\mathbb R$ special case with the corrected factor), A-R3.2.1, A-I3.2.2; prereqs: [Def - Chern-Simons Functional, Thm - Transgression Formula and the Chern-Simons Form, Thm - Second Chern Number of an SU(2)-Bundle over a Closed Four-Manifold is the Clutching Degree, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Def - Manifold with Boundary and Induced Orientation]; statement: (a) Let $X$ be a compact oriented 4-manifold with $\partial X=M$, $P_X\to X$ a principal $SU(2)$-bundle with $P_X|_M\cong P$ and $A_X$ a connection restricting to $A$. Then $\vartheta(A)\equiv\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\int_X\operatorname{tr}(F_{A_X}\wedge F_{A_X})\pmod{\mathbb Z}$ — A's (95). (b) For two such extensions $(X,A_X)$, $(X',A'_X)$, the two integrals differ by an integer (A's well-definedness argument via the closed manifold $X\cup_M\overline{X'}$ and the integrality theorem, written with the gluing of the bundles and connections made explicit — smooth gluing along a collar after making the connections agree on the collar by a homotopy). (c) For $X=M\times[t_0,t_1]$ and a connection $A$ on $X$ with slices $A_t$: $\vartheta(A_{t_1})-\vartheta(A_{t_0})=\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\int_{M\times[t_0,t_1]}\operatorname{tr}(F_A\wedge F_A)$ in $\mathbb R$ (A's Exercise 96(a) second display, with the missing $\tfrac1{8\pi^2}$ restored); proof: (a) $P_X$ over a compact 4-manifold with boundary need not be trivial; but $\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)$ is locally $d\operatorname{cs}$; the writer proves (a) by first treating the case where $P_X$ is trivial (Stokes directly) and then the general case via the clutching description of $P_X$ relative to the boundary (a generic section of the associated $\mathbb C^2$-bundle vanishing nowhere near $M$ — relative version of III's generic-sections theorem — trivialises $P_X$ off a disc in the interior, and the disc contributes the integer $k$ by the integrality theorem); (b) from (a) and the closed-manifold theorem; (c) Stokes with the two boundary components; reference: Freed, *Classical Chern–Simons theory I* §2; Donaldson–Kronheimer §2.1.4 and §5.? ; spec: Convention callout: the orientation of $\overline{X'}$ reversed so that $\partial X'=-M$; A-I3.2.2 (existence of an extension of the bundle and the connection over $X$) is never assumed: the theorem is conditional on an extension being given. Targets: Chern–Simons as the boundary term of the Yang–Mills action (VII), instanton Floer theory (bold outlook).
- `Thm - Critical Points of the Chern-Simons Functional are the Flat Connections` — type: theorem; source items: A-T3.2.1, A-R3.2.2, A-D3.2.3 (link `Def - Flat Connection`); prereqs: [Def - Chern-Simons Functional, Def - Flat Connection, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Thm - Curvature of a Shifted Connection]; statement: For $A\in\Omega^1(M;\mathfrak{su}(2))$ on a closed oriented 3-manifold and $a\in\Omega^1(M;\mathfrak{su}(2))$, $\tfrac{d}{ds}\big|_0\vartheta(A+sa)=\tfrac1{4\pi^2}\int_M\operatorname{tr}(F_A\wedge a)$; hence $d\vartheta_A=0$ iff $F_A=0$: the critical points of $\vartheta$ (well defined on $\mathcal A/\mathcal G$ since $d\vartheta$ is gauge invariant) are exactly the flat connections; proof: A full modulo integration by parts and non-degeneracy — write: expand $\operatorname{cs}(A+sa)$ to first order, integrate by parts $\int\operatorname{tr}(A\wedge da)=\int\operatorname{tr}(dA\wedge a)$ (Stokes, closed $M$, signs displayed), collect $\operatorname{tr}((dA+A\wedge A)\wedge a)$ using cyclicity, and use that $(\xi,\eta)\mapsto-\operatorname{tr}(\xi\eta)$ is positive definite on $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ so the pairing $(F,a)\mapsto\int\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge a)$ is non-degenerate (choose $a=\star F$ with respect to any metric — or, to avoid the Hodge star before VII, choose $a$ supported in a chart with $\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge a)=|F|^2dx^1dx^2dx^3$ locally, written explicitly); reference: Freed, *Classical Chern–Simons theory I* Prop 2.? ; Donaldson–Kronheimer §5.? (Chern–Simons and flat connections); spec: Mechanism: "the Chern–Simons functional is the antiderivative of the curvature." Targets: flat connections as the "vacua" of 3-dimensional gauge theory; the representation variety of V as the critical set; instanton Floer homology (bold).
- `Ex - The Chern-Simons Form Transgresses the Second Chern Form` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X3.2.1(a); prereqs: [Thm - Transgression Formula and the Chern-Simons Form]; spec: verify $d\operatorname{tr}(A\wedge dA+\tfrac23A^{\wedge3})=\operatorname{tr}(F_A\wedge F_A)$ by direct expansion, including the vanishing of $\operatorname{tr}(A^{\wedge4})$ (cyclicity with the sign of moving a 1-form past three 1-forms).
- `Ex - Chern-Simons Invariants of Flat Connections on the 3-Torus` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T3.2.1 drill, A-E3.3.1; prereqs: [Def - Chern-Simons Functional, Thm - Critical Points of the Chern-Simons Functional are the Flat Connections]; spec: for the constant connections $A=\sum_ja_j\,dx^j$ with commuting $a_j\in\mathfrak{su}(2)$ (a maximal torus), show $F=0$ and $\vartheta(A)=0$; then show that for non-commuting constant $a_j$ the connection is not flat and compute $\vartheta$ explicitly: with $dA=0$ only the cubic term survives, $\operatorname{tr}(A\wedge A\wedge A)$ is a fixed multiple (computed by the writer) of $\operatorname{tr}(a_1[a_2,a_3])\,dx^1\wedge dx^2\wedge dx^3$, so $\vartheta(A)$ is that multiple of $\operatorname{tr}(a_1[a_2,a_3])\operatorname{vol}(T^3)/(8\pi^2)$.
- `Ex - The Chern-Simons Functional along a Path of Connections` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X3.2.1(a) (second display); prereqs: [Thm - The Four-Dimensional Formula for the Chern-Simons Functional]; spec: prove (c) of the theorem as a stand-alone drill via Stokes on $M\times[t_0,t_1]$ and identify $\tfrac{d}{dt}\vartheta(A_t)$ with the critical-point formula.
- `Ex - Well-Definedness of the Chern-Simons Functional under Change of Trivialisation` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X3.2.1(c); prereqs: [Thm - Gauge Variation of the Chern-Simons Functional]; spec: two trivialisations differ by $g\colon M\to SU(2)$; the values differ by $\deg g$.
- `Ex - The Differential of the Chern-Simons Functional` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-R3.2.2; prereqs: [Thm - Critical Points of the Chern-Simons Functional are the Flat Connections]; spec: the first-variation computation as a drill, with all integrations by parts displayed.
- `Ex - The Integral of tr(theta cubed) over SU(2)` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X3.2.1(d) (the constant), III Lemma B; prereqs: [Def - The Maurer-Cartan Form, Ex - Volume of the n-Sphere via the Volume Form]; spec: compute $\operatorname{tr}(\theta^3)$ at the identity on the basis $-i\sigma_a$, identify it with $\pm12\,\mathrm{vol}_{S^3}$ for the round metric of radius $1$ (using $\operatorname{Vol}(S^3)=2\pi^2$), and conclude $\int_{SU(2)}\operatorname{tr}(\theta^3)=\pm24\pi^2$; the sign is fixed by the orientation of $SU(2)\cong S^3$ chosen in III.
- `Exercise Index - §6.4 The Chern-Simons Functional and Flat Connections` — lists: [Ex - The Chern-Simons Form Transgresses the Second Chern Form, Ex - Chern-Simons Invariants of Flat Connections on the 3-Torus, Ex - The Chern-Simons Functional along a Path of Connections, Ex - Well-Definedness of the Chern-Simons Functional under Change of Trivialisation, Ex - The Differential of the Chern-Simons Functional, Ex - The Integral of tr(theta cubed) over SU(2)]

Dependency order for chapter VI: Def - Ad-Invariant Polynomial → Def - Chern-Weil Form of an Invariant Polynomial → Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem → Thm - Transgression Formula and the Chern-Simons Form → Thm - Naturality and Isomorphism Invariance of Characteristic Classes → Thm - Trivial Bundles Have Vanishing Characteristic Classes → Def - Chern Classes → Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature → Thm - Axioms and Properties of Chern Classes → Def - Pfaffian → Thm - Properties of the Pfaffian → Def - Euler Class of an Oriented Vector Bundle → Def - Pontryagin Classes → Thm - Second Chern Number of an SU(2)-Bundle over a Closed Four-Manifold is the Clutching Degree → Thm - Gauge Variation of the Chern-Simons Functional → Def - Chern-Simons Functional → Thm - The Four-Dimensional Formula for the Chern-Simons Functional → Thm - Critical Points of the Chern-Simons Functional are the Flat Connections → exercises. (Note: `Thm - Gauge Variation...` precedes `Def - Chern-Simons Functional` because the Def's well-definedness clause cites it; the topic page lists the Def first with a forward link, which is acceptable because both are in the same chapter and the Def's Examples section proves nothing that needs the Thm except the well-definedness corollary, which is stated with the link.)

---

## `Imported without proof` callouts in chapters I–VI

**None.** No page in chapters I–VI carries an `Imported without proof` callout; none of the eight pre-approved imports is used in these chapters. (The chapters' topic pages therefore have no `# Imported Results` section.)

## Coordinator flags (items handled by scope remarks, not by proofs) — decisions requested

The following source items are statements that chapters I–VI neither prove nor use; each is recorded on the named page in bold as "not proved and not used in this series", with the replacing argument named. They are not imports (nothing is derived from them). If the coordinator prefers, any of them can be promoted to a proved page in a later chapter or to a new pre-approved import.

1. A-I2.4.1, A-I2.4.2 (existence of classifying bundles and the classification theorem for arbitrary compact $G$) — proved only for $U(1)$ and $Sp(1)$ on `Thm - Line Bundles over Compact Manifolds are Pulled Back from Projective Space`; general case: scope remark on `Def - Classifying Bundle and Classifying Map`.
2. A-I2.4.3 (CW structure of $\mathbb{CP}^\infty$, $\mathbb{HP}^\infty$), A-I2.4.4 (cellular approximation), A-I2.4.5 (Hopf degree theorem $[M;S^2]\cong\mathbb Z$), A-I3.1.5 (Grassmannians classify $U(k)$) — replaced by clutching/winding-number arguments; scope remarks on the two classification pages and on `Def - Classifying Bundle and Classifying Map`.
3. A-I2.4.6 (integral cohomology of $\mathbb{CP}^\infty$, $\mathbb{HP}^\infty$), A-R3.1.2/A-I3.1.4 (Chern classes lie in the image of $H^\bullet(M;\mathbb Z)$), A-D2.4.5/A-D2.4.7 in their $H^\bullet(M;\mathbb Z)$ form — replaced by de Rham definitions plus the concrete integrality theorems (`Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature` (b), `Thm - Second Chern Number of an SU(2)-Bundle over a Closed Four-Manifold is the Clutching Degree`); scope remarks on `Def - Chern Classes`, `Def - First Chern Class via the Classifying Map`, `Thm - The de Rham Cohomology of Complex Projective Space`. **Downstream consequence (VIII, XI, XIII): use $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ where the sources say $H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$; the completeness of the $SU(2)$ Chern number ($\pi_3(S^3)\cong\mathbb Z$) is not available before XII.**
4. A-I3.2.1 (every closed oriented 3-manifold bounds a compact oriented 4-manifold; Thom/Rokhlin, Lickorish–Wallace) — not used: the Chern–Simons functional is defined three-dimensionally on `Def - Chern-Simons Functional`; the 4-dimensional formula is proved conditionally on a given bounding manifold. A-I3.2.2 (extension of $P$, $A$ over $X$) is likewise never assumed.
5. A-I3.3.4 (fundamental groups of manifolds are finitely presented) — replaced by countability of $\pi_1$ (proved) on `Thm - The Moduli Space of Flat Connections with Compact Structure Group is Compact`; finite generation/presentation stated only as a conditional clause. A-I3.3.5 (presentation of surface groups) — `Ex - Representation Varieties of Surface Groups` works with the abstract presented group; the identification with $\pi_1(\Sigma_g)$ is a scope remark (Seifert–van Kampen is statement-only in the vault; group 3 may prove it in XII).
6. B-I1.1.2 (Gleason–Montgomery–Zippin) — historical remark only, on `Def - Classical Matrix Groups`.
7. A-T2.4.3 for non-compact manifolds of dimension $\le3$ — proved for compact manifolds only (`Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds` (A)); the non-compact case is a scope remark.
8. RG IV Poincaré–Hopf in general (B Rem 1.5.19's "$\chi(M)=0$") — the series proves only the $S^{2n}$ case it uses (`Thm - Hairy Ball Theorem`); the general Poincaré–Hopf theorem is not claimed.
9. Sign conventions to be pinned by the writers and reported back: the orientation of $\mathbb{CP}^1$ and of $SU(2)\cong S^3$ that make $\deg\mathcal O(-1)=-1$, $\int_{SU(2)}\operatorname{tr}(\theta^3)=+24\pi^2$ or $-24\pi^2$, $k(P)=+\tfrac1{8\pi^2}\int\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)$, and $\vartheta(A\cdot g)=\vartheta(A)+\deg g$ simultaneously; the manifest fixes the *statements* and leaves the consistent choice of orientations to the writer of `Def - The Hopf Bundle`, `Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds`, `Thm - Second Chern Number ...` and `Thm - Gauge Variation of the Chern-Simons Functional`, who must agree (the coordinator should assign these four pages to one writer).
10. Duplication with chapter XII: `Thm - Existence of the Universal Cover and the Deck Action of the Fundamental Group` (V) proves the manifold case because V cannot depend on XII; group 3's `Def - Covering and the Universal Covering` should link it for manifolds rather than re-prove, or the coordinator merges.
11. Duplication with chapter X: `Thm - Sard's Theorem when the Source Dimension does not Exceed the Target Dimension` (III) is the restricted case; chapter X's full `Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem` (with finite-dimensional Sard as a lemma) supersedes it and may link it for the easy cases.

## Cross-chapter dependency summary

- **I** depends only on existing vault pages (DG I–V, DG XI, DG XII, RG I–II, Topology IV, Linear Algebra, AT II path lifting, AT III Def - The Hopf Map).
- **II** depends on I (nothing essential: only `Def - Classical Matrix Groups` for $\mathfrak{gl}_k$ notation) and on existing vault pages (DG II, DG VI–VIII, DG XII, RG I, RG III).
- **III** depends on I (`Def - Free, Transitive, Effective, and Proper Group Actions`, `Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action`, `Thm - Quotient Manifold Theorem for Free Proper Actions`, `Thm - Quotient by a Properly Discontinuous Action ...`, `Def - Complex Projective Space as a Quotient`, `Thm - CP^1 is Diffeomorphic to S^2 ...`, `Def - Representation of a Lie Group`, `Thm - Complex Representations of U(1) and SU(2)`, `Ex - SU(2) is the Group of Unit Quaternions`, `Ex - Computing exp on su(2) ...`) and on II (`Def - Operations on Vector Bundles and Pull-Back Bundles`, `Def - Bundle-Valued Differential Forms`, `Thm - Pull-Back Commutes with the Exterior Derivative`, `Def - Euclidean Vector Bundle and Metric Connection`); plus DG IV, DG IX, DG X, DG V, Topology IV, LA, AT II ($\pi_1(S^1)$ as bridge), RG IV (`Def - Brouwer Degree of a Map`).
- **IV** depends on I (fundamental vector fields and their bracket homomorphism, `Thm - Ad is a Smooth Representation ...`, one-parameter subgroups, translations, Lie algebra representations), II (connections, connection matrices, curvature, local curvature formula, exterior covariant derivative, induced connections, affine space, pull-back commutes with $d$), III (§3.2–3.4 pages: principal bundles, frame bundle, associated bundles, basic/equivariant forms, adjoint bundles, sections theorem, cocycles, pull-backs, Hopf bundle); plus DG VIII, DG V, DG X (Frobenius), RG I (bridges).
- **V** depends on IV (connections, horizontal subspaces, local forms, curvature, structure equation, transformation law, induced covariant derivatives, pull-back of connections, Maurer–Cartan form), III (`Thm - Homotopic Maps Pull Back Isomorphic Principal Bundles` (c), `Thm - Winding Number ...`, `Def - Adjoint Bundles ad P and Ad P`, `Thm - Sections of an Associated Bundle are Equivariant Functions`, `Def - Reduction and Extension of the Structure Group`), II (`Def - Gauge Group of a Vector Bundle`, `Thm - Gauge Action on Connection Matrices`, `Def - Euclidean Vector Bundle and Metric Connection`), I (quotient by properly discontinuous actions, discrete groups, exponential map pages); plus DG V, DG X (Frobenius), DG IX (Stokes), RG I (parallel transport), AT II (covering-space theorems, $\pi_1$).
- **VI** depends on IV (curvature, structure equation, Bianchi, Maurer–Cartan equation, pull-back of connections, Hopf connection, induced covariant derivatives), V (gauge action on connections and curvature, flat connections, monodromy theorem (for the simply-connected injectivity clause)), III (§3.5–3.6: degree, winding number, clutching, homotopy invariance, classification pages, $\omega_N$, de Rham cohomology of $\mathbb{CP}^N$, `Def - Complex Vector Bundle and Hermitian Structure`, reductions theorem), II (curvature of shifted connection, induced connections, Levi-Civita page for the $S^2$ examples), I (`Def - Adjoint Representation` link, `Thm - Ad is a Smooth Representation ...`, `Thm - Lie Algebras and Dimensions ...`); plus DG X (homotopy invariance of de Rham cohomology), DG IX, LA (determinants), DG VII/VIII (multilinear algebra), AT I (`Def - Betti Numbers`), AT III definitions as bridges.
- **Provided to later chapters (anchors and their proved dependencies):** VII uses IV (`Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection`, `Thm - Bianchi Identity ...`, `Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Principal Bundle`, `Thm - Exterior Covariant Derivatives on P and on Associated Bundles Agree`), V (`Def - Gauge Transformation`, `Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature`, `Thm - The Reduced Gauge Group Acts Freely on Connections`), VI (`Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem`, `Thm - Transgression Formula and the Chern-Simons Form`, `Def - Chern Classes`, `Def - Pontryagin Classes`, `Thm - Second Chern Number ...`), III (`Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds` for existence of the $k=1$ bundle; `Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class` in its $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ form). VIII uses I (`Def - Representation of a Lie Group`, `Thm - Complex Representations of U(1) and SU(2)`, `Thm - The Exponential Map ...` pages), III (`Def - Reduction and Extension of the Structure Group`, `Def - Associated Bundle`, `Thm - Principal Bundles are Classified by Cocycles`, `Def - The Hopf Bundle`, `Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles ...` in the $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ form), IV (`Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles`), II (`Def - The Levi-Civita Connection Viewed as a Connection on TM`). IX–XI use II (`Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle`, `Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space`, `Def - Gauge Group of a Vector Bundle`, `Thm - Curvature of a Shifted Connection`), V (`Def - Gauge Transformation`, infinitesimal gauge action), VI (`Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature` incl. clause (d)). XII may link V's `Thm - Existence of the Universal Cover ...` and III's `Thm - The Brouwer Degree is an Integer and a Homotopy Invariant`, `Thm - Homotopic Maps Pull Back Isomorphic Principal Bundles`, `Thm - The de Rham Cohomology of Complex Projective Space`. XIII uses VI (`Def - Chern Classes`, `Def - Pontryagin Classes`, `Def - Euler Class ...`, `Thm - Second Chern Number ...`), III (`Thm - Classification of Principal SU(2)-Bundles over Four-Manifolds`, existence and invariance only), and the $\operatorname{Pic}$ convention of flag 3.
